    See *Sifra, Beḥuqqotai* 3:3, 111b; *Aggadat Bereshit* 23:5; BT [[Sanhedrin 102#102a|*Sanhedrin* 102a]]; 2 Enoch 8:3; *Seder Gan Eden* (*Beit ha-Midrash*, 3:138); *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-10b|1:10b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-72a|72a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82b|82b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-92a|92a–b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-136b|136b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-178b|178b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-231b|231b]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-46a|2:46a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-130a|130a–b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-136a|136a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-173b|173b]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-195b|195b–196a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-21b|3:21b–22b]], [[Zohar 7.07 - Parashat Metsora#^z3-52b|52b]], [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-193a|193a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-13c|13c]] (*MhN*). Cf. [[Matthew 25#^25-6|Matthew 25:6]].

[^14]: **all those trees in the garden sing praises…** See *Pereq Shirah*, 2:57, 80; JT *Ḥagigah* 2:1, 77a; BT [[Chagigah 14#14b|*Ḥagigah* 14b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-7a|1:7a]]; *Seder Gan Eden* (*Beit ha-Midrash*, 3:138).

[^15]: **herald proclaims potently…** See [[Daniel 03#^3-4|Daniel 3:4]]; [[Daniel 07#^7-18|7:18]]. In Daniel the phrase *holy ones of the Most High* refers to Israel; here, to the kabbalists who perceive the secrets. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-67b|1:67b]].

[^16]: **When spirit of all spirits wafts the fragrance of the soul…** The following cryptic riddle is nearly impenetrable; I offer here one conceivable interpretation. For other interpretations, see *OY*, Vital, *KP, MM*. Similar numerical riddles appear in *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-32b|1:32b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-72b|72b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-151b|151b]] (*Tos*); [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-12b|2:12b]], [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-95a|95a]]; [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-162a|3:162a]].

    *Ḥokhmah*, known as “spirit of spirits” (*Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-174a|2:174a]]) emanates *Binah*, source of the soul. From Her issues *Tif’eret*, the divine voice, as the flow of energy branches in four directions.

[^17]: **One ascends to one side…** The *sefirah* of *Ḥesed* emanates to the right, and *Gevurah* to the left. *Tif’eret* mediates between them.

[^18]: **Two crown themselves in three…** The pair of *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*, harmonized by *Tif’eret*, adorn themselves in the next sefirotic triad: *Netsaḥ, Hod*, and *Yesod*. These unite with *Shekhinah*.

[^19]: **One radiates colors…** *Shekhinah*, the divine rainbow, radiates colors in two directions.

[^20]: **Six enter twelve… twenty-two** The imagery of colored light now switches to that of divine speech: the Hebrew alphabet, composed of twenty-two letters. See *Sefer Yetsirah* 2:1: “Twenty-two elemental letters: three mother letters, seven double letters, twelve simple letters.”

[^21]: **Six encompassed by ten…** The six aspects are encompassed by the decade of *sefirot*, which themselves are united and animated by *Ein Sof*.

[^22]: **when the body is defiled** By sin or at death.

[^23]: **soul drifts through the atmosphere of the noontime sun…** When a sinner dies, his soul is hurled through the atmosphere of the scorching sun on its way to Hell.

    The image of “the hollow of a sling” derives from [[I Samuel 25#^25-29|1 Samuel 25:29]]: *The soul of your enemies He will sling out as from the hollow of a sling*. See BT [[Shabbat 152#152a|*Shabbat* 152a]]: “Rabbi Eli’ezer said, ‘… The souls of the wicked are continually muzzled, while one angel stands at one end of the world and another stands at the other end, and they sling their souls \[the souls of the wicked\] to each other, as is said: *The soul of your enemies He will sling out as from the hollow of a sling*.’”

    See Radak on the verse in Samuel; Moses de León, *Shushan Edut*, 351–53 (and n. 171, where Scholem cites De León’s likely source in Jacob ha-Kohen’s *Sefer ha-Orah*); idem, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 373 (and n. 6), 399; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-128a|1:128a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-217b|217b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-142b|2:142b]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-25a|3:25a]], [[Zohar 9.08 - Parashat Balaq#^z3-185b|185b–186a]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-213b|213b]]; Liebes, *Peraqim*, 345–47 (who discusses the Zoharic neologism קוספיתא \[*quspita*\], “hollow of a sling”). In a later book (*Sefer ha-Mishqal*, 67–68), De León rejects this particular stage of the afterlife.

[^24]: **Dumah** Literally, “silence,” a name for the netherworld in the Bible, e.g., [[Psalms 094#^94-17|Psalms 94:17]]: *Unless YHVH had been my help, my soul would have nearly dwelt in dumah*. Cf. [[Psalms 115#^115-17|Psalms 115:17]].

    In rabbinic literature Dumah is the angel in charge of souls of the dead (BT [[Berakhot 18#18b|*Berakhot* 18b]], [[Shabbat 152#152b|*Shabbat* 152b]], [[Sanhedrin 94#94a|*Sanhedrin* 94a]]). In the *Zohar* he retains this role but also oversees Hell. See 1:8a–b, 94a, 102a, 124a (*MhN*), 237b.

[^25]: **falling, not rising…** According to M *Eduyyot* 2:10, the wicked are punished in Hell for twelve months. See BT [[Rosh Hashanah 17#17a|*Rosh ha-Shanah* 17a]]: “Transgressors of Israel who sin with their body and transgressors of the Gentiles who sin with their body descend to Hell and are punished there for twelve months. After twelve months, their body is consumed and their soul burned, and the wind scatters them under the soles of the feet of the righteous… But as for the heretics, informers, apostates, skeptics, those who rejected Torah and denied the resurrection of the dead, those who abandoned the ways of the community, those who *spread* their *terror in the land of the living* ([[Ezekiel 32#^32-23|Ezekiel 32:23]]), and those who sinned and made the masses sin…: these descend to Hell and are punished there for generation after generation….Hell will be consumed, but they will not be consumed.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62b|1:62b]].

[^26]: **At that moment** The moment of midnight.

[^27]: **North** Symbolizing *Gevurah*, the divine attribute of Power.

[^28]: **rooster…** The rooster’s crow marks the moment of midnight, when spiritual seekers are expected to rise and study Torah. See above, note 13.

    Cf. Rashi on BT [[Eruvin 53#53b|*Eruvin* 53b]], s.v. *maggidei ba-alatah*, according to whom the rooster’s crows mark the various divisions of the night. See *Pereq Shirah*, 2:57 (s.v. *tarnegol*): “When the blessed Holy One comes to the righteous in the Garden of Eden, all the trees of the Garden sprinkle spices before Him. Then he \[the rooster\] praises.”

    See Naḥmanides on [[Job 38#^38-36|Job 38:36]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-10b|1:10b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-92b|92b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-218b|218b]]; [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-196a|2:196a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-22b|3:22b–23b]], [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-171b|171b]]; *[[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-88a|ZḤ* 88a]] (*MhN, Rut*).

[^29]: **the blessed Holy One and all the righteous…** See BT [[Shabbat 63#63a|*Shabbat* 63a]]: “Rabbi Abba said in the name of Rabbi Shim’on son of Lakish, ‘When two disciples of the wise listen to one another in *halakhah*, the blessed Holy One listens to their voice, as is said: *You who dwell in the gardens, companions listen for your voice; let me hear!*’” See *Zohar* [[#^z1-92a|1:92a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-178b|178b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-231b|231b]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-46a|2:46a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-13a|3:13a]], [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-22a|22a]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-213a|213a]].

[^30]: ***YHVH said to Abram*, \[*“Go you forth*…\]** The verse continues: *from your land, from your birthplace* \[or: *kinfolk*\], *from your father’s house, to the land that I will show you!”*

[^31]: ***Haran*…** Abram’s brother.

[^32]: **What is the point here?** What is the connection between the death of Haran and God’s command to Abram?

[^33]: **no one had ever died in the lifetime of his father In** fact, according to the genealogies of [[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^5-1|Genesis 5]] and 11, a number of earlier biblical figures died while their fathers were still alive, e.g., Abel in the lifetime of his father Adam, Enoch in the lifetime of Jared, and Lamech in the lifetime of Methusaleh. The *Zohar*’s claim here is apparently derived from *Leqaḥ Tov*, [[Torah 02 - Noach#^11-28|Genesis 11:28]]; cf. *Midrash ha-Gadol*, ad loc.

[^34]: **When Abram was cast into the fire, Haran was killed…** According to rabbinic tradition, Abram was cast into a fiery furnace by King Nimrod. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 38:13: “*Haran died in the presence of Terah his father*. Rabbi Ḥiyya said, ‘Terah was an idol-worshiper \[and manufacturer\]. Once he traveled somewhere and left Abraham to sell in his place. A man approached, wanting to buy. He \[Abraham\] asked him, “How old are you?” He replied, “Fifty years old.” He said, “Woe to such a man! You are fifty years old and would bow down to an object one day old!” Ashamed, he walked away. Later a woman came carrying a plate of fine flour. She said, “Here, offer this to them!” He rose, took a stick, and broke them \[the idols\]. Then he put the stick in the hands of the largest. When his father returned, he demanded, “Who did this to them?” He \[Abraham\] replied, “How can I conceal it? A woman came carrying a plate of fine flour and said to me, ‘Offer this to them!’ This one said, ‘I will eat first!’ Another said, ‘I will eat first!’ This largest one rose, took the stick, and broke them.” He \[Terah\] said, “Why are you mocking me? Do they know anything?” He replied, “Don’t your ears hear what your mouth is saying?” He \[Terah\] seized him and delivered him to Nimrod. He \[Nimrod\] said, “Let us worship fire!” He \[Abraham\] replied, “Let us worship water, which extinguishes fire.” He said, “Let us worship water!” He replied, “Let us worship the clouds, which carry water.” He said, “Let us worship clouds!” He replied, “Let us worship the wind, which carries the clouds.” He said, “Let us worship the wind!” He replied, “Let us worship human beings, who withstand the wind.” He said, “You are playing with words. We will worship only fire. I will cast you into it, and let your God whom you worship come and save you!” Haran was standing there, undecided. He said \[to himself\], “If Abram is victorious, I will say that I side with him. If Nimrod is victorious, I will say that I side with him.” When Abram descended into the fiery furnace and was saved, he \[Nimrod\] asked him, “With whom do you side?” He replied, “With Abram.” They seized him and cast him into the fire. His innards were scorched, and he emerged and died in the presence of his father, as is written: *Haran died in the presence of Terah his father.’”*

    See Theodor’s notes on the passage, and Ginzberg, *Legends*, 1:195–203, 213–17, and corresponding notes.

[^35]: **The verse should read: *they set out with him*…** With Terah.

[^36]: **Because Terah and Lot set out with Abram and Sarai…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-31b|2:31b]]. Cf. Rashi on [[Torah 02 - Noach#^11-31|Genesis 11:31]]; Naḥmanides on [[Torah 02 - Noach#^11-28|Genesis 11:28]], 31.

[^37]: **he came around to fulfilling Abram’s desire** Terah acknowledged God. On the theme of Terah’s repentance, see *Bereshit Rabbah* 30:4; *Tanḥuma, Shemot* 18; Rashi on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^15-15|Genesis 15:15]]; Ibn Ezra on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-1|Genesis 12:1]] (*diqduq*); Naḥmanides on [[Torah 02 - Noach#^11-32|Genesis 11:32]]; *Zohar* [[#^z1-78b|1:78b]]; [[Zohar 12.06 - Piqqudin#^z3-111b|3:111b]] (*RM*).

[^38]: **Their desire was to go there** Even though they stayed for some time in Haran.

[^39]: **Whoever arouses himself to purity is assisted** See BT [[Shabbat 104#104a|*Shabbat* 104a]]: “Resh Lakish said, ‘… If one comes to defile himself, he is provided an opening; if one comes to purify himself, he is assisted.’”

    Cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-53b|1:53b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-54a|54a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-76b|76b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-169b|169b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-198b|198b]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-50a|2:50a]]; [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-47a|3:47a]].

[^40]: **An entity above is not aroused until…** A general principle of Kabbalah. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35a|1:35a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82b|82b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-86b|86b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-88a|88a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-164a|164a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-244a|244a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-31b|2:31b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-265a|265a]]; [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-92a|3:92a]], nob.

    “Entity” renders מלה (*millah*), “word, thing,” referring to the divine word spoken to Abraham and generally to any spiritual entity.

[^41]: **Black light is not joined with white light until…** *Shekhinah*, last of the ten *sefirot*, is known as “the speculum that does not shine” and is symbolized by the color black. She has no light of Her own but reflects the light of the other *sefirot*. Her partner, *Tif’eret*, is “the speculum that shines,” symbolized by the color white. The union of this divine couple depends upon the arousal of *Shekhinah*. See the discussion of the colors of the flame in *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-50b|1:50b]]–51b, [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83b|83b]].

[^42]: **אלהים (*Elohim*), *O God, do not be silent*…** אלהים (*Elohim*) is one of the names of *Shekhinah*. Here She is urged to keep singing and thereby arouse the white light of *Tif’eret* to unite with Her. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-86a|1:86a]]; [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-256b|2:256b]] (*Heikh*).

[^43]: ***O invokers of YHVH, do not be silent!*…** Through prayer and holy action, human beings attract the divine.

[^44]: ***They set out with them from Ur of the Chaldeans*** The verse continues: to *go to the land of Canaan*.

[^45]: ***YHVH said to Abram*** The verse continues: “*Go you forth!”*

[^46]: ***Lekh lekha, Go for yourself*** לך (*Lekh*) means “go.” The following word, לך (*lekha*), means literally “to you, for you,” though idiomatically it lends the verb the sense of “separating, taking leave of.” See [[Torah 17 - Yitro#^18-27|Exodus 18:27]]; [[Joshua 22#^22-4|Joshua 22:4]]; Sarna, *Genesis*, ad loc.

    Rabbi El’azar interprets the phrase hyperliterally *Go for yourself*, for your own benefit. See Rashi and Naḥmanides, ad loc.; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-32a|2:32a]]. Cf. 1:254b (*Hash*); 3:17b, 157a; *[[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-71b|ZḤ* 71b]] (*ShS*).

[^47]: **to perfect your rung** Through discovering God and expressing love, Abraham eventually attained the rung of *Ḥesed* (“Love”). See [[Micah 7#^7-20|Micah 7:20]]; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 1:3; *Bahir* 92 (135), 131–32 (190–91); *Zohar* [[#^z1-80a|1:80a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|83a–84a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|96a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-97b|97b]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-98a|98a]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-14a|3:14a]].

[^48]: **among the wicked** Among the idolaters.

[^49]: **tested** מצרף (*Metsaref*), “Smelts, refines, tests.” In *Sefer Yetsirah* the word means “to permute” the letters of the alphabet. By combining and arranging Hebrew letters in all possible ways, God creates the world. See 2:2: “Twenty-two elemental letters. He \[God\] engraved them, carved them, weighed them, transposed them, וצרפן (*ve-tserefan*), and permuted them—forming with them everything formed and everything destined to be formed.” At the end of the book (6:4), Abraham imitates the divine act: “When Abraham our father, peace unto him, gazed, looked, saw, probed, understood, engraved, carved, וצרף (*ve-tseref*), permuted, formed, and succeeded \[in creating\], then the Lord of all, blessed be He, manifested to him, embraced him, kissed him upon the head, and called him *Abraham, My lover* ([[Isaiah 41#^41-8|Isaiah 41:8]]).”

[^50]: **conduits of the inhabited world** See *Tanḥuma, Qedoshim* 10, where it is said that King Solomon knew the various “veins” or channels running through the earth and was thereby able to effortlessly cultivate all kinds of exotic fruit in Jerusalem. See Naḥmanides’ introduction to his Commentary on the Torah.

    Instead of צנורי (*tsinorei*), “conduits,” several manuscripts and witnesses read סטרי (*sitrei*), “directions, sides.”

[^51]: **balance** תיקלא (*Tiqla*), a Zoharic neologism derived from the root תקל (*tql*), “weigh.” The word has a wide range of meaning: “scale, hollow of the hand, fist, potter’s wheel, water clock (*Zohar* [[#^z1-92b|1:92b]]).” See *Bei’ur ha-Millim ha-Zarot*, 178; Liebes, *Peraqim*, 327–35.

    Here, as suggested by Cordovero (*OY*), the word may refer to “an astrolabe,” a circular instrument used to determine the position of the sun and stars. Originally devised by the Greeks, the astrolabe was further developed by Moslem astronomers, and a description of one of the improved models was translated from Arabic into Castilian by a group of Jewish scholars working under King Alfonso X of Castile (1252–1284). See *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.04 - Parashat Ki Tissa#^z2-188a|2:188a]].

    The image here derives partially from *Sefer Yetsirah* 2:2 (cited above, note 49): “Twenty-two elemental letters. He \[God\] engraved them, carved them, שקלן (*sheqalan*), weighed them…” Abraham imitates the divine act of weighing and gauging the elements of creation.

[^52]: **powers appointed throughout the world** According to rabbinic tradition, the seventy nations of the world are governed by seventy angels or heavenly princes appointed by God.

    See [[Daniel 10#^10-20|Daniel 10:20]]; Septuagint, [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-8|Deuteronomy 32:8–9]]; Jubilees 15:31–32; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 02 - Noach#^11-8|Genesis 11:8]], [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-8|Deuteronomy 32:8–9]]; *Tanḥuma, Re’eh* 8; *Leqaḥ Tov*, [[Torah 02 - Noach#^9-19|Genesis 9:19]]; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 24; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-46b|1:46b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-61a|61a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-108b|108b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-33a|2:33a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-151b|151b]]; [[Zohar 9.16 - Commentary on Ha'azinu, continued#^z3-298b|3:298b]]; Ginzberg, *Legends*, 5:204–5, n. 91.

[^53]: **central point of habitation** The land of Israel, sacred center of the world. See *Tanḥuma, Qedoshim* 10: “The land of Israel sits in the center of the world, Jerusalem in the center of the land of Israel, the Temple in the center of Jerusalem, the nave in the center of the Temple, the ark in the center of the nave, and in front of the ark the Rock of Foundation, from which the world was founded.”

    See Ibn Ezra on [[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^1-2|Genesis 1:2]]; Naḥmanides on [[Torah 29 - Achrei Mot#^18-25|Leviticus 18:25]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-157a|2:157a]], [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-184b|184b]], [[Zohar 6.04 - Parashat Ki Tissa#^z2-193a|193a]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-65b|3:65b]], [[Zohar 9.03 - Parashat Shelah Lekha#^z3-161b|161b]].

    On the phrase שתיה‎ אבן (*even shetiyyah*), “Rock of Foundation” or “Rock of Weaving” (from which the world was woven), see M *Yoma* 5:2; *Tosefta, Yoma* 2:14; BT [[Yoma 54#54b|*Yoma* 54b]]; JT *Yoma* 5:4, 42c; *Vayiqra Rabbah* 20:4; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 35; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-71b|1:71b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-231a|231a]]; Ginzberg, *Legends*, 5:14–15, n. 39; Tur-Sinai, *Ha-Lashon ve-ha-Sefer*, 3:272; Lieberman, *Tosefta ki-Fshutah*, 4:772–73.

[^54]: **but could not attain his desire** Abraham could not determine which heavenly power controlled the land of Israel because God Himself controls the Holy Land. See BT *Ta’anit* 10a: “The land of Israel is watered by the blessed Holy One Himself, whereas the rest of the world is watered by a messenger.”

    Cf. *Tanḥuma, Re’eh* 8; BT [[Ketubot 110#110b|*Ketubbot* 110b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-61a|1:61a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-84b|84b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-108b|108b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-33a|2:33a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-151b|151b]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-209a|209a–b]].

[^55]: **there the entire world was planted** אשתיל (*Ishshetil*), “Was planted,” is a play on the rabbinic wording “from which the world הושתת (*hushtat*), was founded.” See above, note 53; Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 74–75 (95); *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-72a|1:72a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82a|82a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-231a|231a]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-48b|2:48b]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-222a|222a]]; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.09 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^zh-28a|28a]] (*MhN*); Liebes, *Peraqim*, 372–73.

[^56]: **inhabited rungs** The rest of the inhabited world. See *Tosafot, Ketubbot* 112a, s.v. *Rabbi Ḥanina*.

[^57]: **the power presiding over it** *Shekhinah*, also known as the central point. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-6a|1:6a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-71b|71b]].

[^58]: ***Haran*…** A city in northwestern Mesopotamia.

[^59]: **What was Abraham’s reason?** Why did he not continue to the land of Canaan?

[^60]: **who overpowered whom** Abraham knew the entire hierarchy of heavenly powers.

[^61]: **this site** The land of Canaan.

[^62]: **לך‎ לך (*Lekh lekha*), *Go to yourself*, to know yourself…** As indicated above (note 46), לך (*lekh*) means “go,” while the following word, לך (*lekha*), means literally “to you, for you,” though idiomatically it lends the verb the sense of “separating, taking leave of,” so the phrase means *Go you forth*. Previously (page 7), Rabbi El’azar read the phrase hyperliterally: *Go for yourself*, for your own benefit. Here he adopts another hyperliteral reading: *Go to yourself*, know yourself.

    See Philo, *On the Migration of Abraham*, 8,11; *On Dreams*, 1:52–60; and the comment of Moses Zacuto (*MM*), citing Ḥayyim Vital: “Every person must search and discover the root of his soul, so he can fulfill it and restore it to its source, its essence. The more one fulfills himself, the closer he approaches his authentic self.”

[^63]: ***From your land*** The full verse reads: *YHVH said to Abram, “Go you forth from your land, from your birthplace* \[or: *kinfolk*\], *from your father’s house, to the land that I will show you.”*

[^64]: **that wisdom through which you envision and gauge your birth…** Abraham is commanded by God to reject (*go forth from*) astrology. See BT [[Shabbat 156#156a|*Shabbat* 156a]]: “Rav Yehudah said in the name of Rav, ‘…ויוצא (*Va-yotse*), *He took* (*him*) *outside* ([[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^15-5|Genesis 15:5]])…. He \[Abraham\] said before Him \[God\], “I have gazed at my horoscope, and I am not fated to engender a son.” He replied, “צא (*Tse*), Leave, your astrological speculations! Israel is immune from planetary influence.”’”

    See *Zohar* [[#^z1-90a|1:90a]]-b; [[Zohar 8.10 - Commentary on Naso, continued#^z3-148a|3:148a]], 281b (*RM*); *[[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-72b|ZḤ* 72b]] (*ShS*).

[^65]: **whether you are rooted…** Whether you are destined.

[^66]: **Why would He say *Go you forth from your land, from your birthplace?*** Abraham had already left his birthplace: Ur.

[^67]: ***I will make of you a great nation*, because it is written…** Rabbi El’azar juxtaposes the various phrases of the divine command ([[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-1|Genesis 12:1]]) and the divine promise (12:2).

[^68]: **Rabbi Shim’on said…** Rabbi El’azar’s father offers a sefirotic interpretation. The first part of the blessing derives from *Ḥesed*, also known as *Gedullah* (“Greatness”), on the right side.

[^69]: **left side** The *sefirah* of *Gevurah*.

[^70]: **aspect of the middle** *Tif’eret*, balancing right and left.

[^71]: **aspect of the land of Israel** *Shekhinah*, who watches over the land of Israel.

[^72]: **throne of four legs, all comprised in Abraham** This sefirotic tetrad constitutes a throne for *Binah*. All are comprised in Abraham because he symbolizes *Ḥesed*, first of the four. See above, note 47.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 47:6: “Resh Lakish said, ‘The patriarchs themselves constitute the \[divine\] Chariot.’”

    Cf. Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 57; Jacob ben Sheshet, *Ha-Emunah ve-ha-Bittaḥon*, 396; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-5b|1:5b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-20a|20a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-60b|60b]], [[Zohar 1.089b|89b]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-99a|99a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-150a|150a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-154b|154b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-173b|173b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-248b|248b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-144a|2:144a]]; [[Zohar 7.05 - Parashat Shemini#^z3-38a|3:38a]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-99a|99a]]; Baḥya ben Asher on [[Torah 08 - Vayishlach#^32-10|Genesis 32:10]].

[^73]: **since they all departed…** Terah and Lot set out together with Abraham and Sarah from Ur of the Chaldeans.

[^74]: **sincere return of sinners** In repentance. See [[Ezekiel 33#^33-11|Ezekiel 33:11]].

[^75]: **לכם‎ לכו (*Lekhu lakhem*), *Go you forth*** In the plural.

[^76]: **לך‎ לך (*Lekh lekha*), *Go you forth*** In the singular.

[^77]: **He** Rabbi Shim’on.

[^78]: **in order to turn back to God** On the theme of Terah’s repentance, see *Bereshit Rabbah* 30:4; *Tanḥuma, Shemot* 18; Rashi on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^15-15|Genesis 15:15]]; Ibn Ezra on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-1|Genesis 12:1]] (*diqduq*); Naḥmanides on [[Torah 02 - Noach#^11-32|Genesis 11:32]]; above, page 6.

[^79]: **Abraham was saved** From Nimrod’s fiery furnace. See above, note 34.

[^80]: **to him** To Terah.

[^81]: **This verse has been established** In the rabbinic discussion of the primordial light of Creation. See BT [[Chagigah 12#12a|*Ḥagigah* 12a]]: “Rabbi El’azar said, ‘With the light created by the blessed Holy One on the first day, one could gaze and see from one end of the universe to the other. When the blessed Holy One foresaw the corrupt deeds of the generation of the Flood and the generation of the Dispersion \[the generation of the Tower of Babel\], He immediately hid it from them, as is written: *The wicked are denied their light* \[or: *The light of the wicked is withheld*\]. For whom did He hide it? For the righteous in the time to come.’”

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 3:6; 41:3; *Shemot Rabbah* 35:1; *Tanḥuma, Shemini* 9; *Bahir* 97–98 (147); *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-31b|1:31b]]-32a, [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-45b|45b-46a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-47a|47a]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-127a|2:127a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-148b|148b–149a]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-220a|220a–b]]; [[Zohar 8.03 - Parashat Qedoshim#^z3-88a|3:88a]], [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-173b|173b]].

[^82]: **Nimrod** King of Babylonia and Assyria. See [[Torah 02 - Noach#^10-8|Genesis 10:8-12]]; above, note 34.

[^83]: **who led all the inhabitants of the world astray** Nimrod promoted idolatry and, according to rabbinic tradition, instigated the building of the Tower of Babel. See BT [[Avodah Zarah 53#53b|*Avodah Zarah* 53b]]; [[Chullin 89#89a|*Ḥullin* 89a]].

[^84]: ***Lekh lekha, Go for yourself*** The phrase means idiomatically: *Go you forth*, but Rabbi Shim’on reads it hyperliterally. See above, note 46.

[^85]: **that supernal light…** The *sefirah* of *Ḥesed*, identified with the primordial light of Creation. In the land of Israel, Abraham attained and manifested this light. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-77b|1:77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-80a|80a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|83a–b]].

[^86]: ***souls they had made in Haran*** The word נפש (*nefesh*), *souls*, is usually understood as *persons*, and the word עשו (*asu*), *made*, is understood as *acquired*, but Rabbi Shim’on reads them literally, following midrashic tradition. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 39:14: “*And the souls they had made in Haran*. Rabbi El’azar said in the name of Rabbi Yose son of Zimra, ‘If all the nations assembled to create a single mosquito, they could not cast a soul into it, yet you say: *and the souls they had made?!* Rather, these are converts. Then the verse should read: \[*and the souls*\] *they had converted*. Why *they had made?* To teach you that whoever draws a gentile near is as though he created him.’ Then the verse should read: *he* \[Abraham\] *had made*. Why *they had made?* Rabbi Ḥunya said, ‘Abraham converted the men, and Sarah the women.’”

    See *Sifrei*, [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-1|Deuteronomy 32]]; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 1:3; *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 12; *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 12; B, 26.

[^87]: ***As for you, you will go to your fathers in peace*** God’s reassurance to Abraham. As indicated in the Midrash (in the name of Rabbi Abba son of Kahana), this divine promise implied that Terah had turned back to God and attained a place in paradise, where he awaited Abraham.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 30:4; *Tanḥuma, Shemot* 18; Rashi on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^15-15|Genesis 15:15]]; Naḥmanides on [[Torah 02 - Noach#^11-32|Genesis 11:32]].

[^88]: **The verse does not read *Abram set out*,… for they had previously set out…** Abram set out on his journey from Ur of the Chaldeans, so there the verse reads: *They set out…from Ur of the Chaldeans to go to the land of Canaan*. After stopping in Haran, Abram continues his journey, so the text reads: *Abram went*.

[^89]: **all those promises** Recorded in [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-2|Genesis 12:2–3]].

[^90]: **to learn from his actions…** According to rabbinic tradition, Lot learned from Abraham how to welcome guests. See [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^19-1|Genesis 19:1-3]]; *Tanḥuma, Vayera* 11; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Vayera* 15; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 25; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-105a|1:105a]].

[^91]: **This verse has been established** See BT *Ta’anit* 11a: “\[The Rabbis\] said, ‘When a human departs to his eternal home, all his deeds are enumerated before him and he is told: “You did such and such in a certain place on a certain day.” He replies, “Yes.” He is then told, “חתום (*Ḥatom*), Sign!” So he signs, as is said: *By hand every human* יחתום (*yaḥtom*), *seals*.’”

    See *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 307; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Bereshit* 29.

[^92]: **to see what he was not permitted…** To attain a vision of *Shekhinah*, who is invisible as long as the soul is dominated by the body. See *Sifra, Vayiqra, dibbura dindavah* 2:12, 4a: “Rabbi Dosa says, ‘Scripture states: *No human can see Me and live* ([[Torah 21 - Ki Tisa#^33-20|Exodus 33:20]]). In their lifetime they do not see, but in their death they do!’”

    See *Bemidbar Rabbah* 19:18: “Death is decreed upon all the righteous who spring from him \[Adam\]. They do not depart this life without first gazing upon the face of *Shekhinah* and reproving Adam, saying: ‘You inflicted death upon us!’ Adam responds: ‘As for me, I possess only one sin, while in your case, every single one of you possesses more than four.’”

    Cf. *Tanḥuma, Ḥuqqat* 16; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Ḥuqqat* 39; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 34; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-65b|1:65b]], [[Zohar 10.06 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-98a|98a]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-99a|99a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-218b|218b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-226a|226a]]; [[Zohar 8.03 - Parashat Qedoshim#^z3-88a|3:88a]].

[^93]: **three messengers stand over him…** See BT [[Ketubot 104#104a|*Ketubbot* 104a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 10.06 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-98a|1:98a]] (*MhN*); [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-199a|2:199a]]; [[Zohar 8.03 - Parashat Qedoshim#^z3-88a|3:88a]], [[Zohar 8.08 - Parashat Naso#^z3-126b|126b]].

[^94]: **he renders an account…** Before the soul separates from the body at the moment of death. See the parable concerning the joint responsibility of body and soul in *Mekhilta de-Rashbi*, [[Torah 16 - Beshalach#^15-1|Exodus 15:1]]; *Vayiqra Rabbah* 4:5; BT [[Sanhedrin 91#91a|*Sanhedrin* 91a]]–b; and *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-65b|1:65b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-130b|130b]]; [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-199b|2:199b]]; [[Zohar 8.08 - Parashat Naso#^z3-126b|3:126b]].

[^95]: **righteous one should grasp him** Urging him to turn back to God.

[^96]: **Elisha, who rejected Gehazi** After the prophet Elisha cured the leprosy of Na’aman the Aramean general, Na’aman offered him a gift. Elisha refused the gift, but his servant Gehazi secretly accepted it. Elisha then punished Gehazi with leprosy and dismissed him. See [[II Kings 05#^5-1|2 Kings 5]]. According to rabbinic tradition, after leaving Elisha, Gehazi assisted King Jeroboam in the sin of the golden calves, leading Israel astray. When Elisha tried to persuade him to turn back to God, Gehazi refused. See BT [[Sotah 47#47a|*Sotah* 47a]]; *Mekhilta, Amaleq* (*Yitro*) 1.

[^97]: **Similarly with Abraham** Who urged Lot to part from him. See [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^13-9|Genesis 13:9]].

[^98]: ***The people of Sodom*…** Lot lived among them and associated with them.

[^99]: **Regarding what you said…** See above, page 12. If the expression *set out* refers to the origin of the journey from Ur of the Chaldeans, then why does it appear here in connection with Haran, which was simply a stage on the way to Canaan?

[^100]: **the setting out was originally from his birthplace** Ur of the Chaldeans. The setting out from Haran was secondary.

[^101]: ***Abram took his wife Sarai*** The verse continues: *his nephew Lot, all the possessions they had accumulated, and the souls they had made in Haran, and they set out for the land of Canaan*.

[^102]: **What does *took* mean? He coaxed her with fine words** The word is explained similarly in *Mekhilta, Beshallaḥ* 1; *Sifra, Tsav, millu’im* 1:2, 40d; *Sifrei*, Numbers 92, 141; *Bereshit Rabbah* 16:5; *Tanḥuma, Tsav* 10; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Qoraḥ* 2; Rashi on [[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^2-15|Genesis 2:15]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-119b|1:119b]].

[^103]: **a man is not allowed to take his wife to another country…** Although according to the Mishnah, a husband is allowed to take his wife to the land of Israel even without her consent. See M *Ketubbot* 13:11; BT [[Ketubot 110#110b|*Ketubbot* 110b]].

[^104]: **Similarly it is said…** See the sources cited above, note 102.

[^105]: **David was destined to issue from him** Ruth the Moabite, great-grandmother of David, was descended from Lot.

[^106]: ***souls they had made…male* and female converts…** See above, note 86.

[^107]: **הנפש‎ ואת (*ve-et ha-nefesh*), *and the souls*, amplifying the meaning…** Grammatically, the accusative particle את (*et*) has no ascertainable independent sense, but Naḥum of Gimzo and his disciple Rabbi Akiva taught that when et appears in a biblical verse, it amplifies the original meaning. See BT [[Pesachim 22#22b|*Pesaḥim* 22b]]; [[Chagigah 12#12a|*Ḥagigah* 12a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-5b|1:5b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-247a|247a]]; [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-90a|2:90a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-135b|135b]].

    Rabbi Abba does not accept Rabbi El’azar’s view that all the converts accompanied Abraham; only some of them did, but the word et implies that in addition the merit of all the other converted souls joined the journey.

[^108]: **whoever leads another to virtue…** See M *Avot* 5:18: “Moses was virtuous and led many to virtue, so the virtue of many was attributed to him.”

[^109]: **Why did He open with לך‎ לך (*Lekh lekha*)?** Why this sudden command without any introduction?

[^110]: **by its sum He hinted at a hundred** … The numerical value of the phrase לך‎ לך (*Lekh lekha*) is 100, indicating that a son would be born to Abraham when he reached this age. See [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^21-5|Genesis 21:5]]; *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 3; *Leqaḥ Tov*, [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-1|Genesis 12:1]].

[^111]: **site through which one must approach…** *Shekhinah*, last of the ten *sefirot* and the first rung of spiritual ascent. The decade of *sefirot* squared equals 100, alluding to all the sefirotic possibilities contained within *Shekhinah*.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-7b|1:7b]], [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-11b|11b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-36b|36b–37a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77a|77a]] (*ST*); Gikatilla, *Sha’arei Orah*, 4b.

[^112]: **until he entered the land…** Only by entering the Holy Land could Abraham attain the rung of *Shekhinah*, who prevails there.

[^113]: **why didn’t he receive kingship over all Israel immediately?** Why only after dwelling in Hebron?

[^114]: **until he joined the patriarchs, who were in Hebron…** The three patriarchs, who symbolize the sefirotic triad of *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*, were buried in the Cave of Machpelah in Hebron. David could not attain kingship until he joined them there because *Shekhinah* (known as *Malkhut* \[“Kingship”\]) is completed only by that triad. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-99a|1:99a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-125a|125a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-246b|246b]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-31a|2:31a]].

[^115]: **he lingered there for seven years…** See [[II Samuel 02#^2-11|2 Samuel 2:11]]. The seven years symbolize the seven lower *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* to *Shekhinah*.

[^116]: **Abraham did not enter the covenant** … Abraham was given the covenantal command of circumcision only after he entered the land, symbolizing *Shekhinah*. See [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^17-1|Genesis 17]]. By being circumcised, one identifies with *Yesod*, the divine phallus, who unites with *Shekhinah*.

[^117]: ***Abram passed through the land*** The verse continues: *as far as the site of Shechem, as far as the terebinth of Moreh. The Canaanite was then in the land*.

[^118]: **holy name by which the world is sealed…** The divine act of sealing limits the expansion of the universe and sets its boundaries.

    See *Sefer Yetsirah* 1:13; *Tanḥuma, Bereshit* 1; *Alfa Beita de-Rabbi Aqiva* (*Battei Midrashot*, 2:363–64); *[[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-76b|ZḤ* 76b]] (*MhN, Rut*); Liebes, *Torat ha-Yetsirah*, 177–89.

    The seventy-two-letter divine name is derived from the description of the splitting of the Red Sea: [[Torah 16 - Beshalach#^14-19|Exodus 14:19-21]]. Each of these three verses contains seventy-two letters. The name is composed of seventy-two triads according to the following pattern: the first letter of the first verse, the last letter of the second verse, the first letter of the third verse (forming the first triad); the second letter of the first verse, the penultimate letter of the second verse, the second letter of the third verse (the second triad); etc.

    Here Rabbi Shim’on finds an allusion to this name in the word ויעבר (*Va-ya’avor*), *He passed*, which can be divided into two numbers: ע"ב (72), the number of triads, and רי״ו (216) the total number of letters. Elsewhere (*Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-132b|2:132b]]; [[Zohar 8.11 - Parashat Be-Ha'alotekha#^z3-150b|3:150b–151a]]) the name is associated with *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*.

    See *Leqaḥ Tov*, [[Torah 16 - Beshalach#^14-21|Exodus 14:21]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 44:19; *Vayiqra Rabbah* 23:2; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 2:2; Hai Gaon, *Otsar ha-Ge’onim, Ḥagigah*, 23; Rashi on BT [[Sukkah 45#45a|*Sukkah* 45a]], s.v. *ani;* Ibn Ezra on [[Torah 16 - Beshalach#^14-19|Exodus 14:19]]; *Bahir* 79 (110); *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-7b|1:7b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-17a|17a]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-51b|2:51b-52a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-152a|152a]], 270a; Trachtenberg, *Jewish Magic and Superstition*, 95-97; Kasher, *Torah Shelemah*, 14:67, 284–86.

[^119]: **there is written: יהוה‎ ויעבר (*Va-ya’avor YHVH*)**… The verse continues with a proclamation of the divine name and attributes.

[^120]: ***The Book of Rav Yeiva Sava*** One of the many volumes housed in the real or imaginary library of the author(s) of the *Zohar*. It is cited frequently (1:47b, 117b; 2:6a, 60b, 206b; 3:7b, 155b, 289a, 290a, 295a \[*IZ*\]). See Matt, *Zohar*, 25; and the comment by Shim’on Lavi, *KP*, 1:22d: “All such books mentioned in the *Zohar*… have been lost in the wanderings of exile… Nothing is left of them except what is mentioned in the *Zohar*.”

[^121]: ***I will make* (*all My goodness*) *pass*** The verse continues: *before your face, I will proclaim the name YHVH in front of you*.

[^122]: **holiness of the land, from a fittingly supernal site** The holiness of *Shekhinah*, symbolized by the land, emanating from a higher sefirotic source.

[^123]: **from one side to the other, fittingly** Apparently, through all aspects of *Shekhinah*, or through the entire realm of the *sefirot*, as reflected in *Shekhinah*.

[^124]: **until then the evil serpent prevailed** … *Canaan* is identified with the demonic serpent who tempted Eve. Even after Abraham entered the land, this demonic power still prevailed. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-73a|1:73a]].

[^125]: **There** In the land of Canaan.

[^126]: **that profound power ruling the land** *Shekhinah*. See above, page 9.

[^127]: ***He moved on from there* ההרה (*ha-harah*), *to the mountain*** The verse continues: *east of Bethel, and spread his tent…, and he built an altar there to YHVH*.

[^128]: **There he discovered ה׳‎ הר (*har he*), Mountain of *He*…** Rabbi Shim’on interprets the final ה (*he*) of the word ההרה (*ha-harah*), *to the mountain*, as an allusion to the divine, because *Shekhinah* is symbolized by the final ה (*he*) of the name יהוה (*YHVH*), or because the letter *he* stands for השם (*hashem*), “the \[divine\] name.”

[^129]: **all those rungs planted there** The various *sefirot* planted in *Shekhinah*.

[^130]: ***He spread* אהלה (*oholoh*), *his tent*, spelled with a ה (*he*)** The spelling here in the biblical text is unusual; normally the masculine pronominal suffix is indicated by the letter ו (*vav*). The ה (*he*), a feminine marker, stimulates Rabbi Shim’on to interpret the word as: *her tent*, namely, the tent of *Shekhinah*.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 39:15; *Minḥat Shai* on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-8|Genesis 12:8]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-73a|1:73a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|83a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-84a|84a]].

[^131]: **spreading a spread, accepting the Kingdom…** Abraham spread the canopy of *Shekhinah*, who is known as *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”), and acknowledged Her dominion.

[^132]: **one for the revealed rung, one for the concealed** One for *Shekhinah*, one for the concealed realm of *Binah*, the Divine Mother. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-219a|2:219a]]; [[Zohar 7.04 - Parashat Tsav#^z3-30a|3:30a–b]].

[^133]: **from rung to rung** Abraham’s journey is not only geographical but also spiritual, proceeding from *Shekhinah* through various *sefirot*.

[^134]: **his rung… *the Negev*—the South, share of Abraham** The Negev is the southern region of Canaan and, by extension, *negev* also means “south” in biblical Hebrew. Here it is identified with the warmth of *Ḥesed* (“Love”). Through discovering God and expressing love, Abraham attained this rung.

    See [[Micah 7#^7-20|Micah 7:20]]; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 1:3; *Bahir* 92 (135), 131–32 (190–91); *Zohar* [[#^z1-77b|1:77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83a|83a–84a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|96a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-97b|97b]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-98a|98a]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-14a|3:14a]].

[^135]: ***famine in the land*, for no one knew how to draw near…** See [[Amos 8#^8-11|Amos 8:11]]: *Behold, days are coming, declares YHVH, when I will send a famine through the land: not a famine of bread or a thirst for water, but of hearing the word of YHVH*.

[^136]: **power over the land had not vitalized or nourished the land…** *Shekhinah* had not nourished the land. Both the land and *Shekhinah* Herself were not yet purified of the demonic, symbolized by Canaan. See above, pages 17–18.

[^137]: **How did Abraham know?** That the land was not yet sanctified.

[^138]: **only through certain rungs issuing from him** The land and its divine provider, *Shekhinah*, would be fully arrayed and perfected only when Isaac and Jacob issued from Abraham. Together, the patriarchal triad symbolizes *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*, who convey the flow of emanation to *Shekhinah*.

[^139]: **equivalent to the Garden of *YHVH*…** Egypt was famous for the rich alluvial soil along the Nile. See [[Torah 46 - Eikev#^11-10|Deuteronomy 11:10]].

[^140]: **one river on the right… *Pishon*…** According to [[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^2-10|Genesis 2:10-11]], a river issuing from Eden watered the Garden and then branched into four streams, the first of which was named Pishon. Various authorities identify Pishon with the Nile; see Samaritan Targum, *Midrash Aggadah*, Sa’adya Gaon, Rashi, Ibn Ezra, Naḥmanides, ad loc.; *Zohar* [[Zohar 10.07 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-125a|1:125a]] (*MhN*).

    Here Rabbi Shim’on indicates that Pishon conveyed the flow of emanation from the right side of the *sefirot* to Egypt.

[^141]: **perfect faith** The realm of the *sefirot*, specifically *Shekhinah*.

[^142]: **all those rungs linked below** Including the demonic realm, symbolized by Egypt.

[^143]: **since Egypt drew from the right, he went down to Egypt** Abraham was linked to *Ḥesed*, on the right, so he journeyed to Egypt, nourished by Pishon from the right.

[^144]: **Famine prevails only when compassion withdraws from justice** When *Tif’eret*, known as *Raḥamim* (“Compassion”), separates from *Shekhinah*, characterized by *Din* (“Justice, Judgment”), the flow of emanation ceases and famine prevails.

[^145]: **The verse should read: קרב‎ כאשר (*Ka’asher qarav*)…** The plain sense of the verse requires an intransitive verb, קרב‎ כאשר (*ka’asher qarav*), *when he came near*. So why does Scripture employ a transitive verb: הקריב‎ כאשר (*ka’asher hiqriv*), *when he drew* \[someone\] *near*?

[^146]: **As is written, *Pharaoh hiqriv, drew* \[someone\] *near*…** See *Shemot Rabbah* 21:5: “What is the meaning of *Pharaoh drew* \[someone\] *near?* He drew Israel back to God. Rabbi Berekhiah said, ‘Pharaoh’s drawing near was better for Israel than a hundred fasts and prayers. Why? Because when \[the Egyptians\] pursued them and \[the Israelites\] saw them, they were terrified and lifted their eyes to heaven and returned \[to God\] and prayed.’” See *Bereshit Rabbah* 40:6; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-47a|2:47a]].

[^147]: **those rungs** The demonic stages linked to the divine.

[^148]: **Because Abraham went down to Egypt without permission…** The wording *Abram went down to Egypt* implies that Abraham went on his own, not as the result of a divine command. See Naḥmanides, ad loc., who explains that Abraham should have remained in Canaan, trusting in God’s ability to provide for him during the famine.

[^149]: **troubled all that night over Sarah** The anguish that Abraham must have experienced when Sarah was taken by Pharaoh ([[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-15|Genesis 12:15]]) was a punishment for his claiming that she was his sister.

[^150]: **until that moment he had never gazed at Sarah’s features…** See *Midrash Aggadah*, ad loc.: “*Now I know*. He had never looked at his wife. When he drew near to Egypt there was a river, and they went in together. She fell and he helped her up. When they reached the riverbank and she was drying her clothes, he saw her leg. He immediately said, *‘Now I know* \[*what a beautiful woman you are*\]*!*’”

    See *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 5: “When they reached the gates of Egypt and stood at the Nile, Abraham saw Sarah’s reflection in the river radiating like the sun. From here the sages have learned that all women compared with Sarah are like an ape compared with a human. He said to her, *‘Now I know what a beautiful woman you are!’* From here you learn that previously he did not know her.”

    See BT [[Bava Batra 16#16a|*Bava Batra* 16a]]; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-11|Genesis 12:11]].

[^151]: **This word ascends in two nuances** Similar expressions appear in *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-48b|1:48b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-54a|54a]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-197b|197b]].

[^152]: **one literal** Abraham claimed that Sarah was his sister, not his wife, because he feared that otherwise Pharaoh would have him killed. See [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^20-12|Genesis 20:12]], where Abraham explains that Sarah was his half-sister.

[^153]: ***Say to wisdom, ‘You are my sister’*** *Shekhinah* is known as Lower Wisdom, deriving from the *sefirah* of *Ḥokhmah* (“Wisdom”). The verse in Proverbs is applied to Her, and similarly Abraham called Her *sister*. See Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 20; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-111b|1:111b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-140b|140b]].

[^154]: **Similarly it is written: *Say you are my sister*, and: *You speak with us*** In both verses, Rabbi Yehudah understands the feminine form את (*at*), *you*, as alluding to the divine feminine, *Shekhinah*. Though in Deuteronomy the verse is addressed to Moses, here it is interpreted as referring to *Shekhinah* speaking through Moses.

[^155]: **thereby…** Through communion with *Shekhinah*, one attains longevity or eternal life in the world to come. See BT [[Kiddushin 39#39b|*Qiddushin* 39b]].

[^156]: **all those Egyptians were steeped in lechery** See *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 5; *Mekhilta de-Rashbi*, [[Torah 15 - Bo#^12-33|Exodus 12:33]]; *Shemot Rabbah* 1:18; BT [[Yevamot 98#98a|*Yevamot* 98a]].

[^157]: **Because he saw *Shekhinah*** And he was sure that She would protect Sarah. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 60:16; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-50a|1:50a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-111b|111b]] 133a, 140b; 3:52a.

[^158]: **He had put her in a chest…** See *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 5: “\[Abraham\] said to her, ‘The Egyptians are steeped in whoredom…. I will put you in a chest and lock you up, for I fear for myself. *When the Egyptians see you* \[*and say, “This is his wife,” they will kill me and let you live*\]’ ([[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-12|Genesis 12:12]]). Having done so, he approached to cross the border. The customs officials gathered and asked him, ‘What are you carrying inside the chest?’ He answered, ‘Barley.’ They said, ‘It must be wheat!’ He said, ‘So collect duty for wheat.’ They said, ‘It is pepper!’ He said, ‘So collect duty for pepper.’ They said, ‘It must be gold!’ Pressing him, they opened the chest and saw it radiant as the sun.” See *Bereshit Rabbah* 40:5.

    The word “customs” renders the Zoharic Aramaic קוסטונא (*qostuna*); cf. the Latin *costumen*.

[^159]: **they saw another radiance in the chest** Instead of נהורא (*nehora*), “radiance,” several manuscripts read: דיוקנא (*deyoqna*), “image,” referring to the image of Eve. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 40:5, where it is said in the name of Rabbi Yitsḥak that Sarah’s beauty outshone Eve’s.

[^160]: **saw her as before** So they realized that the intense radiance emanated from her alone.

[^161]: **Here is written: *great plagues*** The full verse reads: *YHVH plagued Pharaoh and his household with great plagues because of Sarai, Abram’s wife.*

[^162]: ***YHVH displayed signs and wonders, great and terrible, in Egypt*** The word *great* appears in both verses, linking the two sets of plagues. See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 26: “Rabbi Tarfon says, ‘The night that our mother Sarah was taken was the night of Passover. The blessed Holy One brought great plagues upon Pharaoh and his household to indicate that in the future He would smite the Egyptians with great plagues, as is said: *YHVH plagued Pharaoh with great plagues.’”* See Naḥmanides on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-6|Genesis 12:6]], 10.

[^163]: **Just as there ten plagues, so here ten plagues** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-52a|3:52a]].

[^164]: **Why don’t they conclude a blessing with me as they do with Abraham…?** In the *amidah* prayer, the first blessing concludes: “Blessed are You, *YHVH*, Shield of Abraham.” David insists that God also serves as a shield for him, so he too deserves to be mentioned in the conclusion of one of the blessings.

    See BT [[Pesachim 117#117b|*Pesaḥim* 117b]]; [[Sanhedrin 107#107a|*Sanhedrin* 107a]]; *Midrash Tehillim* 18:8, 25; Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 45 (54); Ginzberg, *Legends*, 6:265, n. 94. Cf. *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 1; BT [[Berakhot 16#16b|*Berakhot* 16b]]; [[Acts 02#^2-29|Acts 2:29]].

    Actually, an alternative version of one of the blessings of the *amidah* does conclude with David’s name. See *Tosefta, Berakhot* 3:25; JT *Berakhot* 4:5, 8c; *Rosh ha-Shanah* 4:6, 59c; *Midrash Shemu’el* 26:3; *Midrash Tehillim* 29:2; Abraham ben Isaac, *Sefer ha-Eshkol*, 28; Ezra of Gerona, on [[Megillah 17#17b|*Megillah* 17b]] (cited in Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 57; cf. 21, n. 5); Jacob ben Sheshet, *Ha-Emunah ve-ha-Bittaḥon*, 396; Baḥya ben Asher on [[Torah 08 - Vayishlach#^32-10|Genesis 32:10]].

[^165]: **Abraham I have already tried and tested…** According to rabbinic tradition, Abraham passed ten divine tests. See Jubilees 17:17; 19:8; M *Avot* 5:3; *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 33; B, 36; *Midrash Tehillim* 18:25; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 26.

[^166]: ***reins*** Kidneys, the seat of emotion.

[^167]: **that affair with Bathsheba** The wife of Uriah the Hittite. Seeing her bathing, David was attracted to her and slept with her. He then arranged for her husband, Uriah, to die in battle, after which he married her. See [[II Samuel 11#^11-1|2 Samuel 11]]–12.

[^168]: **that word I conceived** His request to be tested.

[^169]: **they do conclude a blessing with him… Shield of David** The conclusion of the third blessing following the *haftarah* (the reading from the Prophets on Sabbaths and festivals).

[^170]: **This rung is my glory…** King David symbolizes the *sefirah* of *Shekhinah*, known as *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”) and *Kavod* (“Glory”).

[^171]: **Why does Scripture compare the righteous to a palm tree?…** See *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 5; *Bemidbar* 15; *Midrash Tehillim* 92:11; *Bereshit Rabbah* 40(41):1 (on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-17|Genesis 12:17]]); BT *Ta’anit* 25a–b; *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-16a|3:16a]].

[^172]: **Just as a palm tree rises only male and female…** Palm trees are dioecious: male and female flowers grow on separate trees. Male flowers produce pollen, while female flowers develop into fruit. As early as 2300 B.C.E., Mesopotamian farmers had learned to hang a male flower in a female tree to enhance pollination.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 40(41):1; *Midrash Tehillim* 92:11; BT [[Pesachim 56#56a|*Pesaḥim* 56a]]; *Bahir* 117 (172), 139 (198), and Scholem’s note, ad loc.; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-37b|2:37b]], [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-126a|126a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-16a|3:16a]], [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-135b|135b]] (*IR*); Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Mishqal*, 128.

[^173]: **a righteous one arises only male and female** Only if he discovers and joins his sexual partner, thereby imitating the union of the divine couple, *Tif’eret* and *Shekhinah*.

[^174]: **upon a single righteous one…** See BT [[Chagigah 12#12b|*Ḥagigah* 12b]]: “Rabbi El’azar son of Shamu’a says, ‘\[The world stands\] on a single pillar named Righteous One, as is said: *The righteous one is the foundation of the world*.’” Cf. [[Yoma 38#38b|*Yoma* 38b]]. The verse in Proverbs, עולם‎ יסוד‎ וצדיק (*Ve-tsaddiq yesod olam*), which means literally *The righteous one is an everlasting foundation*, is understood as *The righteous one is the foundation of the world*. In Kabbalah the *sefirah* of *Yesod* (“Foundation”) is known as Righteous One, based on this same verse. He constitutes the cosmic pillar.

    See *Bahir* 71 (102); Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 34.

[^175]: **world stands on seven supports…** See BT [[Chagigah 12#12b|*Ḥagigah* 12b]]. The full verse in Proverbs reads: *Wisdom has built her house, she has hewn her seven pillars*.

[^176]: **all those others endure through the seventh…** *Yesod*, Righteous One (also known as *good*), conveys the emanation of all the *sefirot* from *Binah* and below. Counting from Binah, He is the seventh. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-186a|1:186a]].

[^177]: **this support upon which the world stands, who waters the garden…** The river of *Yesod* waters the garden of *Shekhinah*.

[^178]: **Who are they? Souls of the righteous…** Souls are engendered by the union of the divine couple, *Yesod* and *Shekhinah*. Through good deeds and intensive study, righteous souls sustain the world and Torah.

    On souls as fruit, see *Bahir* 14 (22); Ezra of Gerona, *Peirush Shir ha-Shirim*, 489, 504; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15b|1:15b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-19a|19a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-33a|33a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-59b|59b–60a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-85b|85b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-115a|115a–b]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-223b|2:223b]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Mishqal*, 51; idem, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 56 (69). Cf. Ibn Ezra on [[Psalms 001#^1-3|Psalms 1:3]].

[^179]: **at the moment of midnight the blessed Holy One comes…** See above, note 13.

[^180]: **With all of them…** With all souls, both of those who have died and of those still living.

[^181]: **The world on high needs the arousal of the world below** A general principle of Kabbalah. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35a|1:35a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-86b|86b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-88a|88a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-164a|164a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-244a|244a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-31b|2:31b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-265a|265a]]; [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-92a|3:92a]], [[Zohar 8.05 - Parashat Be-Har#^z3-110b|110b]].

[^182]: **they all clothe themselves in supernal light…** To enter and experience higher dimensions, the soul is enveloped in a radiant garment. According to *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-224a|1:224a]]–b, this garment is woven out of one’s virtuous days. Parallels appear in Islamic and Iranian eschatology—and also in Mahayana Buddhism, according to which the Buddha enjoys *sambhogakaya* (“a body of bliss”), generated by merit accrued over aeons.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-7a|1:7a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-66a|66a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91a|91a]]; [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-11a|2:11a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-150a|150a]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-210a|210a–b]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-229b|229b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-247a|247a]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-70b|3:70b]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-101a|101a]], [[Zohar 9.05 - Commentary on Shelah Lekha, continued#^z3-174b|174b]]; *Seder Gan Eden* (*Beit ha-Midrash*, 3:133); Scholem, *Major Trends*, 77–78; idem, in *Tarbiz* 24 (1955): 290–306; idem, *Kabbalah*, 158–59; idem, *On the Mystical Shape of the Godhead*, 264–65; Nakamura Hajime, in *Encyclopedia of Religion*, ed. Eliade, 2:458.

    Cf. *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 36; *Bereshit Rabbah* 19:6; *Shemot Rabbah* 1:35; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 14; [[Matthew 22#^22-1|Matthew 22:1–14]].

[^183]: **Even those on earth? How?** How can souls of the living ascend and commune with God?

[^184]: **Because at midnight all the truly virtuous awaken…** See [[Psalms 119#^119-62|Psalms 119:62]] and BT [[Berakhot 3#3b|*Berakhot* 3b]]: “Rabbi Shim’on the Ḥasid said, ‘There was a harp suspended above \[King\] David’s bed. As soon as midnight arrived, a north wind came and blew upon it, and it played by itself. He immediately arose and engaged in Torah until the break of dawn.’”

    In the *Zohar* this legendary custom is expanded into a ritual: all kabbalists are expected to rise at midnight and adorn *Shekhinah* with words of Torah and song in preparation for Her union with *Tif’eret*. See Scholem, *On the Kabbalah*, 146–50; above, note 13.

[^185]: **a thread of grace emanates upon them by day…** See BT [[Chagigah 12#12b|*Ḥagigah* 12b]]: “Resh Lakish said, ‘Whoever engages in Torah at night, the blessed Holy One emanates a thread of grace upon him by day, as is said: *By day YHVH directs His grace*. Why? Because *at night His song is with me.*’” *His song* is the song of Torah.

    See Maimonides, *Mishneh Torah, Hilkhot Talmud Torah* 3:13; *Zohar* [[#^z1-92a|1:92a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-178b|178b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-194b|194b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-149a|2:149a]].

[^186]: **When the Israelites were confined in their homes…** See [[Torah 15 - Bo#^12-22|Exodus 12:22]]; *Midrash Tehillim* 113:1.

[^187]: **King David would rise at midnight** See above, note 184.

[^188]: **So David is king forever** According to BT [[Berakhot 57#57b|*Berakhot* 57b]], “Sleep is one-sixtieth of death.” By rising at midnight and avoiding sleep, David escaped (or deferred) death.

    See [[Sukkah 26#26b|*Sukkah* 26b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-206b|1:206b]]–207b. Several witnesses (O2, Cr, *KP, OY*) read: “So King David lives forever.” Cf. the saying cited by Rabbi Yehudah ha-Nasi in BT [[Rosh Hashanah 25#25a|*Rosh ha-Shanah* 25a]] (based on [[Psalms 089#^89-38|Psalms 89:38]]): “David, King of Israel, lives and endures.”

[^189]: **If King Messiah is of the living, David is his name…** See *Eikhah Rabbah* 1:51; JT *Berakhot* 2:3, 5a; BT [[Sanhedrin 98#98b|*Sanhedrin* 98b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-232b|2:232b]]; [[Zohar 8.03 - Parashat Qedoshim#^z3-84a|3:84a]]; Ginzberg, *Legends*, 6:272, n. 128. According to kabbalistic theory, the Messiah will be a reincarnation of King David.

[^190]: **prefiguring what I intend to do later** When God afflicted the Egyptians with the ten plagues. See above, pp. 22–23 and n. 162.

[^191]: ***YHVH plagued Pharaoh with great plagues*…** The verse reads: *YHVH plagued Pharaoh and his household with great plagues because of Sarai, Abram’s wife*.

[^192]: **How did he know?** How did Pharaoh know that Sarai was Abram’s wife?

[^193]: **as with Abimelech…** King of Gerar, when a similar episode took place. Then God told Abimelech explicitly that Sarah was Abraham’s wife. According to *Bereshit Rabbah* 52:5, Abimelech was considered a Gentile prophet.

[^194]: **This is what they said to him: *‘Because of Sarai, Abram’s wife’*** As angels struck Pharaoh that night, they told him why he was being punished. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 40:2; *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 5.

[^195]: **He did not converse with him** God did not converse with Pharaoh directly.

[^196]: ***And they sent him off*…** The biblical text is ambiguous, implying either that the guards accompanied and protected Abraham’s clan or enforced their expulsion. Rabbi Yitsḥak adopts the first interpretation. See *Targum Onqelos* and Ibn Ezra, ad loc.; BT [[Sotah 46#46b|*Sotah* 46b]].

[^197]: **The blessed Holy One said to him** To Pharaoh.

[^198]: ***When Pharaoh sent off the people,* escorting them…** See *Mekhilta, Beshallaḥ, Petiḥta*.

[^199]: **site of the world’s greatest magicians** See BT [[Kiddushin 49#49b|*Qiddushin* 49b]]: “Ten kabs \[measures\] of sorcery descended to the world; nine were taken by Egypt \[and one by the rest of the world\].”

[^200]: ***Negev*** The Negev is the southern region of Canaan and, by extension, *negev* also means “south” in biblical Hebrew. Here it is identified with the warmth of *Ḥesed*, the *sefirah* of Abraham. See above, p. 19 and n. 134.

[^201]: **rungs below, to the depths of which Abraham descended** Having discovered the divine realm, Abraham sought to explore the dark underside of wisdom. His descent to Egypt symbolizes his encounter with the demonic, a crucial stage in his spiritual transformation.

[^202]: **not seduced by them like Adam…** According to Kabbalah, Adam’s sin consisted in a misapprehension of the *sefirot*. Deceived by the serpent of false knowledge, he separated *Shekhinah* from the other *sefirot* instead of unifying them through pure contemplation. This tragic separation left *Shekhinah* vulnerable, enabling the demonic powers to dominate Her. Ever since, She has ruled the world under the apsect of the Tree of Death.

    On the nature of Adam and Eve’s sin, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35b|1:35b]]–36b, [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-51a|51a-53b]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z1-221a|221a–b]]; Scholem, *Major Trends*, 231–32, 236, 404–5, n. 105; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 1:373–76. On the relation between *Shekhinah* and the demonic, see Scholem, *On the Mystical Shape of the Godhead*, 189–92; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 1:376–79.

[^203]: **not seduced like Noah…** Noah tried to repair the damage caused by Adam and reunite *Shekhinah* with the higher *sefirot*. However, intoxicated by the wine of false imagining, he blundered and exposed “the breach of the world” (*Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-73a|1:73a]]–b). See Recanati on [[Torah 02 - Noach#^9-20|Genesis 9:20–21]], 20b.

    In the Torah, the final letter of the word אהלה (*oholoh*), *his tent*, is a ה (*he*), rather than the normal masculine possessive suffix ו (*vav*). The suffix ה (*he*) usually denotes the feminine possessive, *her*. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 36:4: “Rabbi Yehudah son of Rabbi Simon and Rabbi Ḥanan said in the name of Rabbi Shemu’el son of Rav Yitsḥak, ‘… It is spelled אהלה (*oholah*), *her tent:* inside the tent of his wife.’” In other words, Noah shamed himself in his wife’s tent. See Theodor’s note, ad loc.

    Here similarly, Rabbi Shim’on reads the word as *oholah, her tent*, but he takes this to mean: “the tent of *Shekhinah*,” site of Noah’s sin. See above, note 130.

[^204]: **supernal rung he had grasped before** Through discovering God and expressing love, Abraham had attained the rung of *Ḥesed* (“Love”).

    See [[Micah 7#^7-20|Micah 7:20]]; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 1:3; *Bahir* 92 (135), 131–32 (190–91); *Zohar* [[#^z1-77b|1:77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-80a|80a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-83b|83b–84a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|96a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-97b|97b]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-98a|98a]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-14a|3:14a]].

[^205]: **been refined** יצטרף (*Yitstaraf*), “Been tested, refined.” Through withstanding the power and temptation of the demonic powers symbolized by Egypt, Abraham was refined.

    See *Zohar* [[#^z1-83b|1:83b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-140b|140b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-147b|147b–148b]] (*ST*); [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-34a|2:34a]], [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-184a|184a]]; Tishby, “*Wisdom of the Zohar*, 2:457–58.

    Abraham’s descent to Egypt constituted one of ten tests that he passed. See Jubilees 17:17; 19:8; M *Avot* 5:3; *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 33; B, 36; *Midrash Tehillim* 18:25; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 26; above, page 23.

[^206]: **share of the blessed Holy One** Selected by God and confirmed as the incarnation of *Ḥesed*. See [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-9|Deuteronomy 32:9]]; *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 312.

[^207]: **if the Holy Land had not been given first to Canaan…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-73b|1:73b]].

[^208]: **people… do not care to know… the foundation…** See BT [[Chagigah 12#12b|*Ḥagigah* 12b]]: “Rabbi Yose says, ‘Woe to creatures, who see yet know not what they see, who stand yet know not upon what they stand!’”

[^209]: **They have established this verse and so have we** See *Bemidbar Rabbah* 3:1; *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-67a|3:67a]]–68a.

[^210]: **his soul leaves him and ascends on high** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 14:9: “It was said in the name of Rabbi Me’ir: ‘This soul fills the body, and when a person sleeps she ascends, drawing down life from above.’”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-130a|1:130a]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-200a|200a]]; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 2:809–14.

[^211]: **pint…** קסטו (*Qistu*), derived from the Greek *xestes*, a measure about the size of a pint. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-33a|1:33a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-121b|121b]]. Barely enough vitality remains to keep the body alive.

[^212]: **countless rungs upon rungs to climb** re reaching the divine realm.

[^213]: **hooded, hunchbacked, dazzling demons…** טהירין‎ קומרין (*Qumrin tehirin*), malevolent forces who block the ascent of an unworthy soul. The cryptic phrase reappears in *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-109a|1:109a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-251a|251a]] (*Hash*).

    *Qumrin* derives from the Greek *qamara*, “arched cover.” See *Bereshit Rabbah* 31:11; M *Eruvin* 8:9–10; *Arukh*, s.v. *qmr; Zohar* 1:61b.

    *Tehirin* is a cognate of טיהרא (*tihara*), meaning “brightness, noon.” One class of demons is named טהרי (*tiharei*), “noonday demons.” See [[Psalms 091#^91-6|Psalms 91:6]], and Rashi, ad loc.; *Targum*, [[Song of Songs 4#^4-6|Song of Songs 4:6]]; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 35 - Nasso#^6-24|Numbers 6:24]], [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-24|Deuteronomy 32:24]]. The Hebrew root טהר (*thr*), “pure,” lends this demonic name a euphemistic tone.

[^214]: **not having been defiled during the day By** sin.

[^215]: **she grasps what is imminent** Since demons possess limited foreknowledge. See Naḥmanides on [[Torah 29 - Achrei Mot#^17-7|Leviticus 17:7]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-130a|1:130a]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-200a|200a]]; [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-251b|2:251b]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-25a|3:25a]].

[^216]: **disseminating deceptions** Through false dreams. A rabbinic tradition in BT [[Berakhot 55#55b|*Berakhot* 55b]] states that angels convey prophetic dreams, while demons convey false ones. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-130a|1:130a]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-200a|200a]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-130a|2:130a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-25a|3:25a]], [[Zohar 8.11 - Parashat Be-Ha'alotekha#^z3-156b|156b]].

[^217]: **to her place** To the body.

[^218]: **all those rungs** Of defilement.

[^219]: **on her own path** Corresponding to her virtues and her conduct on earth.

[^220]: **Countenance of Days** The face of God, Ancient of Days, a title deriving from [[Daniel 07#^7-9|Daniel 7:9]]: *The Ancient of Days sat, …the hair on His head like clean fleece, His throne—flames of fire*.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 35:2; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.089b|1:89b]] (*ST*), 130a, 188a; 3:132b (*IR*), 201a; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.03 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^zh-19a|19a]] (*MhN*).

[^221]: **to gaze upon the loveliness of the King…** A paraphrase of [[Psalms 027#^27-4|Psalms 27:4]].

[^222]: **shares constantly in the world that is coming** He tastes here and now the richness of eternal life.

    דאתי‎ עלמא (*Alma de-atei*), the Aramaic equivalent of the rabbinic Hebrew הבא‎ העולם (*ha-olam ha-ba*), “the world that is coming,” is often understood as referring to the hereafter and is usually translated as “the world to come.” From another point of view, however, “the world that is coming” already exists, occupying another dimension. See *Tanḥuma, Vayiqra* 8: “The wise call it *ha-olam ha-ba* not because it does not exist now, but for us today in this world it is still to come.” Cf. Maimonides, *Mishneh Torah, Hilkhot Teshuvah* 8:8; and Guttmann, *Philosophies of Judaism*, 37: “‘The world to come’ does not succeed ‘this world’ in time, but exists from eternity as a reality outside and above time, to which the soul ascends.”

    In Kabbalah “the world that is coming” often refers to *Binah*, the continuous source of emanation, who gives birth to the lower *sefirot*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-290b|3:290b]] (*IZ*): “the world that is coming, constantly coming, never ceasing.”

    Cf. *Bahir* 106 (160); Asher ben David, *Peirush Shelosh Esreh Middot*, in *Kabbalah 2* (1997): 293; Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 26 (30); idem, *Sod Eser Sefirot*, 375; *Zohar* [[#^z1-92a|1:92a]]–b.

[^223]: **realm from which she issued** *Shekhinah*, who gives birth to the soul.

[^224]: **her rung** *Shekhinah*, symbolized by the night.

[^225]: **רוח (*ruaḥ*), spirit, ruling by day** *Ruaḥ* seeks its source, *Tif’eret*, symbolized by the day. On the various aspects of the soul, see Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 2:684–714.

[^226]: **one rung, two joined as one** These two dimensions of soul, *nefesh* and *ruaḥ*, constitute a unity.

[^227]: **נשמה (*neshamah*), soul breath** The highest dimension of soul.

[^228]: **All those rungs culminate in the mystery of wisdom…** The levels of soul correspond to various *sefirot*, so by contemplating them one discovers aspects of the divine. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-142a|2:142a]].

[^229]: **This *neshamah* enters them and they cleave to it** As the *neshamah* enters a human being, it is clothed in the two lower levels: *nefesh* and *ruaḥ*.

[^230]: ***Nefesh*, soul, is lower arousal…** She vitalizes and animates the body.

[^231]: **Afterward she is arrayed** The *nefesh* is adorned and enhanced by virtuous conduct of the limbs of the body.

[^232]: **throne on which settles *ruaḥ*, spirit** *Ruaḥ* constitutes a higher illumination. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-70b|3:70b]].

[^233]: **through the arousal of *nefesh* fastened to the body** The holy actions of the body are inspired by the *nefesh*.

[^234]: **there is throne upon throne…** The body constitutes a throne for *nefesh, nefesh* for *ruaḥ*, and *ruaḥ* for *neshamah*.

[^235]: **When you contemplate the rungs…** Through contemplating the levels of soul, you discover the mystery of the divine.

[^236]: **candle** בוצינא (*Botsina*), “Lamp,” employed by *Targum Onqelos* (e.g., [[Torah 20 - Tetzaveh#^27-20|Exodus 27:20]]; [[Torah 20 - Tetzaveh#^30-7|30:7–8]]) to render the Hebrew word נר (*ner*), “lamp,” and later, “candle.”

    Wax candles are discussed in a thirteenth-century treatise commissioned by Alfonso X of Castile (1252–1284); see *Libro del saber de astrologia* (University of Madrid MS. 156), fol. 194r. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 85:4; *Tosafot, Shabbat* 20b, s.v. *ad kan; Zohar* 1:50b–51a.

[^237]: **The lower light, which is black, cleaves to the wick…** This dark light cleaving to the wick symbolizes *nefesh*, which cleaves to the body. See the discussion of the colors of the flame in *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-50b|1:50b]]–51b, [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|77b]].

[^238]: **white light…** Symbolizing *ruaḥ*, which provides greater illumination.

[^239]: **concealed light—invisible, unknowable** … The halo around the white flame symbolizes *neshamah*, the highest level of soul.

[^240]: **Similarly, the supernal mystery** The light of the candle symbolizes not only the various levels of soul but also their corresponding *sefirot*, namely, *Shekhinah* (black light), *Tif’eret* (white light), and *Binah* (invisible light).

[^241]: ***to YHVH who had appeared to him*** The verse concludes: *He built an altar there to YHVH who had appeared to him*. See above, page 18.

[^242]: **for that rung** For *Shekhinah*, source of *nefesh*.

[^243]: ***continually journeying*** The verse reads: *Abram journeyed, continually journeying toward the Negev*. See above, pages 18–19, 29.

[^244]: **receiving *ruaḥ*** Issuing from a higher *sefirah: Tif’eret*.

[^245]: ***to YHVH*—uncharacterized, referring to *neshamah*…** In this verse, unlike the preceding one, the name *YHVH* is not “characterized” by the phrase *who had appeared to him*. Here it implies the concealed *sefirah, Binah*, who is the source of *neshamah*. See above, page 18.

[^246]: **to be refined…** Through withstanding the power and temptation of the demonic forces symbolized by Egypt, Abraham was refined. See above, note 205.

[^247]: **those dazzling demons** See above, note 213.

[^248]: **returning to his domain *…to the Negev*** To *Ḥesed*, the *sefirah* of Abraham. See above, pages 18–19, 28.

[^249]: **right hand of the world** *Ḥesed* represents the right hand of the sefirotic body.

[^250]: **East** Symbolizing *Tif’eret*.

[^251]: **West** Symbolizing *Shekhinah*.

[^252]: ***with silver*—on the side of the South** Silver symbolizes the light and warmth of *Ḥesed*, identified with the South.

[^253]: ***with gold*—on the side of the North** Gold symbolizes *Gevurah*, identified with the North. See [[Job 37#^37-22|Job 37:22]]: *From the north gold emerges*.

[^254]: **kissed his hands** A frequent sign of veneration and thanks among the Companions of the *Zohar*.

    See 1:133a, 142a, 250b; 2:21b, 23b, 62a, 68a, 87a, 121b, 193b; 3:31a, 57b, 65b, 73b, 119b, 121a, 221b, 296b (*IZ*); *Bereshit Rabbah* 74:2; BT [[Berakhot 8#8b|*Berakhot* 8b]]; Bacher, “Le baisement des mains dans le Zohar.”

[^255]: ***He went on his journeys*** The verse continues: *from the Negev as far as Bethel, to the place where his tent had been at the beginning between Bethel and Ai*.

[^256]: **spelled למסעו (*le-massa’o*), *on his journey*** Actually, the Masoretic text reads: למסעיו (*le-massa’av*), *on his journeys*, in the plural. In *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-49b|1:49b]], Rabbi Shim’on highlights the plural form, pointing out that one would expect the singular.
