    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-58b|1:58b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-84a|84a]], [[Zohar 10.07 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-122b|122b]] (*MhN*); [[Zohar 10.10 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-5b|2:5b]] (*MhN*), [[Zohar 12.06 - Piqqudin#^z2-41b|41b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-176b|176b]] (*SdTs*); [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-4b|3:4b]], [[Zohar 7.05 - Parashat Shemini#^z3-40b|40b]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-68a|68a]], [[Zohar 8.10 - Commentary on Naso, continued#^z3-148a|148a]], [[Zohar 9.07 - Parashat Huqqat#^z3-184a|184a]], [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-202b|202b]], [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-203b|203b]], [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-207a|207a]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-254a|254a]] (RM); *Minḥat Shai*, ad loc., and on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^13-7|Genesis 13:7]]; [[Torah 35 - Nasso#^7-1|Numbers 7:1]]; [[Torah 39 - Chukat#^21-34|21:34]]; [[Torah 40 - Balak#^23-9|23:9]].

[^257]: **first rung…** *Shekhinah*, the first rung one grasps on the spiritual ascent. See above, pages 16–18.

[^258]: ***stone dressed at the quarry*** The verse, describing the construction of the Temple, reads: *The House, in its being built, was built of stone dressed at the quarry, so no hammer, ax, or any iron tool was heard*… ([[I Kings 06#^6-7|1 Kings 6:7]]). מסע (*Massa*) means both “journey” and “quarry.”

[^259]: **שלמה‎ אבן (*even shelomoh*), *stone of Solomon*, indeed!** The word שלמה (*shelemah*), *dressed*, is spelled without a י (*yod*) and can be read: *shelomoh*, *Solomon*. In his triumphant reign, *Shekhinah* (symbolized by the stone) was complete, and She inspired the construction of the Temple.

    According to the Midrash, just as the moon does not become full until the fifteenth day of its cycle, so it remained incomplete until the glorious reign of Solomon in the fifteenth generation from Abraham. See *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 5:12; and *Shemot Rabbah* 15:26: “When Solomon appeared, the disk of the moon became full.”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-73b|1:73b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-74a|74a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-150a|150a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-223a|223a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-225b|225b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-249b|249b]]; [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-85a|2:85a]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-61a|3:61a]]; *[[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-83b|ZḤ* 83b]] (*MhN, Rut*); Moses de León, *Shushan Edut*, 342. In Kabbalah the moon symbolizes *Shekhinah*.

[^260]: ***Massa*, as has been said** *Shekhinah*, who journeys along with Israel, is called by this name. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-74a|1:74a]].

[^261]: ***his journeys*—all those rungs…** The plural form also pertains.

[^262]: **arraying his site, joining them in consummate union…** Abraham’s journey *from the Negev as far as Bethel* consummates the union of his sefirotic site, *Ḥesed* (symbolized by *the Negev*), with *Shekhinah*. She is called אל‎ בית (*Bet El*), *Bethel*, “House of God,” since She receives and houses the flow of blessing from *Ḥesed*, known as *El* (“God”). The union of these two *sefirot* manifests the divine spectrum.

[^263]: **אהלה (*oholoh*), *his tent*—spelled with a ה (*he*)…** Normally the masculine pronominal suffix would be the letter ו (*vav*). To Rabbi Shim’on the ה (*he*), a feminine marker, implies *her tent*, namely, the tent of the divine feminine, *Shekhinah*, who is also known as *Bethel* and *stone of Solomon*. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 39:15; *Minḥat Shai* on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-8|Genesis 12:8]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-73a|1:73a]]; above, notes 130, 203.

[^264]: ***to YHVH who had appeared to him*** The verse concludes: *He built an altar there to YHVH who had appeared to him*. Rabbi Shim’on understands this as alluding to the manifestation of *Shekhinah*. See above, pages 18, 32.

[^265]: ***the name of YHVH*** *Shekhinah*, who reveals the divine qualities.

[^266]: **perfect faith** The sefirotic realm culminating in *Shekhinah*.

[^267]: **first rung, as we have said…** *Shekhinah*. See above, page 34.

[^268]: **the South, his allotted share** *Ḥesed*, the *sefirah* of Abraham.

[^269]: **when he ascended** When Abraham ascended further, beyond *Ḥesed*.

[^270]: ***to YHVH*, uncharacterized—supernal world** In this verse, unlike the preceding one, the name *YHVH* is not “characterized” by the phrase *who had appeared to him*. This uncharacterizable dimension of God is the concealed *sefirah* of *Binah*. See above, page 33.

[^271]: ***He went on his journeys from the Negev*** The verse continues: *as far as Bethel*.

[^272]: **from the right side, beginning of the supernal world** From *Ḥesed*, on the right, emanating from the supernal world of *Binah*. To one ascending, *Ḥesed* represents the beginning of that concealed realm. Alternatively, the phrase refers to *Ḥokhmah*, situated directly above *Ḥesed*.

[^273]: **Infinity** סוף‎ אין (*Ein sof*), “There is no end.” *Ein Sof* is boundless divinity, the ultimate reality of God beyond all the specific qualities of the individual *sefirot*. Abraham’s spiritual journey led him here.

[^274]: **from above to below** Having reached the ultimate, Abraham retraced his steps, descending the sefirotic ladder to *Shekhinah*.

[^275]: **to its site fittingly** To *Shekhinah*, the divine name.

[^276]: **Her** *Shekhinah*, symbolized by the altar.

[^277]: **becoming the actual share of the blessed Holy One** Selected by God and confirmed as the incarnation of *Ḥesed*. See [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-9|Deuteronomy 32:9]]; *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 312; above, page 29.

[^278]: **Happy are they… *Your people, all of them righteous*…** This verse, appearing frequently in the *Zohar*, reads in full: *Your people, all of them righteous, will inherit the land forever—sprout of My planting, work of My hands, that I may be glorified*. Here the concluding phrase indicates that God is crowned with the righteous. In M *Sanhedrin* 10:1, the verse is cited to demonstrate that “all of Israel have a share in the world that is coming.”

[^279]: ***Turn to me, be gracious to me*…** The verse continues: *grant Your power to Your servant and save the son of Your maidservant*.

[^280]: **we have established it in various places** See *Midrash Tehillim* 86:8; *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-47a|3:47a]], [[Zohar 8.03 - Parashat Qedoshim#^z3-84a|84a]].

[^281]: **How could David say: *Turn to me, be gracious to me?*** How could he command God so impudently? See *Zohar* [[Zohar 8.03 - Parashat Qedoshim#^z3-84a|3:84a]].

[^282]: **rung with which he was crowned** *Shekhinah*, known as *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”). David, the ideal king, had grasped this rung and now asked the masculine divinity to turn toward Her. Through Her, David would be delivered.

[^283]: ***Grant Your power*—supernal power** The *sefirah* of *Gevurah* (“Power”).

[^284]: **The anonymous king, King Messiah** Identified with *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”). The verse in Samuel reads: *He will grant power to His king and exalt the horn of His* משיח (*mashiaḥ*), *anointed. Malkhut*, last of the ten *sefirot*, is annointed by the flow of emanation.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15b|1:15b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29a|29a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-238a|238a]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-127b|2:127b]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-19b|3:19b]].

[^285]: ***to Your servant*—King Messiah…** Again referring to *Malkhut*.

[^286]: **Now, wasn’t he the son of Jesse?** Why did David call himself after his mother (*Your maidservant*) rather than Jesse, his father?

[^287]: **one should mention that of which he is certain…** The mother’s identity is more certain than the father’s. See BT [[Shabbat 66#66b|*Shabbat* 66b]]: “Abbaye said, ‘Mother told me, “All incantations that are repeated several times must contain the name of the patient’s mother.”’”

    Cf. [[Nazir 49#49a|*Nazir* 49a]]; [[Gittin 69#69a|*Gittin* 69a]]–b; *Zohar* [[Zohar 10.10 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-17b|2:17b]] (*MhN*); *Yalqut ha-Makhiri*, [[Psalms 118#^118-22|Psalms 118:22]], par. 28; Trachtenberg, *Jewish Magic and Superstition*, 115–16,139–40.

[^288]: **we have learned that this is the king…** David was praying for the sake of *Malkhut*, known as “king” and also *Your maidservant*, so he called himself *son of Your maidservant*.

[^289]: **ריב (*riv*), *a quarrel*…spelled רב (*rv*), missing the י (*yod*)** In fact, the Masoretic text includes the י (*yod*). See *Minḥat Shai*, ad loc.; above, note 256.

[^290]: **for Lot sought to revert to the idolatry …** The letter י (*yod*) begins and symbolizes the divine name יהוה (*YHVH*). Here its purported lack indicates a lack of divinity, since Lot reverted to the idolatry practiced by the natives of Canaan. Further, the spelling רב (*rv*) implies *rav* (“master”), namely, the Master of the world, over whose identity Abraham and Lot quarreled.

[^291]: **מקדם (*mi-qedem*), *from the east*** Here meaning “eastward.” The phrase recalls *from east of Bethel* ([[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-8|Genesis 12:8]]; cf. 13:3), where Abraham was then encamped.

[^292]: **מקדמונו (*Mi-qadmono*) From the Primordial One, of the world** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 40(41):16 (on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^13-11|Genesis 13:11]]): “*Lot journeyed* מקדם (*mi-qedem*), *from the east*. He removed himself מקדמונו (*mi-qadmono*), from the Primordial One, of the world, saying, ‘I desire neither Abraham nor his God.’”

[^293]: **and there is written: *When they journeyed mi-qedem, from the east*** In the story of the Tower of Babel. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 38:7: “*When they journeyed* מקדם (*mi-qedem*), *from the east*…. Rabbi El’azar son of Rabbi Shim’on said, ‘They removed themselves מקדמונו (*mi-qadmono*), from the Primordial One, of the world, saying, “We desire neither Him nor His divinity.”’” See above, *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-74b|1:74b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-76a|76a]].

[^294]: **whoever associates with a sinner eventually imitates him…** See *Mekhilta, Amaleq (Yitro)* 1: “One should not associate with a wicked person, even to draw him near to Torah.” Cf. M *Avot* 1:7; *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 9, 30. However, the opposite view appears frequently—for example, above, page 14.

[^295]: **Jehoshaphat, who joined with Ahab** King Jehoshaphat of Judah allied himself with the wicked King Ahab of Israel by marrying his son Jehoram to Ahab’s daughter ([[II Kings 08#^8-18|2 Kings 8:18]]). The two kings then fought together against Aram. See *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 9, citing [[II Chronicles 19#^19-2|2 Chronicles 19:2]].

[^296]: **the merit of his ancestors** Jehoshaphat was descended from David.

[^297]: ***Jehoshaphat cried out*…** In the battle against Aram. The verse reads: *When the chariot officers saw Jehoshaphat…, they wheeled around to attack him, and Jehoshaphat cried out and YHVH helped him; God lured them away from him*.

[^298]: **realm of faith** *Shekhinah*, symbolized by the Holy Land.

[^299]: ***YHVH said to Abram, after Lot had parted from him*** The verse continues: *“Raise your eyes now and look from the place where you are, to the north, to the south, to the east, to the west. For all the land that you see I will give to you and to your seed forever.”*

[^300]: ***My dove*—Assembly of Israel** See *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 2:14. In rabbinic Hebrew the phrase ישראל‎ כנסת (*Keneset Yisra’el*), “Assembly of Israel,” denotes the people of Israel. The midrash on the Song of Songs describes the love affair between the maiden (the earthly community of Israel) and her lover (the Holy One, blessed be He). In the *Zohar, Keneset Yisra’el* can refer to the earthly community but also (often primarily) to *Shekhinah*, the divine feminine counterpart of the people, the aspect of God most intimately connected with them. The lovers in the Song of Songs are pictured as the divine couple, *Tif’eret* and *Shekhinah*.

[^301]: ***In the clefts of the rock*—Jerusalem** … See *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 2:14: “Rabbi Tanḥuma said, ‘… I interpret according to the view of the Rabbis:… *My dove, in the clefts of the rock*—for they were hidden in the secrecy of the House of Eternity \[the Temple\].’”

[^302]: **Holy of Holies, heart of the whole world** See *Tanḥuma, Qedoshim* 10: “The land of Israel sits in the center of the world, Jerusalem in the center of the land of Israel, the Temple in the center of Jerusalem, the nave in the center of the Temple, the ark in the center of the nave, and in front of the ark the Rock of Foundation, from which the world was founded.”

    See Ibn Ezra on [[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^1-2|Genesis 1:2]]; Naḥmanides on [[Torah 29 - Achrei Mot#^18-25|Leviticus 18:25]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-157a|2:157a]], [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-184b|184b]], [[Zohar 6.04 - Parashat Ki Tissa#^z2-193a|193a]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-65b|3:65b]], [[Zohar 9.03 - Parashat Shelah Lekha#^z3-161b|161b]]. Cf. *Eikhah Rabbah, Petiḥta* 16.

[^303]: **there *Shekhinah* concealed Herself…** *Shekhinah* concealed Herself within the Holy of Holies for Her husband, *Tif’eret*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-116a|1:116a]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-170b|2:170b–171a]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Rimmon*, 359.

[^304]: **in the secrecy of the steep** A paraphrase of *in the covert of the cliff*, referring to the Holy of Holies.

[^305]: **when She is in exile** *Shekhinah* accompanies Israel in exile. See BT [[Megillah 29#29a|*Megillah* 29a]]: “Rabbi Shim’on son of Yoḥai says, ‘Come and see how beloved are Israel in the sight of the blessed Holy One! Wherever they went in exile, *Shekhinah* accompanied them. When they were exiled to Egypt, *Shekhinah* was with them… When they were exiled to Babylon, *Shekhinah* was with them…. And even when they are destined to be redeemed, *Shekhinah* will be with them.’” See *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 14.

[^306]: **throne…** Symbolizing *Shekhinah*.

[^307]: **They performed the rite, piercing the atmospheric belts of the world…** When the Temple stood, Israel performed sacrificial rites. Their pure intention ascended through all the levels of Heaven, stimulating the union of *Tif’eret* and *Shekhinah* (who is symbolized by “the land”). Ultimately all the *sefirot* joined, ascending together to *Ein Sof*.

    Maimonides had relativized the importance of the sacrificial offerings, explaining them away as a concession to the primitive nature of ancient Israel. The *Zohar* counters by emphasizing their vital role in unifying and sustaining the cosmos. See Maimonides, *Guide of the Perplexed* 3:32; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:878–90.

    On cultivating the land as a metaphor for offering sacrifices and observing the commandments, see *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 41; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^2-15|Genesis 2:15]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 16:5; *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* B, 21; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 12; *Battei Midrashot*, 2:91; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.027a|1:27a]] (*TZ*), 57b, 122a, 141b, 199b; 2:165b.

[^308]: **being nourished only by drippings** See BT *Ta’anit* 10a: “The land of Israel drinks water directly from the rain, while the rest of the world drinks of the drippings.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-108b|1:108b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-152b|2:152b]]; [[Zohar 9.10 - Commentary on Balaq, continued#^z3-209b|3:209b]]; Naḥmanides on [[Torah 46 - Eikev#^11-10|Deuteronomy 11:10]].

    Here Rabbi Abba teaches that when Israel dwelled in the land, they imbibed the flow of emanation directly from *Shekhinah*, while the other nations were nourished merely by the residue and were relatively powerless.

[^309]: **before Israel defiled the land** Through sin.

[^310]: **they thrust *Shekhinah* from Her site, as it were** Sin displaces the Divine Presence. See BT [[Chagigah 16#16a|*Ḥagigah* 16a]]: “Rabbi Yitsḥak said, ‘Whoever sins secretly, it is as if he thrusts away the feet of *Shekhinah*.’” Cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-57a|1:57a]]–b, [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-61a|61a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-69a|69a]].

    See [[Isaiah 50#^50-1|Isaiah 50:1]]: *Because of your transgressions your mother was divorced*. Cf. *Bereshit Rabbah* 19:7: “Rabbi Abba son of Kahana said, ‘The essence of *Shekhinah* was in the lower realms. As soon as Adam sinned, it withdrew to the first heaven.’” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-53b|1:53b]].

[^311]: **She was drawn toward another place** Toward the demonic realm, symbolized by foreign lands.

[^312]: **The land of Israel is not ruled by any official…** According to rabbinic tradition, Israel is ruled directly by God, while the other seventy nations of the world are governed by angels or heavenly princes appointed by Him.

    See [[Daniel 10#^10-20|Daniel 10:20]]; Septuagint, [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-8|Deuteronomy 32:8–9]]; Jubilees 15:31–32; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 02 - Noach#^11-8|Genesis 11:8]], [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-8|Deuteronomy 32:8–9]]; *Tanḥuma, Re’eh* 8; *Leqaḥ Tov*, [[Torah 02 - Noach#^9-19|Genesis 9:19]]; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 24; BT [[Ketubot 110#110b|*Ketubbot* 110b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-46b|1:46b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-61a|61a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-108b|108b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-33a|2:33a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-151b|151b]]; [[Zohar 9.16 - Commentary on Ha'azinu, continued#^z3-298b|3:298b]]; Ginzberg, *Legends*, 5:204–5, n. 91.

    See BT *Ta’anit* 10a: “The land of Israel is watered by the blessed Holy One Himself, while the rest of the world is watered by a messenger.”

[^313]: **they attracted alien gods to *Shekhinah*, interlinking them…** By offering incense to alien gods, Israel rejected the true God and ruined the union of the divine couple. This left *Shekhinah* vulnerable to the alien powers, who drew close to Her and imbibed Her nourishment.

[^314]: **incense constitutes a nexible nexus** לאתקטרא‎ הוא‎ קטורא‎ קטרת (*Qetoret qittura hu le-itqattara*), “Incense is a link to be linked.” The *Zohar* often plays on the root קטר (*qtr*), which in Hebrew forms the basis of קטרת (*qetoret*) and in Aramaic means “to tie, link, connect.” Through incense offered to God, the *sefirot* are linked, while incense offered to idols links *Shekhinah* with the demonic.

[^315]: **prophets have ceased** See *Tosefta, Sotah* 13:3: “Once the last \[biblical\] prophets—Haggai, Zechariah, and Malachi—died, the Holy Spirit ceased from Israel.”

    Cf. JT *Sotah* 9:13, 24b; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 8:8.

[^316]: **those supernal rungs** The *sefirot*.

[^317]: **never venturing outside the house to reveal Herself** Never leaving the Temple or the land of Israel.

[^318]: **That is why Jonah fled outside the Holy Land…** See *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 1: “Rabbi El’azar son of Tsadok said, ‘…*Shekhinah* is not revealed outside the land \[of Israel\], for it is said: *Jonah set out to flee to Tarshish from the presence of YHVH* ([[Jonah 1#^1-3|Jonah 1:3]]). Was he fleeing *from the presence of YHVH*? Has it not already been said: *Where can I go from Your spirit?* \[*Where can I flee from Your presence?*\] *If I ascend to heaven, You are there*. \[*If I make my bed in Sheol, here You are*\]…([[Psalms 139#^139-7|Psalms 139:7–8]]). Rather, Jonah said, “I will go outside the land, where *Shekhinah* is not revealed.”’”

    See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 10; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-121a|1:121a]]; 2:170b.

[^319]: ***Happening it happened*** The verse continues: *that the word of YHVH came to Ezekiel the priest, son of Buzi, in the land of the Chaldeans by the River Kevar*. The opening phrase, היה‎ היה (*Hayoh hayah*), *Happening it happened*, combines the infinitive and finite forms of the verb “to be.”

[^320]: **what happened had never happened before…** The unusual phrase, *happening it happened*, implies a unique event. When *Shekhinah* appeared to Ezekiel in Babylon, it was the first time She had appeared outside the land of Israel since the Temple was built, but this was an emergency: the stunned people in exile had to be comforted and reassured.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-149a|1:149a]]; [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-2a|2:2a–b]], [[Zohar 10.10 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-5a|5a]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-82a|82a]]; Moses de León, *Peirush ha-Merkavah*, 58–59. Cf. *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 1; BT *Mo’ed Qatan* 25a.

[^321]: **River of Already…** The prophet Ezekiel experienced his vision at the River Kevar, a stream near Nippur in Babylon. In Hebrew, though, the word כבר (*kevar*) means “already” or “long ago,” so Rabbi Abba sees here an allusion to the primordial river issuing from Eden.

    See *Re’uyyot Yeḥezqel*, ed. Gruenwald, in *Temirin* 1 (1972): 111–14; Matt, *Essential Kabbalah*, 126, 205; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-6b|1:6b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-149a|149a–b]].

    On the relation between prophetic revelation and the purity of water, see *Mekhilta, Pisḥa* 1 (citing [[Daniel 08#^8-2|Daniel 8:2]]; [[Daniel 10#^10-4|10:4]]; [[Ezekiel 01#^1-3|Ezekiel 1:3]]); Idel, in *Sinai* 86 (1979): 1–7.

[^322]: ***A river issues from Eden to water the garden*…** The river of emanation flows from the highest *sefirot* to water the garden of *Shekhinah*.

[^323]: **This is one of them** The River Kevar is identified with the Euphrates, one of the four riverheads issuing from the primordial river of Eden. See [[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^2-14|Genesis 2:14]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 16:3, in the name of Rabbi Yudan.

[^324]: **She does not reveal Herself** *Shekhinah*, the Divine Presence, does not reveal Herself outside the land of Israel.

[^325]: **to revert to his repulsiveness** To worship idolatry. See above, page 37.

[^326]: **Holy Spirit** Often identified with *Shekhinah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-67a|1:67a]]; [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-97b|2:97b]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-238b|238b]]; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-61a|3:61a]].

[^327]: **As soon as Lot parted from him…** See *Tanḥuma, Vayetse* 10: “*Shekhinah* does not dwell in a house containing images of idolatry…. You should realize that as long as Abraham cleaved to Lot, the blessed Holy One was not revealed to him. As soon as he parted from him, He was revealed to him. How do we know? Rabbi El’azar son of Pedat said in the name of Rabbi Yose son of Zimra, ‘As is said: *YHVH said to Abram*. When? *After Lot had parted from him.’”*

[^328]: **As soon as he parted from him** As soon as Lot parted from Abraham.

[^329]: **Where you cleaved at first…** The rung of *Shekhinah*. See above, pages 16–18, 32–34.

[^330]: ***To the north, to the south*…** The four directions symbolize, respectively, *Gevurah, Ḥesed, Tif’eret*, and *Shekhinah*.

[^331]: ***Negev*** The southern region of Canaan, or “south,” symbolizing *Ḥesed*, Abraham’s *sefirah*. See above, pages 18–19, 29.

[^332]: **The first rung that was revealed to him…** *Shekhinah*. See above, note 329.

[^333]: **for this rung comprises all those rungs, all seen within it** All the *sefirot* manifest in *Shekhinah*.

[^334]: **He rose at night to engage in Torah…** On this practice, see above, pages 2–3, 26.

[^335]: **In a village castle…** דקוסטא‎ בקיסטרא (*Be-qistera de-qusta*). *Qistera* derives from the Latin *castrum* (pl. *castra*), “castle, fortress.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29a|1:29a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-30a|30a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62a|62a]] (*Tos*). The word *qusta* means “village” in BT *Ta’anit* 24a; see Rashi, ad loc. To Rabbi El’azar, about to engage in Torah, the simple inn is transformed into a royal dwelling.

[^336]: ***Like an apple tree*—the blessed Holy One…** The midrash on Song of Songs interprets the biblical poem as a love song between God (the blessed Holy One) and the Assembly of Israel. From a kabbalistic perspective, “Assembly of Israel” signifies not only the people Israel but also their divine counterpart, *Shekhinah*, so the song celebrates not only human-divine love but also the love between *Shekhinah* and *Tif’eret*, known as the blessed Holy One. This verse is sung by Her to Him.

    *Tif’eret* is symbolized by the apple because He combines the white of the pulp, the red of the skin, and the green of the leaves, representing the sefirotic triad of *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*.

    See *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 2:3; Ezra of Gerona, *Peirush Shir ha-Shirim*, 489; *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.14 - Parashat Ha'azinu#^z3-286b|3:286b]].

[^337]: **other officials** Angelic powers who rule over the nations of the world. See above, note 312.

[^338]: **From the day Abraham came into the world…** When Abraham appeared in the world, *Shekhinah* was stimulated by his love of God; ever since She has delighted in the blessed Holy One.

[^339]: **Isaac, holy fruit** A divine soul born to Abraham and Sarah.

[^340]: **Jacob** Culmination of the patriarchs, symbolizing *Tif’eret*.

[^341]: **Joseph the Righteous, who generates holy fruit in the world** Joseph symbolizes *Yesod*, who fructifies *Shekhinah*, generating souls.

    Joseph is known as “the Righteous” because he resisted the sexual advances of Potiphar’s wife. See BT [[Yoma 35#35b|*Yoma* 35b]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 93:7; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana, nispaḥim*, 460; cf. *Tanḥuma, Bereshit* 5; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 38, which cite [[Amos 2#^2-6|Amos 2:6]].

    *Yesod* is known as Righteous, based on [[Proverbs 10#^10-25|Proverbs 10:25]]: עולם‎ יסוד‎ וצדיק (*Ve-tsaddiq yesod olam*). The verse, which literally means *The righteous one is an everlasting foundation*, is understood as *The righteous one is the foundation of the world*. See BT [[Chagigah 12#12b|*Ḥagigah* 12b]]; *Bahir* 71 (102); Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 34.

[^342]: ***These are the generations of Jacob: Joseph*…** Rabbi El’azar ends his citation of the verse here, implying that Joseph, symbolizing *Yesod*, embodies all twelve tribes. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 84:6; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-21b|1:21b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-158a|158a]].

[^343]: **So Israel is named after Ephraim…** All twelve tribes of Israel are named after Ephraim, son of Joseph. See *Aggadat Bereshit* 5:3, citing [[Jeremiah 31#^31-8|Jeremiah 31:8]].

[^344]: **Abraham, who resembles an apple emitting fragrance** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 39:2: “Rabbi Berekhiah opened, *‘The scent of your oils is fragrant* ([[Song of Songs 1#^1-3|Song of Songs 1:3]]). What did Abraham resemble? A flask of balsam tightly covered and lying in a corner, so its fragrance did not spread. As soon as it was moved, its fragrance spread. Similarly, the blessed Holy One said to Abraham, “Move yourself from place to place, so your name will be enhanced in the world. *Go you forth* ([[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-1|Genesis 12:1]]).”’”

[^345]: **Because no one else on earth…** In his generation.

[^346]: **But it is written: *and the souls they had made in Haran*!** In this verse, the word נפש (*nefesh*), *souls*, is usually understood as *persons*, and the word עשו (*asu*), *made*, is understood as *acquired*, but Rabbi Ḥizkiyah reads them literally, following midrashic tradition. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 39:14: “*And the souls they had made in Haran*. Rabbi El’azar said in the name of Rabbi Yose son of Zimra, ‘If all the nations assembled to create a single mosquito, they could not cast a soul into it, yet you say: *and the souls they had made*?! Rather, these are converts. Then the verse should read: \[*and the souls*\] *they had converted*. Why *they had made*? To teach you that whoever draws a gentile near is as though he created him.’ Then the verse should read: *he* \[Abraham\] *had made*. Why *they had made*? Rabbi Ḥunya said, ‘Abraham converted the men, and Sarah the women.’”

    Here Rabbi Ḥizkiyah’s point is that Abraham was not the only one who had discovered true faith.

    See *Sifrei*, [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-1|Deuteronomy 32]]; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 1:3; *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 12; *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* A, 12; B, 26; above, page 12.

[^347]: **They had not yet attained the supernal rungs…** They had not climbed the sefirotic ladder as had Abraham. See above, pages 18–19, 28–36.

[^348]: **Later he told him** Rabbi El’azar told Rabbi Ḥizkiyah.

[^349]: **all three of them constituted the patriarchs of the world** The triad of Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob symbolize the sefirotic triad of *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*.

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 47:6: “Resh Lakish said, ‘The patriarchs themselves constitute the \[divine\] Chariot.’” When all three had appeared, harmony reigned and Abraham was fulfilled.

[^350]: **the blessed Holy One … the blessed Holy One … the blessed Holy One** According to this view, all three phrases spoken by *Shekhinah* refer to Her partner, *Tif’eret*, known as the blessed Holy One. For a similar string of identifications with “the blessed Holy One,” see *Vayiqra Rabbah* 30:9. Cf. *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 2:3.

[^351]: ***We will do and we will listen*** With these words Israel demonstrated true faith by committing themselves to fulfill and enact God’s word even before hearing the details. See BT [[Shabbat 88#88a|*Shabbat* 88a]]; *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 2:3.

[^352]: ***And his fruit is sweet to my mouth*—words of Torah…** See *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 2:3; *Shir ha-Shirim Zuta* 2:3.

[^353]: ***His fruit is sweet to my mouth*—souls of the righteous…** According to this interpretation, *Shekhinah* sings of the souls born from Her union with *Tif’eret*.

    On souls as fruit, see *Bahir* 14 (22); Ezra of Gerona, *Peirush Shir ha-Shirim*, 489, 504; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15b|1:15b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-19a|19a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-33a|33a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-59b|59b–60a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82b|82b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-115a|115a–b]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-223b|2:223b]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Mishqal*, 51; idem, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 56 (69). Cf. Ibn Ezra on [[Psalms 001#^1-3|Psalms 1:3]].

[^354]: **All souls…are all one…they all separate…** Human souls exist originally without gender distinction. On their journey toward the world, they become male and female but are still united androgynously until they enter an individual male or female body. See the rabbinic tradition on the original nature of Adam (*Bereshit Rabbah* 8:1): “Rabbi Yirmeyah son of El’azar said, ‘When the blessed Holy One created Adam, He created him androgynous, as is said: *Male and female He created them* ([[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^1-27|Genesis 1:27]]).’ Rabbi Shemu’el son of Naḥman said, ‘When the blessed Holy One created Adam, He created him with two faces. Then He sawed him and gave him two backs, one on this side and one on that.”

    See Plato, *Symposium* 189d–191d; BT [[Berakhot 61#61a|*Berakhot* 61a]]; [[Eruvin 18#18a|*Eruvin* 18a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-13b|1:13b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-47a|47a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91b|91b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-108a|108a]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-55a|2:55a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-246a|246a]] (*Heikh*); [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-43a|3:43a–b]], [[Zohar 9.13 - Parashat Va-Yelekh#^z3-283b|283b]]; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:1355–56; Matt, *Zohar*, 217.

[^355]: **Desire of female for male…desire of male for female…** The desire of *Shekhinah* for *Tif’eret* generates the feminine aspect of the soul, while His desire for Her generates the masculine aspect. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.04 - Parashat Va-Yeshev#^z1-182a|1:182a]], [[Zohar 3.06 - Parashat Va-Yiggash#^z1-209a|209a]].

[^356]: **later the blessed Holy One couples them…** Ideally God arranges marriages between two souls who were originally paired. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 68:4, in the name of Rabbi Yehudah son of Simon: “A matron asked Rabbi Yose, ‘In how many days did the blessed Holy One create His world?’ He replied, ‘In six days.’ ‘Since then what has He been doing?’ He replied, ‘He sits and matches couples: this man for that woman, this woman for that man.’ She said, ‘If that seems difficult, I can do it!’ She went and matched \[her slaves\], giving this woman to that man, and this man to that woman. After a while, those who had been matched starting beating one another. This woman said, ‘I don’t want that man!’ This man said, ‘I don’t want that woman!’ He \[Rabbi Yose\] said to her \[the matron\], ‘If it seems easy to you, it is as difficult for the blessed Holy One as splitting the Red Sea!’”

    See BT [[Sotah 2#2a|*Sotah* 2a]]; and *Mo’ed Qatan* 18b: “Rav Yehudah said in the name of Shemu’el, ‘Every single day a heavenly echo issues, proclaiming: “The daughter of so-and-so for so-and-so!”’” Cf. *Zohar* [[#^z1-89a|1:89a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-90b|90b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91b|91b]]; [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-101a|2:101a]].

[^357]: **Happy is the human who acts virtuously…** Such a person is rewarded by being reunited with his original soul mate. See BT [[Sotah 2#2a|*Sotah* 2a]]: “When Resh Lakish opened the discussion of the straying wife, he said as follows: ‘A man is only coupled with a woman according to his deeds.’”

[^358]: **he becomes a complete, fitting human being** Regaining androgynous oneness. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-7a|3:7a]], [[Zohar 8.03 - Parashat Qedoshim#^z3-81a|81a–b]].

[^359]: ***His fruit* מתוק (*matoq*), *is sweet*…—תקונא (*tiqquna*), a restoration…** *Shekhinah* praises the sweetness of the soul, generated through Her union with *Tif’eret*. When a righteous human being regains his soul mate, their union below stimulates the union of the divine couple, ensuring a flow of blessing to the world.

[^360]: **The blessed Holy One says to Assembly of Israel** *Tif’eret* says to *Shekhinah*. On this name of *Shekhinah*, see above, note 300.

[^361]: ***From Me*, literally, *your fruit appears*** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-115b|1:115b]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-223b|2:223b]].

[^362]: **Afterward, the two of them appear in the world** The male and female souls are embodied separately.

[^363]: ***From Me your fruit appears*** According to this view, the verse is spoken by *Shekhinah*.

[^364]: ***In the days of Amraphel king of Shinar*…** This chapter of Genesis describes a battle between a confederacy of four eastern kings versus five Canaanite vassals who revolted against them. In the course of the action, Lot (Abram’s nephew) is taken captive by the eastern confederacy. When Abram hears the news, he musters an armed force, routs the attackers, and rescues his nephew.

[^365]: ***Who aroused from the East, calling righteousness to follow him?*** The verse continues: *He delivers up nations to him and tramples kings*.

[^366]: **This verse has been established by the Companions** In various rabbinic sources, the verse is applied to Abraham, who was aroused by God from the East (Mesopotamia). See *Bereshit Rabbah* 42(43):3 (on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-15|Genesis 14:15]]); BT [[Bava Batra 15#15a|*Bava Batra* 15a]].

[^367]: **The blessed Holy One fashioned seven expanses above** See BT [[Chagigah 12#12b|*Ḥagigah* 12b]]: “Resh Lakish said, ‘There are seven רקיעים (*reqi’im*), heavens \[or: expanses, firmaments\].’” Here the expanses are the *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* to *Shekhinah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-32b|1:32b]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-56b|2:56b]].

[^368]: **supernal expanse, concealed, above them…** *Binah*, the Divine Mother, source of the seven lower *sefirot*.

[^369]: **susceptible to questioning yet unknown…called מי (*Mi*), Who?** A spiritual seeker may inquire about *Binah*, but such questions do not yield ordinary answers. The identity of the divine is discovered only in a realm beyond words. The mystical name “Who” becomes a focus of meditation, as question turns into quest. See Shim’on Lavi, *KP*, 1:91a: “Concerning everything that cannot be grasped, its question constitutes its answer.”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-1b|1:1b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29b|29b–30a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-45b|45b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-237b|237b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-126b|2:126b–127a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-138a|138a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-139b|139b]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-226a|226a]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-231b|231b]].

[^370]: ***From the womb of* מי (*mi*), *whom, emerged the ice*…** From the womb of *Binah* emerged *Shekhinah*, whose frozen sea thaws through the warmth of *Ḥesed*, flowing love. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29b|1:29b]].

[^371]: **Below is an expanse lower than them all, not shining** *Shekhinah*, who reflects the light of the other *sefirot* but has no light of Her own.

[^372]: **expanse above them joins it** *Binah*, the supernal expanse, joins *Shekhinah*.

[^373]: **These two letters it absorbs and is called ים (*Yam*), Sea…** *Shekhinah* absorbs the letters of *Binah:* מי (*Mi*), “Who,” and reflects them in reverse: ים (*Yam*), “Sea.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-30a|1:30a]].

[^374]: **all those other expanses turn into streams…** The *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* through *Yesod* transmit the flow of emanation from *Binah* to *Shekhinah*.

[^375]: **fruit and fish, according to their species** Various souls and angels. On souls as fruit, see above, note 353. On angels as fish, see *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-30b|2:30b]].

[^376]: ***Who aroused from the East*—Abraham** *Binah*, known as *Who*, emanated the lower *sefirot* beginning with *Ḥesed*, symbolized by Abraham. As indicated above (note 366), midrashic tradition applies this verse to Abraham, who was aroused by God from the East.

[^377]: ***Calling righteousness to follow him*…** The emanation of the *sefirot* culminates in *Shekhinah*, known as *righteousness* and Sea.

[^378]: **Who? This lowest expanse…who wreaks vengeance…** Who delivered up the four kings to Abraham (as related in [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-1|Genesis 14]])? It was *Shekhinah*, the attribute of Judgment.

[^379]: **On this David prided himself…** King David symbolizes *Shekhinah*, known as *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”), and in this verse he celebrates Her power.

[^380]: **nations pursued by Abraham** As related in [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-14|Genesis 14:14–15]]. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 42(43):3 (on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-15|Genesis 14:15]]).

[^381]: **Kings appointed over them above** According to rabbinic tradition, the seventy nations of the world are governed by seventy angels or heavenly princes appointed by God.

    See [[Daniel 10#^10-20|Daniel 10:20]]; Septuagint, [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-8|Deuteronomy 32:8–9]]; Jubilees 15:31–32; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 02 - Noach#^11-8|Genesis 11:8]], [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-8|Deuteronomy 32:8–9]]; *Tanḥuma, Re’eh* 8; *Leqaḥ Tov*, [[Torah 02 - Noach#^9-19|Genesis 9:19]]; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 24; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-46b|1:46b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-108b|108b]]; [[Zohar 9.16 - Commentary on Ha'azinu, continued#^z3-298b|3:298b]]; Ginzberg, *Legends*, 5:204–5, n. 91.

[^382]: **when the blessed Holy One executes judgment against a nation…** See *Shemot Rabbah* 21:5: “Rabbi El’azar son of Pedat said, ‘The blessed Holy One does not overthrow a nation until He first overthrows their prince.” Cf. *Mekhilta, Shirta* 2; and [[Isaiah 24#^24-21|Isaiah 24:21]]: *On that day YHVH will punish the host of heaven in heaven and the kings of earth on earth*.

[^383]: **Abraham pursued them and the blessed Holy One…** See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 1:4: “Rabbi Isaac said, ‘Can anyone pursue those already killed, as is written: *He smote them and pursued them as far as Hobah* ([[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-15|Genesis 14:15]])? Rather this teaches that the blessed Holy One slew and Abraham pursued.’” Cf. Margaliot’s edition and note; *Bereshit Rabbah* 42(43):3 (on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-15|Genesis 14:15]]).

[^384]: **the blessed Holy One, called *Shalom*, Peace** See BT [[Shabbat 10#10b|*Shabbat* 10b]]: “The \[divine\] Name itself is called Peace, as is written: *He called it ‘YHVH is peace’* ([[Judges 06#^6-24|Judges 6:24]]).” In Kabbalah this name suits the blessed Holy One (*Tif’eret*), who harmonizes the polar opposites *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-10b|3:10b]], [[Zohar 9.05 - Commentary on Shelah Lekha, continued#^z3-176a|176a–b]].

[^385]: **Would you imagine that Abraham was traveling in the clouds…?** Obviously he traveled on foot. Why does the verse need to specify *feet*?

[^386]: **Rather, *By way of his feet he did not travel*…** *His* and *he* refer to God, who manifested and traveled Himself, not through angels.

[^387]: ***On that day His feet will stand*…** The verse continues: *on the Mount of Olives*.

[^388]: **When the blessed Holy One aroused the world…** The battle of the kings ([[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-1|Genesis 14]]) was instigated by God to stimulate Abraham to act virtuously.

[^389]: **because Jacob was destined to issue from him…** According to this interpretation, the verse means *Who* (*Binah*) *aroused* Abraham *from* (i.e., because of) *the East* (Jacob, symbolizing *Tif’eret*, identified with *East*).

[^390]: ***Righteousness calling him*…for the blessed Holy One called him…** *Shekhinah*, known as *Righteousness*, designated Jacob from the beginning.

[^391]: ***the people who follow you*** Various witnesses cite instead the similar phrase in [[I Kings 20#^20-10|1 Kings 20:10]].

[^392]: ***Who aroused from the East*, for there light begins to radiate** Apparently playing with the homonyms העיר (*he’ir*), *aroused*, and האיר (*he’ir*), *radiated*. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 42(43):3 (on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-15|Genesis 14:15]]).

[^393]: **potency of the light of the South issues from the East** The potency of *Ḥesed* (symbolized by South) manifests through *Tif’eret* (symbolized by East).

[^394]: ***Who aroused* that light of the South. *From the East*…** Understanding the verse to mean *Who* (*Binah*, the supernal expanse) *aroused* the light of the South (*Ḥesed*, symbolized by Abraham) *from the East* (through *Tif’eret*).

[^395]: ***Righteousness calling him to follow him*—West…** *Shekhinah*, known as *Righteousness* and symbolized by West, calls constantly to Her partner, *Tif’eret*, stimulating Him *to follow* Her, to unite with Her. אלהים (*Elohim*) is one of the names of *Shekhinah*. See *Zohar* [[#^z1-77b|1:77b]]; [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-256b|2:256b]] (*Heikh*).

[^396]: **from Him He receives power…** From *Tif’eret, Shekhinah* receives power.

[^397]: **Abraham, who absorbed arousal…solely from the East…** For various versions of this legend, see Apocalypse of Abraham, 7; *Ma’aseh Avraham* (*Beit ha-Midrash*, 2:118); *Sefer ha-Yashar, Noaḥ*, 68; *Midrash ha-Gadol*, [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-1|Genesis 12:1]]; Ginzberg, *Legends*, 5:210, n. 16.

[^398]: **Do not read: בהבראם (*be-hibbare’am*), *when they were created*…** According to Rabbi Yehoshu’a son of Korḥah (*Bereshit Rabbah* 12:9), בהבראם (*be-hibbare’am*), *when they were created*, is an anagram of באברהם (*be-Avraham*), “through Abraham,” indicating that the world was created for his sake. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-3b|1:3b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91b|91b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-93a|93a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-105b|105b]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-128b|128b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-154b|154b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230b|230b]]; [[Zohar 8.07 - Parashat Be-Midbar#^z3-117a|3:117a]].

    See BT [[Chagigah 12#12b|*Ḥagigah* 12b]]: “Rabbi El’azar son of Shamu’a says, ‘\[The world stands\] upon a single pillar named Righteous One, as is said: *The righteous one is the foundation of the world* ([[Proverbs 10#^10-25|Proverbs 10:25]]).’”

[^399]: **descendants who will destroy the Temple…** In rabbinic literature Rome is referred to as Esau, Abraham’s grandson.

[^400]: **Immediately the world stabilized for his sake** Jacob’s virtuous descendants are contrasted with Abraham’s son (Ishmael) and Isaac’s son (Esau) in *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 312; *Bereshit Rabbah* 68:11. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-118b|1:118b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-119b|119b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-148a|148a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-154b|154b]].

[^401]: **as is written: *Declaring what is right*** Referring to the “uprightness” of Jacob’s sons.

[^402]: **all diverge in meaning** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-16b|1:16b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-157a|157a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-234b|234b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-249a|249a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-25b|2:25b]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-83a|83a]]; [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-50b|3:50b]], [[Zohar 9.03 - Parashat Shelah Lekha#^z3-161a|161a]], [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-132b|132b–133a]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-293a|293a]] (*IZ*).

[^403]: ***Vaydabber, He spoke*… an outer rung… *righteousness*** This verb alludes to *Shekhinah*, known as *righteousness*. As the last *sefirah*, She is symbolized by fully articulated divine speech.

[^404]: ***Va-yagged, He declared*… controlling speech…** *Tif’eret*, who precedes and controls *Shekhinah*.

[^405]: **Supernal rung inhabited by Jacob…*You established what is right*** Jacob attained the rung of *Tif’eret*, who constitutes the mediating principle (*what is right*) between the polar opposites *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-51b|2:51b]].

[^406]: **But look…ויגד (*Va-yagged*), *He declared, to you His covenant*** In this verse, the verb alludes to *Yesod*, the divine phallus symbolized by the covenant of circumcision. The noun גיד (*gid*), “sinew,” serves as a rabbinic euphemism for “phallus.” See M *Yevamot* 8:2.

[^407]: **That rung controls the lower one: *speaking righteousness*** *Yesod* also controls *Shekhinah*, known as divine speech and *righteousness*.

[^408]: **דבר (*davar*), word** *Shekhinah*, divine speech.

[^409]: **filled with everything, a supernal rung** *Shekhinah* contains the entire flow of emanation from the higher *sefirot*.

[^410]: **Your mnemonic: *It is not an empty word for you*** *Shekhinah*, the divine word, is full. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 1:14, in the name of Rabbi Yishma’el: “*It is not an empty word* מכם (*mi-kem*), *for you*, and if it is empty, it is *mi-kem*, from you—because of you, because you do not know how to interpet.” See *Midrash Tanna’im*, [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-47|Deuteronomy 32:47]]; JT *Pe’ah* 1:1, 15b.

[^411]: **his father-in-law** See Vol. 1, p. 30, n. 205.

[^412]: **arousal above depends on desire below** A general principle of Kabbalah. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35a|1:35a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82b|82b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-88a|88a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-164a|164a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-244a|244a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-31b|2:31b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-265a|265a]]; [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-92a|3:92a]], [[Zohar 8.05 - Parashat Be-Har#^z3-110b|110b]].

[^413]: **אלהים (*Elohim*), *O God, do not be silent*…This is arousal below** *Shekhinah*, known by the divine name *Elohim*, stimulates the higher *sefirot*.

[^414]: **David exclaimed** King David addresses his corresponding *sefirah: Shekhinah*, known as *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”).

[^415]: **Right** *Ḥesed*, on the right side of the sefirotic tree.

[^416]: **When all those kings joined forces to wage war against Abraham** See [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-1|Genesis 14]].

[^417]: **he resembled Abraham** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 40(41):6 (on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^13-8|Genesis 13:8]]).

[^418]: **that entire war was fought over him…** The idolatrous kings sought to defeat Abraham. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 41(42):3, 7 (on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-1|Genesis 14:1]], 7); *Midrash ha-Gadol*, [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-7|Genesis 14:7]]; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 27.

[^419]: **to enhance Abraham’s name on earth and draw him to His service** Abraham’s miraculous victory over the kings made him famous and drew him closer to God.

[^420]: ***Elohim, do not be silent!*—until all linked with Abraham** *Shekhinah*, known as *Elohim*, began to arouse the higher *sefirot* until She was fully joined with *Ḥesed*, symbolized by Abraham.

[^421]: ***Melchizedek, king of Salem*** The verse continues: *brought out bread and wine; he was priest of God Most High*.

[^422]: **Rabbi Shim’on opened** As *KP* indicates, this passage (86b–87a) interrupts the story of the journey of Rabbi El’azar. The Sulzbach edition of the *Zohar* places Rabbi Shim’on’s teaching at the conclusion of the story.

[^423]: **lamp of impenetrable darkness** דקרדינותא‎ בוצינא (*Botsina de-qardinuta*). See Vol. 1, pp. 107–8, and n. 4 there; and the wealth of material collected and analyzed by Liebes, *Peraqim*, 145–51, 161–64. קרדינותא (*Qardinuta*) recalls a phrase in BT [[Pesachim 7#7a|*Pesaḥim* 7a]]: קורדניתא‎ חיטי (*ḥittei qurdanaita*), “wheat from Kurdistan,” which, according to Rashi, is very hard. In *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15a|1:15a]], several witnesses record the variants קדרינותא (*qadrinuta*) or קדרוניתא (*qadrunita*), “darkness.” See *[[Zohar 11.07 - Matnitin#^zh-2a|ZḤ* 2a]], where דסיהרא‎ קרדנותא (*qardenuta de-sihara*) means “eclipse of the moon,” corresponding to the Hebrew הירח‎ קדרות (*qadrut ha-yareaḥ*), “darkening of the moon.”

    The lamp (or “spark”) is so potently brilliant that it overwhelms comprehension. Many mystics convey similar paradoxical images: “a ray of divine darkness” (Dionysius, *Mystical Theology* 1:1); “the luminous darkness” (Gregory of Nyssa, *Life of Moses* 2:163); “the black light” (Iranian Sufism; see Corbin, *The Man of Light in Iranian Sufism*, 99–120). Prior to the *Zohar*, Azriel of Gerona and the author of *Ma’yan ha-Ḥokhmah* mention “the light darkened from shining.” See Verman, *The Books of Contemplation*, 59–60, 158–59; Scholem, *Origins of the Kabbalah*, 336. Cf. Maimonides, *Guide of the Perplexed* 1:59: “We are dazzled by His beauty, and He is hidden from us because of the intensity with which He becomes manifest, just as the sun is hidden to eyes too weak to apprehend it.”

    Here the blinding light is the impulse of emanation flashing from *Ein Sof* and proceeding to delineate the various *sefirot*. See *ZḤ* [[Zohar 12.09 - Qav ha-Middah#^zh-57d|57d–58a]] (*QhM*); *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15a|1:15a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-18b|18b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-172a|172a]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-133b|2:133b]], [[Zohar 5.06 - Sifra di-Tsni'uta#^z2-177a|177a]], [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-233a|233a]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-254b|254b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-260a|260a]]; [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-48b|3:48b–49a]], [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-135b|135b]], [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-139a|139a]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-292b|292b]], [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-295a|295a–b]] (*IZ*).

[^424]: **He generated a single drop and joined them as one…** The drop, symbolizing the flow of *Ḥesed*, is joined with the flame, symbolizing the fire of *Gevurah*. By balancing the qualities of love and rigor, God creates the world. See *Bereshit Rabbah* 12:15, and 4:7: “*God called the expanse* שמים (*shamayim*), *Heaven* ([[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^1-8|Genesis 1:8]]). Rav said, ‘ומים‎ אש (*Esh u-mayim*), Fire and water.’ Rabbi Abba son of Kahana said in the name of Rav, ‘The blessed Holy One took fire and water and intermingled them, and from them heaven was made.’”

[^425]: **Between them issued שלים‎ רוח (*ruaḥ shelim*), perfect spirit…** *Tif’eret*, symbolized by *ruaḥ* (“spirit” or “air”), harmonizes the polar opposites *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*, symbolized by fire and water. See above, page 2.

[^426]: **the rung stabilized** *Tif’eret*, the central *sefirah*, stabilized—and with it, the entire sefirotic ladder.

[^427]: **ה (*He*) was crowned with ו (*vav*), ו (*vav*) with ה (*he*)…** The last three letters of the divine name יהוה (*YHVH*) symbolize *Binah, Tif’eret*, and *Shekhinah*. Here *Binah* is crowned with *Tif’eret*, and *Tif’eret* with *Shekhinah* (or alternatively, *Shekhinah* is crowned with *Tif’eret*, and *Tif’eret* with *Binah*). Then *Shekhinah* ascends toward *Binah*.

[^428]: ***Melchizedek*, שלם‎ מלך (*melekh shalem*), *king of Salem*…** *Melchizedek* symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who is known as *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”) and *Tsedeq* (“Righteousness”). Now She is *shalem* (“perfect, complete”), united with the higher *sefirot*, empowered by them and reigning over the world. Similarly, the *tabernacle* referred to by Rabbi Shim’on (above, page 54) symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who is now *in Salem*, in a state of perfection.

[^429]: **On Yom Kippur, when all faces beam** On this holiest of days, the *sefirot* unite in joy. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-69a|3:69a]]–b.

[^430]: **last world** *Shekhinah*, last of the ten *sefirot*.

[^431]: **supernal world** *Binah*, the Divine Mother.

[^432]: **Even the lower world constitutes all…** Although *Shekhinah* is the last of the ten *sefirot*, She includes them all.

[^433]: ***Brought out bread and wine*** The verse reads: *Melchizedek, king of Salem, brought out bread and wine; he was priest of God Most High*.

[^434]: **She contains both of these** *Shekhinah* includes both forms of emanation, the second more subtle and refined.

[^435]: **world serving world** *Shekhinah* is the priest who serves *Binah, God Most High*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-152a|2:152a]].

[^436]: ***He was priest*—the Right…** According to this interpretation, *priest* symbolizes *Ḥesed*, on the right, who derives from *Binah, God Most High*.

[^437]: **So the priest must bless the world** As *Ḥesed* conveys blessing from *Binah* to the worlds below, so the human priest conveys blessing to the world.

[^438]: **blessings absorbed by this lower world…High Priest** The blessings absorbed by *Shekhinah* when She is joined with *Ḥesed*.

[^439]: ***he blessed him, saying, ‘Blessed be Abram of God Most High*** The verse continues: *Possessor of Heaven and Earth!’* Rabbi Shim’on apparently takes the verse to mean that *Binah* blesses *Ḥesed*, symbolized by Abram, who conveys the flow of blessing to *Shekhinah*. For other interpretations, see *KP* and Galante.

[^440]: **priest below must cluster clusters…** The human priest must stimulate the union of the *sefirot*, linking *Shekhinah* with *Ḥesed* on the right, so that She joins with *Binah*, and so that this world joins the upper world.

[^441]: **Mystery of the word: instituting blessings** The wording of the verse in Genesis prefigures the traditional formula of a blessing: “Blessed are You, *YHVH* our God, King of the world…”

[^442]: ***He blessed him*, from below** *Melchizedek*, symbolizing *Shekhinah*, blessed Abraham, symbolizing the higher *sefirah: Ḥesed*.

[^443]: ***Blessed be God Most High*…from above to below** Here the blessing flows from *Binah, God Most High*, down toward *Shekhinah* and the lower worlds.

[^444]: ***tenth*…the site where a cluster clusters below** *Shekhinah*, last of the ten *sefirot* and the site where they all join. According to the plain meaning of Genesis, the subject is Abraham, who gives a tenth to Melchizedek, but here it is unclear exactly how Rabbi Shim’on interprets the pronouns *he* and *him*. See *KP;* Galante; *MM;* below, page 60; *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.05 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^zh-25a|25a]] (*MhN*); Kasher, *Torah Shelemah*, [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-20|Genesis 14:20]], n. 133.

[^445]: **Why is it not written: *A psalm of David*, or *Of David, a psalm*?** Why the unusual, abbreviated title: *Of David*?

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-123b|1:123b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-239a|239a]]. On the distinction between the two complete titles, see BT [[Pesachim 117#117a|*Pesaḥim* 117a]]; *Midrash Tehillim* 24:1; Rashi on [[Psalms 023#^23-1|Psalms 23:1]]; Jacob bar Sheshet, *Meshiv Devarim Nekhoḥim*, 92; Todros Abulafia, *Sha’ar ha-Razim*, 48; *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.04 - Heikhalot Be-Reshit#^z1-39b|1:39b]], 67a; 2:50a, 140a, 170a.

[^446]: **on behalf of his rung…** In this psalm, King David sang on behalf of *Shekhinah*, whom he symbolizes. She is known as *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”). See below, page 84.

    In thirteenth-century Castile, King Alfonso X (1252–1284) also sang to the idealized feminine, imagining himself as troubadour of the Virgin Mary. In his *Cantigas de Santa María* (1:102, 19–26) he chants: “I wish today to be her Troubadour, and I beg that she will desire me for her Troubadour and will want to receive my song.”

[^447]: ***To You, YHVH*—above** *Tif’eret*, known by this divine name.

[^448]: **Who is *my soul*? David, first rung…** *Shekhinah*, first rung on the ascending sefirotic ladder.

[^449]: ***I lift up*—I raise, as is said…** David sought to raise *Shekhinah* and unite Her with the triad of *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*, symbolized by *the mountains*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-110a|2:110a]].

[^450]: ***Bless* יהוה‎ את (*et YHVH*), *O my soul*…** Grammatically, the accusative particle את (*et*) has no ascertainable independent sense, but Naḥum of Gimzo and his disciple Rabbi Akiva taught that when *et* appears in a biblical verse, it amplifies the original meaning. See BT [[Pesachim 22#22b|*Pesaḥim* 22b]]; [[Chagigah 12#12a|*Ḥagigah* 12a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-247a|1:247a]]; [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-90a|2:90a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-135b|135b]].

    In the *Zohar* the word את (*et*) symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who comprises the entire alphabet of divine speech from א (*alef*) to ת (*tav*). See the Christian parallel in [[Revelation 01#^1-8|Revelation 1:8]]: “I am *alpha* and *omega*.” Here the phrase יהוה‎ את (*et YHVH*) indicates the union of *Shekhinah* and Her partner, *Tif’eret*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-18a|1:18a]]; [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-81b|2:81b]].

[^451]: **Other creatures of the field** Angelic forces of *Shekhinah*, who is known as “field” or “Holy Apple Orchard.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-29b|1:29b]], and on the image of the field, 1:142b; 2:61b; cf. BT *Ta’anit* 29b; *Bereshit Rabbah* 65:22; Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 35–37.

[^452]: **as is said: *My innards stirred for him*** The kabbalistic reading of Song of Songs interprets this verse as an expression of the passion of *Shekhinah* for *Tif’eret*. Her *inmost being*, the angels, participate in Her yearning.

[^453]: **speaking on his own behalf** David called upon his own soul to bless the divine union.

[^454]: ***Et YHVH*—consummation of all…** The union of *Shekhinah* and *Tif’eret*.

[^455]: **I see that you have arrived united with *Shekhinah*!** See M *Avot* 3:2: “Rabbi Ḥananya son of Teradyon said, ‘…If two are sitting engaged in words of Torah, *Shekhinah* dwells between them.’”

    On the importance of engaging in Torah while on a journey, see [[Torah 45 - Vaetchanan#^6-7|Deuteronomy 6:7]]; BT [[Eruvin 54#54a|*Eruvin* 54a]]; *Ta’anit* 10b; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-7a|1:7a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-58b|58b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-69b|69b–70a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-76a|76a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-115b|115b]].

[^456]: **I’ve been walking with him for three parasangs** Walking with this companion. According to Rav Sheshet (BT [[Sotah 46#46b|*Sotah* 46b]]), one should escort his teacher a distance of a parasang. A distinguished teacher, however, is to be escorted for three parasangs. (The Greek parasang equals about 3.5 miles.)

    See *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 18:5; *Bereshit Rabbah* 32:10; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-51a|1:51a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96b|96b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-150b|150b]]; [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-14a|2:14a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-164a|164a]], [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-187a|187a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-8b|3:8b]].

[^457]: **radiant lamp** דנהיר‎ בוצינא (*Botsina de-nahir*). See [[II Samuel 21#^21-17|2 Samuel 21:17]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 85:4; BT [[Ketubot 17#17a|*Ketubbot* 17a]], where Rabbi Abbahu is called: דנהורא‎ בוצינא (*Botsina di-Nhora*), “Lamp of Light”; and [[Berakhot 28#28b|*Berakhot* 28b]], where Rabban Yoḥanan son of Zakkai is called ישראל‎ נר (*Ner Yisra’el*), “Lamp of Israel.”

    In the *Zohar* Rabbi Shim’on is called קדישא‎ בוצינא (*Botsina Qaddisha*), “Holy Lamp.” See 1:3b–4a, 156a, 197b; 3:171a; *ZḤ* 85d (*MhN, Rut*). Cf. above, n. 423.

[^458]: **Let Yo’ezer and El’azar sit together!** The names are virtually identical in meaning, combining a divine name with the verb “to help.”

[^459]: **Why two times: *I*, *I*?** Note how this repetition of the divine pronoun immediately follows the two virtually identical names Yo’ezer and El’azar.

[^460]: ***He*** As the verse continues: *I*, *I am He*.

[^461]: **showing there is no separation between above and below** One *I* signifies *Shekhinah*, who manifested at Sinai; the other *I* signifies *Binah*, the Divine Mother. The singular pronoun *He* indicates the union of the two. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-85b|2:85b]].

[^462]: **The wicked of the world produce a defect above…** Sin ruins the harmony above, splitting the divine couple and interrupting the flow of blessing. *Tif’eret* (symbolized by compassion) separates from *Shekhinah* (“this rung”), and She no longer conveys emanation to those below. So God obliterates human transgression *for My own sake*, to ensure that *Shekhinah* will continue to receive the flow. The wandering Jew is playing on למעני (*le-ma’ani*), *for My own sake*, and יתמנעון (*yitman’un*), “will be withheld.”

[^463]: ***I will bless those who bless you*** Indicating that the *sefirot* who bless Abraham will themselves be blessed with emanation from above.

[^464]: **Isaac appeared…aroused judgment…** Isaac symbolizes *Gevurah*, also known as *Din* (“Judgment”). On the phrase “judgment and Judge prevail,” see *Bereshit Rabbah* 26:6; JT *Sanhedrin* 10:2, 28d; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-72a|1:72a]].

[^465]: **Jacob appeared and aroused compassion…** Jacob symbolizes *Tif’eret*, also known as *Raḥamim* (“Compassion”), balancing right and left.

[^466]: ***Melchizedek, king of* שלם (*Shalem*), *Salem*** The verse continues: *brought out bread and wine; he was priest of God Most High*.

[^467]: **throne was crowned…** Abraham aroused blessings for *Shekhinah*, the divine throne, also known as *Melchizedek* (above, page 55) and *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”). Now She manifested in full royalty.

[^468]: ***Let the earth bring forth*** The verse continues: *living beings, according to their kind*. In the discussion of this verse in *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-13a|1:13a]] and 46b, *the earth* symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who generates life.

[^469]: **He brought forth nourishment…** The subject is *Melchizedek*, symbolizing *Shekhinah*.

[^470]: **for all manifested in supernal perfection** *Shekhinah* conveyed the flow of emanation from *Binah* (*God Most High*).

[^471]: ***He gave him a tenth of everything*** According to the plain meaning of Genesis, the subject is Abraham, who gives a tenth to Melchizedek, but the wandering Jew reverses the roles. See Kasher, *Torah Shelemah*, [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-20|Genesis 14:20]], n. 133; above, note 444.

[^472]: **Of those blessings issuing *from everything*…** The entire flow of emanation proceeds through *Yesod*, known as *everything*, to *Shekhinah*, the tenth *sefirah*, known as *a tenth*. The verse now means: Melchizedek, symbolizing *Shekhinah*, provided Abraham with a tenth of the blessings of emanation.

[^473]: **the blessed Holy One gave him a tenth** Abraham is the recipient. See *ZḤ* [[Zohar 10.05 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^zh-25a|25a]] (*MhN*).

[^474]: **A rung on which abide all openings of faith…** *Shekhinah*, the gateway to the divine realm.

[^475]: **A tenth, one of ten, ten of a hundred** *Shekhinah* is the tenth *sefirah*, and each *sefirah* includes ten aspects.

[^476]: **this sage** Rabbi Yeisa.

[^477]: **Rabbi Shim’on** Father of Rabbi El’azar.

[^478]: **he engaged in the washing of hands** Studying the laws of ritual washing upon waking and before eating.

[^479]: **He** Rabbi Shim’on.

[^480]: **He brings poverty upon himself** See BT [[Shabbat 62#62b|*Shabbat* 62b]]: “Three things lead a person to poverty: urinating in front of one’s bed naked, neglecting the washing of the hands, and being cursed by one’s wife in his presence.” See *Orḥot Ḥayyim*, par. 12.

[^481]: **Fill his hands with Your blessings!** מברכותיך‎ ידיו‎ מלא (*Malle yadav mi-birkhote-kha*). The initial letters of these three words spell מים (*mayim*), “water.” See BT [[Shabbat 62#62b|*Shabbat* 62b]] (the continuation of the passage cited above): “Rav Ḥisda said, ‘I washed with handfuls of water and was granted handfuls of goodness.’” From a kabbalistic perspective, the ten fingers of the human hand symbolize the ten *sefirot*. By pouring water generously over the fingers, one stimulates an abundant flow of blessing above.

[^482]: ***After these things the word of YHVH came to Abram in a vision*** The verse continues: *saying: “Fear not, Abram, I am your shield; your reward is very great.”*

[^483]: **through an arousal below arises an arousal above…** Human action stimulates the divine. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-35a|1:35a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77b|77b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-82b|82b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-86b|86b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-164a|164a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-244a|244a]]; [[Zohar 4.04 - Parashat Va-Era#^z2-31b|2:31b]], [[Zohar 12.05 - Heikhalot Pequdei#^z2-265a|265a]]; [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-92a|3:92a]], [[Zohar 8.05 - Parashat Be-Har#^z3-110b|110b]].

[^484]: **wife of Obadiah…** According to the account in Kings, the prophet Elisha performed a miracle for an anonymous poor woman, providing her with an immense amount of oil. The woman is identified only as *a certain woman, the wife of one of the sons of the prophets* ([[II Kings 04#^4-1|2 Kings 4:1]]), but according to various sources she was the widow of the prophet Obadiah.

    See Josephus, *Antiquities of the Jews* 9:4:2; *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 2:5; *Tanḥuma, Ki Tissa* 5; *Targum*, Rashi, and Radak on [[II Kings 04#^4-1|2 Kings 4:1]]; *[[Zohar 11.04 - Midrash ha-Ne'lam al Rut#^zh-82c|ZḤ* 82c]] (*MhN, Rut*).

[^485]: **blessings from above do not abide on an empty table…** See BT [[Sanhedrin 92#92a|*Sanhedrin* 92a]]: “Rabbi El’azar said, ‘Whoever does not leave bread on his table will never see a sign of blessing.’” Cf. Naḥmanides on [[Torah 19 - Terumah#^25-24|Exodus 25:24]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-240a|1:240a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-250a|250a]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-63b|2:63b]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-67a|67a]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-87b|87b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-153b|153b]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-155a|155a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-157b|157b]]; [[Zohar 7.04 - Parashat Tsav#^z3-34a|3:34a]].

[^486]: **only enough to anoint a little finger** Rabbi Yehudah is playing on the words אסוך (*asukh*), “flask,” and סוך (*sukh*), “to anoint.” See M *Shabbat* 8:1; Rashi on BT [[Shabbat 77#77b|*Shabbat* 77b]], s.v. *ever qatan*.

[^487]: **Here blessings abide** Since the verse describing oil is followed by a reference to blessing, Rabbi Yehudah concludes that blessings abide in oil.

[^488]: **not *oil, dew*!** The verse mentioning blessing concerns dew, not oil. It reads in full: *Like the dew of Herman that descends upon the mountains of Zion, for there YHVH ordained blessing, everlasting life*.

[^489]: **oil and dew are one and the same** Both symbolize the flow of emanation.

[^490]: **wine to the left, oil to the right** Wine and its intoxicating power symbolize *Gevurah*. Oil and its rich nourishment symbolize *Ḥesed*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-72b|1:72b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|96a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-7b|3:7b]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-88b|88b]].

[^491]: **from there Holy Kingdom is anointed** *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”), last of the ten *sefirot*, is anointed by the flow of emanation from *Ḥesed*.

[^492]: **Since oil was prepared below first…** Since Obadiah’s wife had a flask of oil, immense amounts of oil began flowing miraculously. See [[II Kings 04#^4-2|2 Kings 4:2–6]].

[^493]: **to pour upon David and Solomon so that their descendants will be blessed** To anoint David and Solomon and eventually enthrone their royal descendants, who required no subsequent anointing.

[^494]: **How do we know?** That the kings descended from David and Solomon required no anointing.

[^495]: ***The oil stood*** The full verse reads: *When the vessels were full, she said to her son, “Bring me another vessel.” He replied, “There are no more vessels.” Then the oil stood*. The final phrase means: “the oil stopped,” but the word *stood* implies for Rabbi Yehudah that the flow of oil lasted for generations, symbolically anointing the future kings.

[^496]: **Here is written: *stood*, and there is written: *the root of Jesse…stands*…** The repetition of the verb links the miraculous oil with the line of Davidic kings.

[^497]: **table of the Bread of the Presence…** In the wilderness Tabernacle and later in the Temple in Jerusalem, twelve loaves of bread, arranged in two rows, were placed each Sabbath on a golden table. They remained there until the following Sabbath, when they were replaced by twelve fresh loaves.

    See [[Torah 19 - Terumah#^25-30|Exodus 25:30]]; [[Torah 31 - Emor#^24-5|Leviticus 24:5–9]]; Naḥmanides on [[Torah 19 - Terumah#^25-24|Exodus 25:24]]. According to M *Menaḥot* 11:7, the weekly ritual of replacement was performed with such precision that the table was never left empty: “Within \[the sanctuary\] was a table of gold on which the Bread of the Presence lay continually. Four priests entered, two bearing two rows \[of bread\] in their hands and two bearing two dishes \[of frankincense\] in their hands. Four preceded them, two to remove two rows and two to remove two dishes… These withdrew \[the old\] and the others laid down \[the new\], the handbreadth of one coinciding with the handbreadth of the other, for it is written: *before Me continually* ([[Torah 19 - Terumah#^25-30|Exodus 25:30]]).”

[^498]: **one does not bless over an empty table…** When reciting the Grace after Meals, some bread must remain on the table. See above, note 485.

[^499]: **First, *I am my beloved’s*…** First I express my love to God; then once His passion is aroused, He responds. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-132b|3:132b]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.15 - Idra Zuta#^z3-288a|288a]] (*IZ*).

[^500]: ***Shekhinah* is not found among sinners** See BT [[Chagigah 16#16a|*Ḥagigah* 16a]]: “Rabbi Yitsḥak said, ‘Whoever sins secretly, it is as if he thrusts away the feet of *Shekhinah.’”*

[^501]: **If one comes to purify himself…** See BT [[Shabbat 104#104a|*Shabbat* 104a]]: “Resh Lakish said, ‘…If one comes to defile himself, he is provided an opening; if one comes to purify himself, he is assisted.’”

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-54a|1:54a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-56b|56b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-62a|62a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-76b|76b]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-169b|169b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-198b|198b]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-50a|2:50a]]; [[Zohar 7.06 - Parashat Tazri'a#^z3-47a|3:47a]].

[^502]: **after Abraham pursued these kings…** See [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-1|Genesis 14]], and *Vayiqra Rabbah* 1:4: “Rabbi Isaac said, ‘Can anyone pursue those already killed, as is written: *He* \[Abraham\] *smote them and pursued them as far as Hobah* ([[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-15|Genesis 14:15]])? Rather this teaches that the blessed Holy One slew and Abraham pursued.’” Cf. Margaliot’s edition and note; *Bereshit Rabbah* 42(43):3 (on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^14-15|Genesis 14:15]]); above, page 50.

[^503]: **Perhaps, Heaven forbid, I have diminished that reward!…** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 44:4: “Rabbi Levi said, ‘\[*Fear not, Abram…, your reward is very great*.\] Because Abraham was frightened, thinking, “Perhaps there was a righteous or God-fearing man among those troops I killed!”… The blessed Holy One said to him, “Those troops whom you killed were cut-down thorns \[already doomed\].”’” Here Rabbi Yehudah refashions this midrash in accord with the one cited in the previous note, indicating that God, not Abraham, killed the kings.

[^504]: **In that חיזו (*ḥeizu*), mirror—a rung in which all images appear** The Aramaic word חיזו (*ḥeizu*) means “vision, appearance,” but in the *Zohar*, also “mirror.” See 1:91a, 183a; 2:267a. This added sense may derive from the Hebrew word מראה (*mar’ah*), which means both “vision” and “mirror.” Here the mirror is *Shekhinah*, who reflects the images of the higher *sefirot*.

[^505]: **Who was that? *Maḥazeh…Shaddai*** *Shekhinah*, known by the name *Shaddai*. *Shaddai* is an obscure divine name, which may mean “of the mountain.” The traditional rendering “Almighty” is unjustified. See below, page 74.

[^506]: **Once he was circumcised, all those rungs rested upon this rung…** The higher *sefirot* manifested through *Shekhinah*, known as *El Shaddai* (“God of *Shaddai*”). *Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob* symbolize the triad of *Ḥesed, Gevurah*, and *Tif’eret*. Through the rite of circumcision, one is inititated into divine fullness.

    On the link between circumcision and divine vision, see *Bereshit Rabbah* 48:2; *Bemidbar Rabbah* 12:8; *Zohar* [[#^z1-91a|1:91a]]–b, [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-97b|97b–98b]]; Wolfson, *Circle in the Square*, 29–48; idem, *Through a Speculum That Shines*, 342, 357–59, 396–97; idem, “Woman—The Feminine as Other in Theosophic Kabbalah,” 186–88.

    The translation *through El Shaddai* reflects Rabbi Shim’on’s understanding of the phrase in Exodus שדי‎ באל (*be-El Shaddai*), usually translated: *as El Shaddai*. The Hebrew preposition ב (*be*) means “in, as, through, with.”

[^507]: **At first is written: *YHVH appeared to Abram*…** See above, pages 18–19, where these verses allude to various *sefirot: Shekhinah, Ḥesed*, and *Binah*.

[^508]: **mystery of faith** The realm of the *sefirot*.

[^509]: **this lower rung…** *Shekhinah*.

[^510]: **Once circumcised, he enters His name…** The rite of circumcision symbolizes the divine phallus, *Yesod*. Through this act, one participates in the union between *Yesod* and *Shekhinah*, who is known as the divine name. According to *Tanḥuma, Tsav* 14, *Shemini* 8, the mark of circumcision is identified with the י (*yod*) of the divine name שדי (*Shaddai*). A German Hasidic tradition identifies the mark with the *yod* of יהוה (*YHVH*).

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-13a|1:13a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-56a|56a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-95a|95a–b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-216b|216b]]; [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-142a|3:142a]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-215b|215b]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-220a|220a]], 256a (*RM*); Wolfson, in *JQR* 78 (1987): 77–112; idem, *Circle in the Square*, 29–48.

[^511]: **Abraham was linked with it before he was circumcised!** He had already experienced *Shekhinah* and explored the realm of the *sefirot*.

[^512]: **Covenant—cluster of all those supernal rungs…** *Yesod*, symbolized by the covenant of circumcision. He includes the higher *sefirot*, channeling them to *Shekhinah*.

[^513]: **בראשית (*Be-reshit*), *In the beginning*…namely, ברית (*berit*), covenant** The first and last two letters of בראשית (*Be-reshit*) spell ברית (*berit*), “covenant.” On the connection between the two words, see Naḥmanides on [[Torah 02 - Noach#^6-18|Genesis 6:18]]; *TZ, Haqdamah*, 10b.

[^514]: **through Covenant the blessed Holy One erected…** See BT [[Shabbat 137#137b|*Shabbat* 137b]]: “Were it not for the blood of the covenant \[of circumcision\], heaven and earth would not endure, as is said: *Were it not for My covenant day and night, I would not have established the laws of heaven and earth*.” Cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-32a|1:32a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-56a|56a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-59b|59b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-66b|66b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91b|91b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-93b|93b]].

[^515]: **Covenant is the nexus of *day and night*, inseparable** *Yesod* links *Tif’eret* and *Shekhinah*, symbolized by *day and night*.

[^516]: **When the blessed Holy One created the world, it was on condition…** See BT [[Shabbat 88#88a|*Shabbat* 88a]]: “Resh Lakish said, ‘Why is it written: *There was evening and there was morning*, הששי‎ יום (*yom ha-shishi*), *the sixth day*? Why the extra ה (*ha*) \[the definite article, which appears only in this phrase and not in any of the corresponding phrases describing the first five days of Creation\]? This teaches that the blessed Holy One stipulated a condition with the works of Creation, saying to them: “If Israel accepts the Torah \[to be offered at Mount Sinai on the sixth day of the month Sivan\], you will endure. If not, I will return you to *chaos and void* ([[Torah 01 - Bereshit#^1-2|Genesis 1:2]]).”’”

[^517]: **Ever since that day…Human couplings…** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 68:4, in the name of Rabbi Yehudah son of Simon: “A matron asked Rabbi Yose, ‘In how many days did the blessed Holy One create His world?’ He replied, ‘In six days.’ ‘Since then what has He been doing?’ He replied, ‘He sits and matches couples: this man for that woman, this woman for that man.’ She said, ‘If that seems difficult, I can do it!’ She went and matched \[her slaves\], giving this woman to that man, and this man to that woman. After a while, those who had been matched starting beating one another.
