    See [[Psalms 091#^91-6|Psalms 91:6]] and Rashi, ad loc.; *Targum*, [[Song of Songs 4#^4-6|Song of Songs 4:6]]; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 35 - Nasso#^6-24|Numbers 6:24]], [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-24|Deuteronomy 32:24]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-125a|1:125a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-130b|130b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-198b|198b]], [[Zohar 3.05 - Parashat Mi-Qets#^z1-200a|200a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-232b|232b]] (*Tos*); [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-130a|2:130a–b]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-195b|195b]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-205a|205a]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-207a|207a]]. The Hebrew root טהר (*thr*), “pure,” lends this demonic name a euphemistic tone.

[^719]: **he is not handed over to Dumah to descend to Hell** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 48:8: “Rabbi Levi said, ‘In the time to come, Abraham will be sitting at the door to Hell, and he will not let any circumcised Israelite de scend there.’” Cf. BT [[Eruvin 19#19a|*Eruvin* 19a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-8a|1:8a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-93a|93a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-95b|95b]].

[^720]: **for he is linked with the name of the blessed Holy One** See above, note 690.

[^721]: **אותו (*Oto*), *Himself, and His abode*** As David flees Jerusalem, he says this to the priest Zadok. The literal meaning of the phrase is either *Himself and His abode, it* (the ark) *and its abode,* or *it and His abode*. These alternatives are interrelated, since the ark is the abode of the Divine Presence.

[^722]: **he was being punished this way** By being divested of kingship and banished from Jerusalem. See above, page 94.

[^723]: **no longer called righteous** Only one who preserves the covenant of circumcision is called “righteous,” linked with *Yesod,* the divine phallus, known as Righteous One. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-59b|1:59b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-247b|247b]].

[^724]: **What is oto? ‘Sign’ of the holy covenant** The anonymous speaker understands אותו (*oto*) not as: *Himself*, but as: *His sign*. A similar midrashic reading appears in *Shemot Rabbah* 1:20; see *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-47b|1:47b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-112b|112b]]; [[Zohar 9.07 - Parashat Huqqat#^z3-184a|3:184a]].

[^725]: **For these two—Kingdom and Jerusalem—are linked with this** See above, page 94.

[^726]: **so that Kingdom of this ‘sign’ would be restored to its site** So *Malkhut* (“Kingdom”) would be reunited with *Yesod*.

[^727]: **The verse should read: *from my being*** The entire structure of the human being—not merely the flesh—is created in the divine image and reveals the glory of God.

[^728]: **However, *from my flesh*, literally!** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 48:2: “*From my flesh I will see God.* \[Abraham said,\] ‘If I had not done so \[circumcised myself\], how would the blessed Holy One have been revealed to me?’”

[^729]: ***The flesh of holiness… My covenant will be in your flesh*** Both references to *flesh* are understood as indicating the site of circumcision. “Flesh” is a biblical euphemism for “phallus”; see [[Torah 28 - Metzora#^15-2|Leviticus 15:2]]; [[Ezekiel 16#^16-26|Ezekiel 16:26]]; [[Ezekiel 23#^23-20|23:20]].

[^730]: **As long as a person is inscribed… he sees the blessed Holy One…** See Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 2–3 (5).

[^731]: **not having preserved this sign** Succumbing to illicit sexual temptation.

[^732]: **inscription of the blessed Holy One** The mark of circumcision symbolizes the *yod* of יהוה (*YHVH*). See above, note 690.

[^733]: **When he is married** See BT [[Sotah 17#17a|*Sotah* 17a]]: “Rabbi Akiva expounded: ‘ואשה‎ איש (*Ish ve-ishshah*), “Man and woman”: If they are worthy, *Shekhinah* abides between them; if not, fire consumes them.’” As indicated by Rashi (ad loc.), the two Hebrew words ואשה‎ איש (*ish ve-ishshah*), “man and woman,” share the letters א (*alef*) and ש (*shin*), which spell אש (*esh*), “fire”; in addition each contains one of the two letters of the divine name יה (*Yah*). Without divinity between them, only fire remains.

[^734]: **this sign enters its site** When he unites with his wife.

[^735]: **Why do ה׳‎ ו׳ (*vav, he*) go together?** In the name יהוה (*YHVH*), as well as in the alphabet.

[^736]: **one is male and one is female** The letter ו (*vav*), whose numerical value is six, symbolizes *Tif’eret*, who is surrounded by five other *sefirot* (*Ḥesed* through *Yesod*). The letter ה (*he*), often a feminine marker, symbolizes *Tif’eret’s* female partner, *Shekhinah*.

[^737]: **אל (*El*), God, flowing forth from supernal Wisdom** The name אל (*El*) symbolizes *Ḥesed*, which emanates from *Ḥokhmah* (“Wisdom”). See *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-175b|2:175b]]; [[Zohar 7.04 - Parashat Tsav#^z3-30b|3:30b–31a]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-65a|65a]].

[^738]: **crowned by male, so female is rendered fragrant** *Ḥesed* is consummated by *Tif’eret*, who then sweetens *Shekhinah*, normally influenced by severe judgment from the left side.

[^739]: **אלוה (*Eloah*), *God*, is: אל (*El*), God…** This divine name comprises *Ḥesed* (radiating from *Ḥokhmah*), *Tif’eret*, and *Shekhinah*.

[^740]: **why are you dwelling here?** In Kefar Tarsha (Clod Village). Their astounding knowledge of Torah deserves finer surroundings. See above, note 631.

[^741]: **glistening rivers enlighten us with Torah…** See BT [[Horayot 12#12a|*Horayot* 12a]]: “Rabbi Me-sharsheya said to his sons, ‘… When you study, study by a river, so as the waters flow, your teachings may flow. Better to sit on the dunghills of Mata Meḥasya than in the palaces of Pumbedita.’” According to *Zohar* [[Zohar 10.06 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-101a|1:101a]]–b (*MhN*), Kefar Tarsha was renamed Mata Meḥasya.

[^742]: **This place was once condemned above…** According to *Zohar* [[Zohar 10.06 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-101a|1:101a]]–b (*MhN*), a plague ravaged Kefar Tarsha, apparently for the crime of neglecting the study of Torah. When its sinful residents turned back to God, they devoted themeselves to constant study.

[^743]: **Whoever departs from here is like one who departs from eternal life** See BT [[Kiddushin 66#66b|*Qiddushin* 66b]], where Rabbi Tarfon says to Rabbi Akiva, “Akiva, whoever departs from you is as though he departs from life!”

[^744]: **Ordained smoke grasped on earth!** אתפסת‎ בארעא‎ דהורמנא‎ קוטרא (*Qutra de-hurmana be-ar’a ittepisat*). *Qutra* means both “knot” and “smoke” in the *Zohar*. See 1:15a, 30a, 33b, 161b, 172a; 2:80a, 124a; 3:45b, 51a–b, 107a, 289b, 295b (*IZ*). *Hurmana* means “decree, authority.” See BT [[Berakhot 58#58a|*Berakhot* 58a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-15a|1:15a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-76b|76b]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-97a|97a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-108a|108a]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 10.06 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-109b|109b]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-147a|147a]] (*Tos*); [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-123a|2:123a]]; *[[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-67c|ZḤ* 67c]] (*ShS*), 121d. The phrase דהורמנותא‎ קוטרא (*qutra de-hurmanuta*) appears in *[[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-67c|ZḤ* 67c]] (*ShS*); cf. 3:270b (*Mat*).

    Apparently, Rabbi Abba means that the fire decreed above has been perceived and predicted by the prophetic children.

[^745]: **encompassed by fire** See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 16:4: “Ben Azzai was sitting and expounding, and fire was blazing around him.” Cf. BT [[Chagigah 14#14b|*Ḥagigah* 14b]]: “Rabbi El’azar son of Arakh opened, expounding the work of the Chariot, and fire descended from heaven, encompassing all the trees in the field.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.14 - Parashat Ha'azinu#^z3-287b|3:287b]] (*IZ*).

[^746]: **Rabbi Abba’s face blazed in fire…** For similar descriptions, see *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* B, 13; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 2; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Ḥuqqat*, add. 1; *Vayiqra Rabbah* 1:1; 21:12.

[^747]: **house was swathed in smoke** See [[Torah 17 - Yitro#^19-18|Exodus 19:18]]: *Mount Sinai was all in smoke, for YHVH had descended upon it in fire*.

[^748]: **fresh and ecstatic…** חדתאן (*Ḥadtan*), meaning “new” but apparently also implying חדאן (*ḥadan*), “joyous.” See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 16:4: “Ben Azzai was sitting and expounding, and fire was blazing around him. They \[his disciples\] came and told Rabbi Akiva…. He went to him and said, ‘Perhaps you are engaged in the chambers of the Chariot \[the secrets of Ezekiel’s vision of the Chariot\].’ He replied, ‘No, I am stringing words of Torah to the Prophets, and words of the Prophets to the Writings, and the words of Torah are as joyous as on the day they were given from Sinai.’”

    See *Sifrei*, Deuteronomy 58; *Tanḥuma* (Buber), *Yitro* 7; *Ki Tavo* 3.

[^749]: **they did not know if it was day or night** This ecstatic description derives from a story about Rabbi Eli’ezer: When he expounded Torah, “no one knew if it was day or night.” See *Avot de-Rabbi Natan* B, 13; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 2, and David Luria, ad loc., n. 16. Cf. Ibn Ezra on [[Zechariah 14#^14-7|Zechariah 14:7]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 10.10 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-15a|2:15a]] (*MhN*), 105b.

[^750]: **wedding celebration** הלולא (*Hilula*). Circumcision initates the male into an intimate relationship with *Shekhinah*. See above, note 683.

[^751]: **one within the other, one above the other** Symbolizing ever deeper, higher realms of divinity. Cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-129b|1:129b]]–130a.

[^752]: ***Your courts… Zion… Jerusalem*…** Zion and Jerusalem symbolize *Yesod* and *Shekhinah*, the first stages of sefirotic ascent. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-57b|2:57b]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-83a|83a]]; cf. 1:248b; 2:79b.

[^753]: ***By wisdom a house is built*** The primordial point of Wisdom emanates the *house* of *Binah*, source of the lower seven *sefirot*.

[^754]: ***A river issues from Eden to water the garden*** The emanation of *Binah* streams from *Ḥokhmah*, watering *the garden* of *Shekhinah*.

[^755]: **כל‎ י׳‎ ה׳ (*He yod kol*), this and that, ‘all’ consummated as one** The word היכל (*heikhal*), *temple*, comprises *Binah* (symbolized by the ה \[*he*\] of יהוה \[*YHVH*\]), *Ḥokhmah* (symbolized by the primordial point, י \[*yod*\]), and the word כל (*kol*), “all,” signifying their union and the sefirotic totality.

[^756]: **When a person brings his son as an offering…** The anonymous speaker sees the ritual of circumcision as a sacrificial offering. See above, note 656.

[^757]: **He desires him** God desires the newborn.

[^758]: **accommodating him in two chambers** Initiating him into the *courts* of divinity *Shekhinah* and *Yesod*.

[^759]: **he** possesses That is, the newborn.

[^760]: **this place** Kefar Tarsha.

[^761]: **they would open by proclaiming…** It became customary to recite this verse at the beginning of the ritual of circumcision.

[^762]: **by him** By the father.

[^763]: **he would say** That is, the father.

[^764]: **‘who has sanctified us with His commandments…’** The blessing begins: “Blessed are You, *YHVH* our God, King of the world.”

[^765]: **‘As you have initiated him into the covenant…’** The response continues: “so may you merit to initiate him into Torah, the wedding canopy, and good deeds.” The blessing and response derive from BT [[Shabbat 137#137b|*Shabbat* 137b]].

[^766]: **First, a person should pray for himself, then for another** Just as the respondents first pray, “*May we be sated*…” before saying, “As you have initiated him into the covenant, so may you merit to initiate him into Torah, the wedding canopy, and good deeds.”

    Cf. BT [[Bava Kamma 92#92a|*Bava Qamma* 92a]], where the opposite sequence is recommended: “Whoever seeks compassion for his fellow, while he himself needs the same thing, will be answered first.”

[^767]: ***he will make atonement for himself*, first; then…** See BT [[Yoma 44#44a|*Yoma* 44a]]: “*He will make atonement for himself and for his household and for the whole congregation of Israel*. His own atonement precedes the atonement for *his household*, and the atonement for *his household* precedes the atonement for his fellow priests, and the atonement for his fellow priests precedes the atonement for *the whole congregation of Israel*.”

[^768]: **this custom** The custom of the ancient *ḥasidim*.

[^769]: **ten canopies that the blessed Holy One intends to erect…** The anonymous speaker here combines two separate traditions reported in BT [[Bava Batra 75#75a|*Bava Batra* 75a]]: “Rabbah said in the name of Rabbi Yoḥanan, ‘The blessed Holy One will someday erect seven canopies for every single righteous person.’ … Rabbi Ḥama son of Rabbi Ḥanina said, ‘The blessed Holy One erected ten canopies for Adam in the Garden of Eden, as it is said: *You were in Eden, the garden of God; every precious stone* \[*was your adornment: carnelian, chrysolite, and amethyst; beryl, lapis lazuli, and jasper; sapphire, turquoise, emerald, and gold*\] ([[Ezekiel 28#^28-13|Ezekiel 28:13]]).’” The ten gems suggest ten canopies.

    A similar combination appears in *Seder Gan Eden* (*Beit ha-Midrash*, 3:138).

[^770]: **all tied to this** All connected with this verse.

[^771]: **so ten words of faith appear in this verse…** The Hebrew verse contains ten words, each one alluding to one of the canopies and to one of the ten *sefirot*, the realm of mystical faith.

[^772]: **as if you yourselves stood at Mount Sinai…** See above, note 748.

[^773]: **Whoever brings his son as this offering…** Circumcision is the ideal sacrifice. See Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 55 (68); *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-66b|2:66b]]; above, note 656.

[^774]: **one should arrange an altar in a vessel filled with earth…** So that the foreskin is covered with dust. See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 29: “One covers the foreskin and blood with the dust of the earth.”

    Cf. David Luria, ad loc., n. 52; idem, *Qadmut Sefer ha-Zohar*, 15; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 40 - Balak#^23-10|Numbers 23:10]]; Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 55 (68); *Zohar* [[Zohar 12.06 - Piqqudin#^z3-44a|3:44a]] (*Piq*), 257a (*RM*); *TZ, Haqdamah*, 11a; *NZ*, 1:95a, n. 2; *Maḥazor Vitri* 505 (625); Ibn Yarḥi, *Sefer ha-Manhig, Hilkhot Milah*, 127; David Abudarham, *Sefer Abudarham, milah;* Joseph Karo, *Beit Yosef, Yoreh De’ah* 265:10.

    According to [[Torah 29 - Achrei Mot#^17-13|Leviticus 17:13]], the blood of an animal that has been hunted and killed must be covered with the dust of the earth.

[^775]: **What does this mean: *I record My name*? Circumcision** According to *Tanḥuma, Tsav* 14, *Shemini* 8, the mark of circumcision is identified with the י (*yod*) of the divine name שדי (*Shaddai*). A German Hasidic tradition identifies the mark with the *yod* of יהוה (*YHVH*).

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-13a|1:13a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-56a|56a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-95a|95a–b]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-216b|216b]]; [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-142a|3:142a]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-215b|215b]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-220a|220a]], 256a (*RM*); Wolfson, in *JQR* 78 (1987): 77–112; idem, *Circle in the Square*, 29–48.

[^776]: ***The secret of YHVH… His covenant*** See *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 19: “*The secret of YHVH is for those in awe of Him; to them He reveals His covenant*. Which secret did He reveal to *those in awe of Him?* Circumcision, for the blessed Holy One revealed the mystery of circumcision only to Abraham…, to whom was said: *For you are in awe of God* ([[Torah 04 - Vayera#^22-12|Genesis 22:12]]).” Cf. *Bereshit Rabbah* 49:2.

[^777]: ***If you make an altar of stones for Me*** The verse continues: *do not build it hewn, for by wielding your tool upon it you defile it*.

[^778]: **One must introduce him to worshiping the blessed Holy One** See BT [[Yevamot 47#47a|*Yevamot* 47a]]–b.

[^779]: **One should rejoice in this share** Appreciating the blessed gift of the *mitsvah* of circumcision.

[^780]: ***El Shaddai*** “God of *Shaddai*.” *Shaddai* is an obscure divine name, which may mean “of the mountain.” The traditional rendering “Almighty God” is unjustified.

[^781]: **first, שנה (*shanah*), *year*, and then שנים (*shanim*), *years*** The phrase שנה‎ תשעים (*tish’im shanah*), *ninety years*, employs the normally singular form שנה (*shanah*), literally “year.” The anonymous speaker subjects this idiomatic usage to a hyperliteral reading, insisting on the singular connotation.

[^782]: ***appeared* is not written** Actually the identical expression, *YHVH appeared to Abram*, occurs in [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^12-7|Genesis 12:7]]. See above, page 74; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-98a|1:98a]].

[^783]: **obstructed and concealed** Uncircumcised, still covered by the foreskin. See above, pages 90–91; *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]].

[^784]: **did not reveal Himself to him fittingly** See *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 20: “*Abram fell on his face* \[*and God spoke with him*\] ([[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^17-3|Genesis 17:3]]). Until he was circumcised, whenever *Shekhinah* spoke with him, he fell down. Once he was circumcised, He spoke with him while he was standing, as is said: *Abraham remained standing in the presence of YHVH* (ibid. 18:22). Further, He revealed Himself to him even while he was sitting, as is said: *YHVH appeared to him by the terebinths of Mamre* \[*as he was sitting at the opening of the tent*\] (ibid., 1).”

    See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 29: “Rabban Gamliel son of Rabbi Yehudah the Prince said, ‘… The blessed Holy One said to the ministering angels, “Come and see the power of circumcision. Before Abraham was circumcised, he fell and then I spoke with him, as is said: *Abram fell on his face* \[*and God spoke with him*\]. Now that he is circumcised, he sits and I stand, as is said: *He was sitting at the opening of the tent* (ibid. 18:1), and it is written: *He raised his eyes and saw: here, three men standing over him* (ibid. 18:2).”’”

[^785]: **this sign, the holy crown** In the ritual of circumcision, first the foreskin is removed, disclosing the mucous membrane, which is then torn down the center and pulled back, revealing the corona. The removal of the foreskin symbolizes *Yesod*, the divine phallus, while uncovering the corona symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who is known as *Atarah* (“Corona, Crown”). Here the word for “crown” is not עטרה (*atarah*) but כתר (*keter*), which also alludes to *Shekhinah* (above, page 94).

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-13a|1:13a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-32a|32a]] (*Tos*), [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91b|91b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96b|96b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-98b|98b]] (*ST*); [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-91b|3:91b]]; Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 55 (67); idem, *Sefer ha-Mishqal*, 133. On Abraham and uncovering the corona, see BT [[Yevamot 71#71b|*Yevamot* 71b]]; *Tosafot*, ad loc., s.v. *sof milah*; Rashi on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^17-25|Genesis 17:25]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-163b|3:163b]]. On *Shekhinah* as corona of the phallus, see Wolfson, *Through a Speculum That Shines*, 274–75, n. 14, 357–68; idem, “Woman—The Feminine as Other in Theosophic Kabbalah”; idem, *Circle in the Square*, 85–92.

[^786]: **He was also about to generate from him holy seed…** Isaac. See *Maḥazor Vitri*, 507: “Until Abraham was circumcised, he did not engender holy seed.”

[^787]: **for his days were not days** Because Abraham was uncircumcised, his life was deficient.

[^788]: **Now that he arrived here…** Now, as he was about to be circumcised, his entire life was fulfilled.

[^789]: **lower crowns, unholy, below** Demonic forces, parallel to the *sefirot*—which are holy crowns.

[^790]: **ד׳‎ ש׳ (*shin, dalet*)—nothing more** Spelling שד (*shed*), “demon.” See *Tanḥuma, Tsav* 14; David ben Judah he-Ḥasid, *Mar’ot ha-Ẓove’ot*, 70.

[^791]: **enter under the wings of *Shekhinah*** The phrase usually applies to conversion. See *Sifrei*, [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-1|Deuteronomy 32]]; BT [[Shabbat 31#31a|*Shabbat* 31a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-13a|1:13a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-14a|3:14a]]. Cf. [[Ruth 2#^2-12|Ruth 2:12]].

[^792]: **The holy mark י (*yod*) is revealed in them… שדי (*Shaddai*)…** According to *Tanḥuma, Tsav* 14, *Shemini* 8, the mark of circumcision is identified with the י (*yod*) of the divine name שדי (*Shaddai*). Here the *yod* transforms the word שד (*shed*), “demon,” into שדי (*Shaddai*).

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-13a|1:13a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-56a|56a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-89a|89a]]; [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]], [[Zohar 6.05 - Parashat Va-Yaqhel#^z2-216b|216b]]; [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-142a|3:142a]] (*IR*), [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-215b|215b]], [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-220a|220a]], 256a (*RM*); Wolfson, in *JQR* 78 (1987): 77–112; idem, *Circle in the Square*, 29–48.

[^793]: **The one from whom blessings issue…** *Yesod*.

[^794]: **he does not descend to Hell** See *Bereshit Rabbah* 48:8: “Rabbi Levi said, ‘In the time to come, Abraham will be sitting at the door to Hell, and he will not let any circumcised Israelite descend there.’” Cf. BT [[Eruvin 19#19a|*Eruvin* 19a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-8a|1:8a]]; above, pages 88, 94.

[^795]: **All those who are circumcised are called *righteous*…** Through the act of circumcision, one imitates *Yesod*, the divine phallus—known as Righteous One—who unites with *Shekhinah*, symbolized by *the land.* All those who are circumcised escape Hell and attain *Shekhinah*.

    The verse from Isaiah, appearing frequently in the *Zohar*, is cited in M *Sanhedrin* 10:1 to demonstrate that “all of Israel have a share in the world that is coming.”

[^796]: **Of you is written: *This one will say*…** Rabbi Abba may be applying the three parts of the verse to the three last speakers.

[^797]: **that land called Land of the Living** *Shekhinah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-65b|1:65b]]–66a, [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-115a|115a]], [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-124b|124b]], [[Zohar 2.05 - Parashat Toledot#^z1-143b|143b]]; Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh,* 62 (77).

[^798]: **manna of holy dew** Symbolizing the emanation from above. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-61b|2:61b]], [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-62b|62b]]. Cf. 1:143b, 225b, 232a; 2:176b (*SdTs*)*,* 210a; 3:128b (*IR*), 135a-b (*IR*), 288a (*IZ*), 292b (*IZ*).

[^799]: **supernal land, regulating all supernal life…** *Shekhinah*, who conveys the vivifying flow of emanation.

[^800]: ***a land that YHVH your God looks after*** She is cared for by Her spouse, *Tif’eret*.

[^801]: ***where you will not lack* כל (*kol*), *anything*, precisely!** *Shekhinah* contains the entirety of emanation, conveyed to Her by *Yesod*, known as *kol*, “everything, anything.”

[^802]: **the blessed Holy One, as is said: *My firstborn son is Israel*** *Tif’eret* (the blessed Holy One), whose full name is *Tif’eret Yisra’el*, “Beauty of Israel,” is the son of higher sefirotic parents. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-124b|1:124b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-223b|223b]].

[^803]: **all freedom comes from Jubilee** In the Bible every seventh year is a Sabbatical, during which the land must lie fallow and at the end of which all debts are remitted ([[Torah 32 - Behar#^25-1|Leviticus 25:1–24]]; [[Torah 47 - Re'eh#^15-1|Deuteronomy 15:1–3]]). After seven Sabbaticals comes the Jubilee, proclaimed every fifty years, when slaves are released and land reverts to its original owner ([[Torah 32 - Behar#^25-8|Leviticus 25:8–55]]).

    In Kabbalah the Jubilee symbolizes *Binah*, the Divine Mother, who generally is characterized by the number fifty, based on BT [[Rosh Hashanah 21#21b|*Rosh ha-Shanah* 21b]], where Rav and Shemu’el teach: “Fifty gates of בינה (*binah*), understanding, were created in the world, all of which were given to Moses except for one, as is said: *You made him little less than God* ([[Psalms 008#^8-6|Psalms 8:6]]).” *Binah* is the source of redemption and liberation, specifically the Exodus from Egypt. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-21b|1:21b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-47b|47b–48a]]; [[Zohar 4.06 - Parashat Be-Shallah#^z2-46a|2:46a]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-83b|83b]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-85b|85b]], [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-93a|93a]]; [[Zohar 9.12 - Parashat Va-Ethannan#^z3-262a|3:262a]].

[^804]: **So, *son of freedom*** *Tif’eret* is the son of *Binah* (Jubilee).

[^805]: ***son of* חורין (*ḥorin*), *free ones*, not *son of* חירות (*ḥerut*), *freedom*** The verse actually reads: *son of* חורים (*ḥorim*), a Hebrew equivalent of the Aramaized form חורין (*ḥorin*). The question is: If King *Tif’eret* is the son of *Binah*, who is symbolized by freedom, why the plural form *ḥorin, free ones*?

[^806]: **our concealed Mishnah** The *Zohar* often cites teachings from a secret, mystical Mishnah known only to its own circle. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-37b|1:37b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-55b|55b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-74a|74a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91b|91b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-93a|93a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-96a|96a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-224a|224a]], 252a (*Hash*); [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-57b|3:57b]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-61b|61b]], [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-78a|78a]], [[Zohar 9.13 - Parashat Va-Yelekh#^z3-284b|284b]], [[Zohar 9.13 - Parashat Va-Yelekh#^z3-285a|285a]]. This is to be distinguished from the *Matnitin* of the *Zohar*, on which see Scholem, *Kabbalah*, 216.

[^807]: **When י (*yod*) unites with ה (*he*), then this verse is fulfilled…** The י (*yod*) of יהוה (*YHVH*) symbolizes the primordial point of *Ḥokhmah* (Divine Father), while the next letter, ה (*he*), a feminine marker, symbolizes *Binah* (Divine Mother). Their union generates the flow of emanation watering the garden of *Shekhinah*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-10b|3:10b]].

[^808]: **Do not say: ‘when they unite,’ but rather: ‘they unite,’ literally!** Do not imagine that the union of *Ḥokhmah* and *Binah* is intermittent; it is constant, as indicated by the present tense: *A river issues*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-83a|2:83a]]: “Therefore it is written: *issues—issues* incessantly.” Cf. 3:65b: “It is written: *A river issues from Eden—issues*, not *issued*.”

    On the perpetual union of *Ḥokhmah* and *Binah*, see 1:123a, 153b; 2:11b; 3:4a, 78a.

[^809]: **So it is written: *son of ḥorin, free ones*** The plural indicates both *Ḥokhmah* and *Binah*.

[^810]: ***and whose princes*…** The *princes* of *Shekhinah* (the *land*) are either angelic powers beneath Her or the virtuous among Israel.

[^811]: **this one below** Earth.

[^812]: **All other lands of other nations were granted…** According to rabbinic tradition, the seventy nations of the world are governed by seventy angels or heavenly princes appointed by God.

    See [[Daniel 10#^10-20|Daniel 10:20]]; Septuagint, [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-8|Deuteronomy 32:8–9]]; lubilees 15:31–32; *Targum Yerushalmi*, [[Torah 02 - Noach#^11-8|Genesis 11:8]], [[Torah 53 - Ha'Azinu#^32-8|Deuteronomy 32:8–9]]; *Tanḥuma, Re’eh* 8; *Leqaḥ Tov*, [[Torah 02 - Noach#^9-19|Genesis 9:19]]; *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 24; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-46b|1:46b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-108b|108b]]; [[Zohar 9.16 - Commentary on Ha'azinu, continued#^z3-298b|3:298b]]; Ginzberg, *Legends*, 5:204–5, n. 91.

[^813]: **he of whom is written: *I was a child*… the Prince of the World** Metatron, the chief angel, also known as נער (*na’ar*), the “child,” “lad,” or heavenly “servant.” See BT [[Yevamot 16#16b|*Yevamot* 16b]]: “Rabbi Shemu’el son of Naḥman said in the name of Rabbi Yonatan, ‘This verse was spoken by the Prince of the World: *I was a child and now I am old*. Who spoke it? If you suggest the blessed Holy One, does He age? Then perhaps David spoke it; but was he so old? You must conclude that the Prince of the World spoke it.’”

    See *Tosafot, Yevamot* 16b, s.v. *pasuq zeh; Zohar* 1:124b, 143a, 162a–b, 181b; Scholem, *Kabbalah*, 379–80; Margaliot, *Mal’akhei Elyon*, 89–90.

[^814]: **Woe to the world, sucking from this side!** Sucking from Metatron and not directly from *Shekhinah*.

[^815]: **like one who sucks from an alien power** In exile, Israel is deprived of direct divine sustenance and is nourished only by the demonic realm.

    See *Sifra, Behar* 5:4, 109c: “Every Israelite who dwells in the land of Israel accepts upon himself the yoke of the Kingdom of Heaven, and everyone who leaves the land is like an idol worshiper.”

    See *Tosefta, Avodah Zarah* 4:5; BT [[Ketubot 110#110b|*Ketubbot* 110b]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-153a|1:153a]], [[Zohar 3.03 - Parashat Va-Yishlah#^z1-177a|177a]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-152b|2:152b]]; [[Zohar 9.12 - Parashat Va-Ethannan#^z3-266b|3:266b]].

    See BT *Ta’anit* 10a: “The land of Israel is watered by the blessed Holy One Himself, while the rest of the world is watered by a messenger…. The land of Israel drinks water directly from the rain, while the rest of the world drinks of the drippings.”

[^816]: ***And whose princes*** The princes of this world are the heavenly powers appointed over the seventy nations.

[^817]: **When the sun begins to shine…** See BT [[Berakhot 7#7a|*Berakhot* 7a]]: “It was taught in the name of Rabbi Me’ir: ‘When the sun begins to shine and all the kings of the East and West put on their crowns and bow down to the sun, immediately the blessed Holy One turns angry.’” Cf. *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.04 - Parashat Ki Tissa#^z2-188a|2:188a]].

[^818]: **at the time of afternoon prayer wrath looms over the world** As evening approaches, the power of strict Judgment strengthens. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-132b|1:132b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-230a|230a]]; Tishby, *Wisdom of the Zohar*, 3:963.

[^819]: **Who causes this?… that one called *child*** Metatron, who, unlike *Shekhinah*, conveys only a fraction of emanation. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.04 - Parashat Hayyei Sarah#^z1-124b|1:124b]].

[^820]: **holy ones of the Most High** In [[Daniel 07#^7-18|Daniel 7:18]], the phrase refers to Israel; here, to the virtuous illuminati of Kefar Tarsha. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-67b|1:67b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-77a|77a]].

[^821]: **from this side** From Metatron.

[^822]: **from that holy site above** From *Shekhinah*.

[^823]: **It should be called *rebuke*** In Isaiah’s parable, the vineyard symbolizes Israel, who has yielded bad fruit and will be punished. See verses 3–7.

[^824]: **the word should be: לדודי (*le-dodi*), *for my lover*, as is written…** The more common word, *dodi*, appears immediately afterward and often in the Song of Songs, where it is understood midrashically as referring to God, Israel’s lover.

[^825]: **I have searched through the entire Torah…** Rabbi Abba understands the phrase *qeren ben shemen* as a place-name. His exhaustive search is modeled on midrashic exegesis. See *Midrash Tanna’im*, [[Torah 44 - Devarim#^1-1|Deuteronomy 1:1]]; *Bereshit Rabbah* 85:7; 100:6; *Qohelet Rabbah* on 7:1; *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 13; *Pesiqta Rabbati* 16; *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-71b|3:71b]].

[^826]: **Companions have already established these verses…** See BT [[Sukkah 49#49a|*Sukkah* 49a]]; *Tanḥuma, Vayelekh* 2.

[^827]: **Isaac, who was *beloved*, called *beloved* before issuing into the world** According to BT [[Shabbat 137#137b|*Shabbat* 137b]], the blessing over circumcision includes the phrase: “who sanctified the beloved one from the womb.” Rashi interprets this as a reference to Isaac. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.05 - Parashat Shemini#^z3-39b|3:39b]]; [[Torah 04 - Vayera#^22-2|Genesis 22:2]]; *Ba’al ha-Turim* on [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^17-2|Genesis 17:2]].

[^828]: **circumcised, called ‘complete,’ augmented by ה (*he*) for consummation** God’s covenant with Abraham was marked both by the sign of circumcision and a change of name ([[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^17-1|Genesis 17]]). With the addition of the letter ה (*he*), the patriarch’s name was changed from אברם (*Avram*) to אברהם (*Avraham*). His new name and circumcision rendered him complete, as indicated by [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^17-1|Genesis 17:1]]: *Walk in My presence and be complete*. Further, the full name אברהם (*Avraham*) is numerically equivalent to 248, the total number of limbs in the human body (actually joints or bones covered with flesh and sinews, indicating that now Abraham attained mastery over all his limbs. See M *Oholot* 1:8; *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 16; BT [[Nedarim 32#32b|*Nedarim* 32b]].

[^829]: **Similarly, with Sarah: this ה (*he*) was bestowed upon her** Her name was changed from שרי (*Sarai*) to שרה (*Sarah*). See [[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^17-15|Genesis 17:15]].

[^830]: **For Sarah, ה (*he*) is appropriate, but for Abraham…** The letter ה (*he*) is a feminine marker and symbolizes the divine feminine, *Shekhinah*, so it befits Sarah. Abraham, however, should have been given the letter י (*yod*), a masculine prefix. See *[[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-72b|ZḤ* 72b]] (*ShS*).

[^831]: **supernal ה (*he*), World of the Male** *Binah* is symbolized by the first ה (*he*) in the name יהוה (*YHVH*). Although often depicted as the Divine Mother, *Binah* is also described as “World of the Male,” encompassing the entire configuration of *sefirot* from *Ḥesed* through *Yesod*. Together they constitute a masculine entity ready to join *Shekhinah*.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-147a|1:147a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-149a|149a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-160b|160b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-246a|246a–247a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-248b|248b]]; [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-127b|2:127b]]; *[[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-72b|ZḤ* 72b]] (*ShS*). Cf. 1:17b, 46b, 163a; 2:4a.

[^832]: **Higher *he* and lower he…** *Binah* and *Shekhinah;* the former is dependent on the masculine *sefirah* of *Ḥokhmah* and shares His masculinity, while the latter is entirely female.

[^833]: **כה (*Koh*), *So, shall your seed be*…** The verse reads: *He took him* \[*Abraham*\] *outside and said: ‘Look now toward heaven and count the stars, if you can count them.’ Then He said to him, ‘So shall your seed be.’*

    In Kabbalah *Koh* (“so, thus, here”) is a name of *Shekhinah*, the Divine Presence. Rabbi Abba may be implying that the numerical value of כה (*Koh*), 25, is the product of ה (*he*) times ה (*he*), 5 × 5. Through circumcision the prototypical convert, Abraham, entered the covenant of *Shekhinah*. All future converts who are circumcised enter Her as well and become children of Abraham, “*your seed*, literally.” See above, 90b; *Zohar* [[Zohar 8.10 - Commentary on Naso, continued#^z3-148a|3:148a]]; Gikatilla, *Sha’arei Orah*, 12a–13a.

[^834]: **‘convert of Righteousness’…** Convert of *Shekhinah*, who is known as Righteousness. Converts partake of *Shekhinah* but ascend no higher. See Judah Halevi, *Kuzari* 1:27; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-13a|1:13a]]; [[Zohar 4.07 - Parashat Yitro#^z2-87a|2:87a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-14a|3:14a–b]].

[^835]: **is called thus: Abraham** It was customary for converts to adopt the name Abraham or be called “son (or daughter) of Abraham.”

[^836]: **if *he* had not been transmitted to Sarah…** Without her *he*, Abraham’s sole he would have symbolized *Shekhinah* (*So*), who engenders below, outside the sefirotic realm.

[^837]: **Once *he* was transmitted to Sarah, the two *he*’s joined as one…** Now, since Sarah’s *he* symbolizes *Shekhinah*, Abraham’s *he* symbolizes *Binah*. Through their union they engender above.

[^838]: **What issued from them is י (*yod*)** Whose numerical value is 10, equal to ה (*he*) plus ה (*he*), 5 + 5.

[^839]: **so *yod* is the first letter of יצחק (*Yitsḥaq*), Isaac—male** The letter *yod*, a masculine prefix, characterizes Isaac. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-142a|3:142a]] (*IR*).

[^840]: **From here the male begins to expand** Isaac symbolizes *Gevurah*, the extension of the masculine configuration of *sefirot* known as *Ze’eir Anpin* (“Impatient One”), which begins with *Ḥesed*. The letter י (*yod*) is linked with *Ḥesed* in *Zohar* [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-36a|2:36a]]; [[Zohar 8.09 - Idra Rabba#^z3-142a|3:142a]] (*IR*).

[^841]: ***through Isaac*, not through you** Through Isaac, who was born to a circumcised father, not through you, born to one uncircumcised.

[^842]: **Isaac engendered above…** Isaac engendered Jacob, who attained *Tif’eret* (symbolized by *truth*) and harmonized the opposite qualities of *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*.

[^843]: **Was Abraham linked here, no higher?** Did Abraham attain only the rung of *Shekhinah?*

[^844]: ***Ḥesed, loving-kindness, to Abraham*** The continuation of the verse just cited: *You give truth to Jacob, loving-kindness to Abraham*. Through discovering God and expressing love, Abraham eventually attained the rung of *Ḥesed* (“Love”). See *Shir ha-Shirim Rabbah* on 1:3; *Bahir* 92 (135), 131–32 (190–91); above, pages 18–19, 29.

[^845]: **from here he began** See above, pages 16–18, 32–34.

[^846]: **So Abraham was circumcised only at the age of ninety-nine** Verging on one hundred, which symbolizes *Shekhinah*, who includes ten aspects of all ten *sefirot*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-127a|2:127a]]; 3:179a, [[Zohar 9.11 - Parashat Pinehas#^z3-254a|254a]] (*RM*).

[^847]: **established in our Mishnah** The *Zohar* often cites teachings from a secret, mystical Mishnah known only to its own circle. See 1:37b, 55b, 74a, 91b, 93a, 95b, 224a, 252a (*Hash*); 3:57b, 61b, 78a, 284b, 285a. This is to be distinguished from the *Matnitin* of the *Zohar*, on which see Scholem, *Kabbalah*, 216.

[^848]: **Isaac—severe Judgment… while for engendering he was called *Ḥesed*** Isaac symbolizes the *sefirah* of *Gevurah*, or *Din* (severe “Judgment”); but in engendering Jacob, he tended toward *Ḥesed* (“Love”).

[^849]: **their shares above** *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*.

[^850]: **from below to above** Toward *Tif’eret*, the site of Jacob.

[^851]: ***Israel*… blended colors…** Jacob—known as Israel—combined the white of *Ḥesed*, the red of *Gevurah*, and the green of *Tif’eret*.

[^852]: **So here appears *song… a song*—indeed!** Signifying celebration, not rebuke. See above, page 107.

[^853]: **called to engender male, called so before issuing into the world** Isaac was destined to engender Jacob, who symbolizes consummation.

[^854]: ***Let me sing for my beloved*—Abraham** … See BT [[Menachot 53#53b|*Menaḥot* 53b]]: “Rabbi Yitsḥak said, ‘When the Temple was destroyed, the blessed Holy One found Abraham standing there. He said to him, *‘What is My beloved doing in My house?’* He replied, ‘I have come concerning my children.’”

[^855]: **Abraham inherited this allotted share** Abraham, *My beloved*, attained *Ḥesed*.

[^856]: ***This is my lover; my lover is radiant and ruddy*** The Midrash on Song of Songs interprets this *lover* as God. Here the *lover* is *Tif’eret* (the blessed Holy One), who is both *radiant* and *ruddy*, harmonizing *Ḥesed* and *Gevurah*.

[^857]: ***My beloved* embraces *my lover*, male, and from him a vineyard is sown…** *Gevurah* (symbolized by Isaac, *my beloved*) joins *Tif’eret* (the blessed Holy One, *my lover*), sowing the vineyard of Israel. See [[Isaiah 05#^5-7|Isaiah 5:7]].

[^858]: **It begins *on the horn of Jubilee*** In the Bible every seventh year is a Sabbatical, during which the land must lie fallow and at the end of which all debts are remitted ([[Torah 32 - Behar#^25-1|Leviticus 25:1–24]]; [[Torah 47 - Re'eh#^15-1|Deuteronomy 15:1–3]]). After seven Sabbaticals comes the Jubilee, proclaimed every fifty years, when slaves are released and land reverts to its original owner ([[Torah 32 - Behar#^25-8|Leviticus 25:8–55]]).

    In Kabbalah the Jubilee symbolizes *Binah*, the Divine Mother, who generally is characterized by the number fifty, based on BT [[Rosh Hashanah 21#21b|*Rosh ha-Shanah* 21b]], where Rav and Shemu’el teach: “Fifty gates of בינה (*binah*), understanding, were created in the world, all of which were given to Moses except for one, as is said: *You made him little less than God* ([[Psalms 008#^8-6|Psalms 8:6]]).” *Binah* is the source of redemption and liberation, specifically the Exodus from Egypt. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-21b|1:21b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-47b|47b–48a]] 2:46a, 83b, 85b, 93a, 95b; 3:262a.

    *The horn of Jubilee* is *Shekhinah*, intimately linked with the vineyard of Israel. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-123a|2:123a]], [[Zohar 5.06 - Sifra di-Tsni'uta#^z2-179a|179a]] (*SdTs*); [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-19b|3:19b]].

[^859]: **What is *ben shamen, son of oil*?… חורין‎ בן (*ben ḥorin*), *son of free ones*** *Tif’eret*, generated by the flow of emanation from *Ḥokhmah* and *Binah*. See above, pages 104–6, where He is described as *son of free ones*, namely, the divine parents: *Ḥokhmah* and *Binah*.

[^860]: **kindling lamps** Kindling the lower *sefirot*.

[^861]: **So royalty is always anointed with a horn…** See *Vayiqra Rabbah* 10:8: “Kings are anointed only out of a horn. Saul and Jehu were anointed out of a flask, so their kingdoms were transient; David and Solomon were anointed out of a horn, so their kingdom was everlasting.” Here the horn symbolizes *Shekhinah*, with whom David was intimately linked.

    See [[I Samuel 10#^10-1|1 Samuel 10:1]]; [[I Samuel 16#^16-13|16:13]]; [[I Kings 01#^1-39|1 Kings 1:39]]; [[II Kings 09#^9-1|2 Kings 9:1–3]]; JT *Sheqalim* 6:1, 49d; BT [[Horayot 12#12a|*Horayot* 12a]]; *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.06 - Sifra di-Tsni'uta#^z2-179a|2:179a]] (*SdTs*); Moses de León, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 71–72 (90)

[^862]: **ויעזקהו (*Vay’azzeqehu*), *He enclosed it*,…like עזקא (*izqa*), a ring…** God protected Israel on all sides. See Rashi and Radak, ad loc.

[^863]: **removing from it and its share all those princes…** Removing from Israel and *Shekhinah* the seventy heavenly princes who control the other nations of the world. See above, note 812.

[^864]: **כלה (*kulloh*), *entirely*, spelled with a ה (*he*): כלה (*kallah*), *bride*** The normal spelling would be: כלו (*kullo*), with the pronominal suffix ו (*vav*). The ancient pronominal form ה (*he*) stimulates Rabbi Abba to read the word as כלה (*kallah*), *bride*, alluding to the divine bride, *Shekhinah*.

[^865]: **from this issued *seed of truth—entirely seed of truth*, indeed** Alluding to Jacob, who symbolizes *Tif’eret*, called *truth*. See above, page 109.

[^866]: **כה (*Koh*), *So, shall your seed be*** כה (*Koh*), *So*, is a name of *Shekhinah*, from whom Abraham began to engender. See above, pages 108–9.

[^867]: **all one entity** Both *kulloh* and *koh* refer to *Shekhinah*.

[^868]: ***tower of strength… into which runs the righteous one*, literally!** The tower is *Shekhinah* (the divine name), who is entered by *Yesod*, known as Righteous One. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-9a|1:9a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-37b|37b]]; [[Zohar 9.04 - Rav Metivta#^z3-164a|3:164a–b]].

    *Yesod* is called Righteous One, based on [[Proverbs 10#^10-25|Proverbs 10:25]]: עולם‎ יסוד‎ וצדיק (*Ve-tsaddiq yesod olam*). The verse, which literally means *The righteous one is an everlasting foundation*, is understood as *The righteous one is the foundation of the world*.

    See BT [[Chagigah 12#12b|*Ḥagigah* 12b]]; *Bahir* 71 (102); Azriel of Gerona, *Peirush ha-Aggadot*, 34.

[^869]: **gate of Righteousness…** Opening of *Shekhinah*, known as Righteousness, through which one enters *Yesod*. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-54b|1:54b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-93a|93a]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-98b|98b]] (*ST*), [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-103b|103b]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-14a|3:14a]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-95a|95a]].

[^870]: **enters both of them, attaining both of them** One who is circumcised enters both *Shekhinah* and *Yesod*. In the ritual of circumcision, first the foreskin is cut and removed, disclosing the mucous membrane, which is then torn down the center and pulled back, revealing the corona. See M *Shabbat* 19:6: “If one circumcises but does not uncover the circumcision, it is as if he has not circumcised.”

    In Kabbalah the two acts symbolize the sefirotic couple of *Yesod* and *Shekhinah*. Through the double procedure, one who is circumcised unites—and communes with—both of them. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.06 - Haqdamat Sefer ha-Zohar#^z1-13a|1:13a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-32a|32a]] (*Tos*), [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-32a|32a–b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-93b|93b]], [[Zohar 2.03 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-98b|98b]] (*ST*); [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-3b|2:3b]], [[Zohar 4.05 - Parashat Bo#^z2-40a|40a]], [[Zohar 5.04 - Sava de-Mishpatim#^z2-125b|125b]]; [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-91b|3:91b]], [[Zohar 8.04 - Parashat Emor#^z3-95b|95b]]; Moses de León, *Sefer ha-Mishqal*, 133; idem, *Sheqel ha-Qodesh*, 55 (67).

[^871]: **Whoever brings his son…** On circumcision as a sacrificial offering, see above, note 656. On the connection between circumcision and the divine name, see above, note 775.

[^872]: **Upon this, heaven and earth are erected…** See BT [[Shabbat 137#137b|*Shabbat* 137b]]: “Were it not for the blood of the covenant \[of circumcision\], heaven and earth would not endure, as is said: *Were it not for My covenant day and night, I would not have established the laws of heaven and earth.” Heaven* and *earth* symbolize *Tif’eret* and *Shekhinah*, whose union is maintained by the covenant of circumcision, symbolizing *Yesod*, the divine phallus. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-32a|1:32a]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-56a|56a]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-59b|59b]], [[Zohar 1.08 - Parashat Noah#^z1-66b|66b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-89a|89a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-91b|91b]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-93b|93b]].

[^873]: **wedding celebration** הלולא (*Hillula*). Circumcision initates the male into an intimate relationship with *Shekhinah*. See above, note 683.

[^874]: **seeing the blessed Holy One face-to-face this day!** Through the secrets of Torah that were revealed. See above, pages 89, 98.

[^875]: ***Everyone who is called by My name*** Through the ritual of circumcision, the divine name is inscribed in the flesh. See above, note 775.

[^876]: **They escorted Rabbi Abba for three** miles According to Rav Sheshet (BT [[Sotah 46#46b|*Sotah* 46b]]), one should escort his teacher a distance of a parasang. A distinguished teacher, however, is to be escorted for three parasangs. (The Greek parasang equals about 3.5 miles.)

    See *Pesiqta de-Rav Kahana* 18:5; *Bereshit Rabbah* 32:10; *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-51a|1:51a]], [[Zohar 2.02 - Parashat Lekh Lekha#^z1-87a|87a]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-150b|150b]]; [[Zohar 4.03 - Parashat Shemot#^z2-14a|2:14a]], [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-164a|164a]], [[Zohar 6.03 - Parashat Tetsavveh#^z2-187a|187a]]; [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-8b|3:8b]].

[^877]: **That man** Rabbi Abba’s host.

[^878]: **My wife was the wife of my brother…** According to [[Torah 49 - Ki Teitzei#^25-5|Deuteronomy 25:5–10]], if a man’s married brother dies childless, he is obligated to marry the widow. The first son born to the new couple is named after the deceased brother, *so that his name will not be blotted out of Israel* (ibid., 6). See BT [[Yevamot 24#24a|*Yevamot* 24a]]; Rashi on [[Torah 09 - Vayeshev#^38-8|Genesis 38:8]].

    This ritual, known as *yibbum* (“levirate marriage”), is cited in Kabbalah as proof of reincarnation: the newborn son is the reincarnation of the deceased childless brother, who is now granted another opportunity to marry and engender new life. See Scholem, *Kabbalah*, 345.

[^879]: **He** Rabbi Abba.

[^880]: **“From now on, call him Idi”—that is, Idi son of Ya’akov** The Aramaic word אידי (*iddei*) means “this” but also conveys “that, the other, another.” Here the various meanings apply, alluding to the complicated status of the child: he is “this” new creature but also “that other” one, a reincarnation of his father. The name also recalls the phrase אידך‎ פתח (*petaḥ iddakh*), “another opened,” which appears often on the preceding pages, introducing the various anonymous expositions.

    The full name, Idi son of Ya’akov, maintains the confusion of father and son, reversing the name of the Talmudic figure Ya’akov son of Idi. Perhaps the form “Idi son of Ya’akov” reflects the variant preserved in *Ein Ya’aqov, Ḥagigah* 5b: “Idi son of Rabbi Ya’akov son of Idi.” See *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.05 - Parashat Terumah#^z2-152b|2:152b]]–153a.

[^881]: **When he arrived…** Back at the academy of Rabbi Shim’on. Rabbi Abba informs Rabbi Shim’on’s son, Rabbi El’azar, but not the master himself because he fears his reaction to the divulgence of secrets.

[^882]: **until he was circumcised, he fell on** his face… See *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 20: “*Abram fell on his face* \[*and God spoke with him*\]. Until he was circumcised, whenever *Shekhinah* spoke with him, he fell down. Once he was circumcised, He spoke with him while he was standing, as is said: *Abraham remained standing in the presence of YHVH* ([[Torah 04 - Vayera#^18-22|Genesis 18:22]]).”

    See *Pirqei de-Rabbi Eli’ezer* 29; above, pages 102–3.

[^883]: ***here is My covenant with you*, for he found himself circumcised** See *Tanḥuma, Lekh Lekha* 17: “Abraham was sitting and wondering how he would be circumcised. As soon as the blessed Holy One said to him, *‘I will set My covenant between Me and you’* ([[Torah 03 - Lech Lecha#^17-2|Genesis 17:2]]), what is written next? *Abraham fell on his face*. As soon as he fell on his face, the blessed Holy One gestured to that spot and a scorpion bit him, so he was circumcised. How do we know this? From what is written \[next\]: *and God spoke with him, saying, ‘As for Me, here is My covenant with you*. Look! You are circumcised.’ Further, it is written: *On this very day Abraham was circumcised* (ibid., 26). It is not said: *circumcised himself*, but rather: *was circumcised*.”

    See *Bereshit Rabbah* 47:3: “Rabbi Levi said, ‘It is not written: *Abraham circumcised himself*, but rather: *was circumcised*. He examined himself and found himself circumcised.’”

[^884]: **I fear they might be punished because of me** I fear that because I am telling you that the residents of Kefar Tarsha divulged secrets, they will be punished.

[^885]: ***Do not withhold good from those who deserve it*…** See *Zohar* [[Zohar 5.03 - Parashat Mishpatim#^z2-98a|2:98a]]; [[Zohar 9.16 - Commentary on Ha'azinu, continued#^z3-297a|3:297a]].

[^886]: **with this Torah they will be exiled to Babylonia…** The divulgers of Kefar Tarsha will be exiled from Galilee to Babylonia, apparently to the Torah center of Mata Meḥasya. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 10.06 - Parashat Va-Yera#^z1-101b|1:101b]] (*MhN*); above, note 631.

    Despite Rabbi Shim’on’s promise not to punish, he does. See BT *Me’ilah* 17b, where Rabbi Shim’on promises not to punish Rabbi El’azar son of Yose but does.

[^887]: **Your heart’s configuration is apparent in your face!** Similar expressions appear in *Zohar* [[Zohar 7.03 - Parashat Va-Yiqra#^z3-6a|3:6a]], [[Zohar 9.03 - Parashat Shelah Lekha#^z3-157a|157a]]; *[[Zohar 11.06 - Zohar al Shir ha-Shirim#^zh-72a|ZḤ* 72a]] (*ShS*). “Configuration” renders the Zoharic neologism טופסרא (*tufsera*), based on the Aramaic טופסא (*tufsa*), “mold, form,” derived from the Greek *tupos*.

    See *Zohar* [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-20b|1:20b]], [[Zohar 1.07 - Parashat Be-Reshit#^z1-30b|30b]], [[Zohar 2.06 - Parashat Va-Yetse#^z1-157a|157a]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-241b|241b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-242b|242b]], [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-243b|243b]]; [[Zohar 6.06 - Parashat Pequdei#^z2-235b|2:235b]] (*Tos*); [[Zohar 8.07 - Parashat Be-Midbar#^z3-120a|3:120a]], [[Zohar 9.12 - Parashat Va-Ethannan#^z3-270b|270b]]; *Bei’ur ha-Millim ha-Zarot*, 178–80.

[^888]: **they will learn those ways…** The ways of the Babylonians, who conceal secrets rather than divulging them. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 3.07 - Parashat Va-Yhi#^z1-224b|1:224b]]–225a; Liebes, “Ziqqat ha-Zohar le-Erets Yisra’el,” 35–36.

[^889]: **For words are revealed only among us…** Only Rabbi Shim’on and his circle have been authorized by God to reveal mysteries. See *Zohar* [[Zohar 6.04 - Parashat Ki Tissa#^z2-190b|2:190b]]–191a; [[Zohar 7.08 - Parashat Aharei Mot#^z3-79a|3:79a]]; *TZ, Haqdamah*, 1a; Liebes, *Studies in the Zohar*, 29; Meroz, “Va-Ani lo Hayiti Sham?”
